# Vaishnava-lilamrita

*"The Nectar of the Pastimes of the Vaishnavas"*

*A poem by Madhava Pattanayaka (Odia, c. 1535)*

*Translated from the Odia*

---

## Translator's note

The *Vaishnava-lilamrita* is an Odia poem in nine chapters by Madhava Pattanayaka, written, by the author's own
account, in the forty-eighth regnal year of Prataparudra (c. 1535). Madhava was a disciple of Gadadhara Pandita,
from whom he received the Gopala mantra. An orphan from Khandapada, as a youth he saw the kirtan of Madhavendra
Puri, was taken into the house of Bhavananda Raya and became the scribe of Ramananda Raya, and later lived in Puri
in the company of Mahaprabhu. Many of his accounts are therefore eyewitness testimony: of the Jagannatha temple and
its kings, of Ramananda, of the Panchasakha — the five Odia Vaishnava poets (Balarama Dasa, Jagannatha Dasa,
Achyutananda, Ananta and Yashovanta) — and of Mahaprabhu's life in Puri. Earlier Madhava had written the
*Chaitanya-vilasa*, on Sri Chaitanya before His sannyasa and arrival in Puri.

**Source.** The text of the poem follows the edition of B. Ch. Acharya (Sambalpur, 1986), read line by line from
the page images. **Only Madhava's text** is translated; the editor's introduction and notes were neither translated
nor paraphrased.

**How the translation was made.** No native speaker of Odia took part. Every couplet was read from the scan,
transliterated and translated word for word (this material will appear in the full edition, published separately);
the literary translation rests on that work. Doubtful places are marked "(?)", unclear ones "(??)"; words supplied
for the sense are in square brackets. Questions for a specialist in Odia are listed separately.

**Numbering.** Couplets are numbered as in the edition: "chapter.verse". The "Eight Verses" in chapter 6 (Sanskrit
verses with their Odia rendering) are marked 6.Sh1…6.Sh8e, and Jagannatha Dasa's song in chapter 8 8.P0…8.P4.
Section headings are the translator's (the edition has none).

**Madhava and the Gaudiya Vaishnavas.** Madhava belongs to the Odia line of Jagannatha Dasa (the Atibadi). In much
he agrees with the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* — on the Lord's descent to taste the bhava of Radha, on the conversation
with Ramananda Raya, on Mahaprabhu's mahabhava in the Gambhira — but at times he departs from Gaudiya Vaishnava
teaching: he places Jagannatha Dasa beside the Lord Himself, ascribes the *Shikshashtaka* to him, speaks of
Nilachala as the highest abode, and tells of Mahaprabhu's departure according to Odia tradition. The translation
renders the author faithfully, softening nothing; at each such place a note briefly gives the Gaudiya Vaishnava
position after Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati: Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself, who has taken the mood and lustre
of Radha; His body is sat-chit-ananda, and His departure is a disappearance by His own will.

---

# Madhava Pattanayaka. *Vaishnava-lilamrita*, Chapter 1
## Introduction: the Glory of Jagannatha and Nilachala


*Obeisance to Sri Ganesha!*  
*May there be no obstacles!*

**1.1.** O Jagannatha, sat-cit-ananda! Your name is all the goals of human life.[^01-1]  
**1.2.** You made all the tirthas one; You assumed the fourfold form.[^01-2]  
**1.3.** The name "Sri Hare Rama Krishna" is hidden (?) in Your body.[^01-3]  
**1.4.** The universe abides in Your belly; You are the God unattainable [even] by the shastras.  
**1.5.** All the tirthas are at Your feet, all the gods are in Your name.  
**1.6.** You, O God, are the refuge of the gods; what can I tell of Your purpose (?)?[^01-4]  
**1.7.** You are the eternal, everlasting God; in Your body is the pure pranava.[^01-5]  
**1.8.** You are the eternal Truth, Parabrahman; in Your body all abodes are gathered.  
**1.9.** (??) … nigama …; Your name, protecting (?) living beings, is their support.[^01-6]  
**1.10.** O Lord of the world, Jagannatha! Apart from You (?) there is no other path.  
**1.11.** By constantly serving Your feet, living beings cross the waters of existence.  
**1.12.** You deliver the fallen — who can describe Your limit?  
**1.13.** Infinite, changeless, imperishable! Your name is the sum, full of Shunya.[^01-7]  
**1.14.** From Your name the pranava arose; from Your name the Rigveda (?) was born.  
**1.15.** Your form is full of condensed rasa; it is fearlessness for the people of the world.[^01-8]  
**1.16.** Give me shelter at Your feet, so that no sorrow remains (??) …[^01-9]  
**1.17.** In Your body is the lila of Radha and Krishna; this is the eternal abode, this the eternal play.[^01-10]  
**1.18.** Nilachala is the eternal abode, pure, sattvic, incomparable.[^01-11]  
**1.19.** Nilachala is the eternal Goloka; here is the eternal play of Krishna.  
**1.20.** This lila is eternal and pure (?); it is manifest in Nilachala.  
**1.21.** If one takes refuge in the eternal abode, no darkness remains for a living being.  
**1.22.** Taking refuge in Nilachala, living beings have crossed over samsara.  
**1.23.** In every age the devotees of Vishnu took refuge in Your abode and were born [there] (?).[^01-12]  
**1.24.** The faith of the devotees is at Your feet; there their resting place is gathered.  
**1.25.** When [You] grant living beings liberation, [they] are settled in the circle (?) of Goloka.[^01-13]  
**1.26.** Who would ask You for liberation? You (?) are the refuge of those who have no refuge.  
**1.27.** Whoever worships on the path of devotees obtains shelter at Your feet.  
**1.28.** You are the faith of the devotees; who can tell Your beginning and end?  
**1.29.** Devotees, gathering here, worshipped Your name and went away (?).[^01-14]

## The Glory of the Vaishnavas of Nilachala; the Author on Himself

**1.30.** Whatever hosts of devotees there are — who can describe their glory?[^01-15]  
**1.31.** Those who lived taking refuge at Your feet, and those who came to Your feet;  
**1.32.** those who lived in Your abode with devoted minds, in the kirtan of the name;  
**1.33.** those Vaishnavas, great ascetics — who can describe their lilas?  
**1.34.** The lilas that all the Vaishnavas of Nilachala brought about —  
**1.35.** the wondrous deeds of the Vaishnavas — who is able to hear them?  
**1.36.** Who can tell the deeds of the Vaishnavas? They are truly amrita.  
**1.37.** A little shall I describe, after reflection (?), meditating on the feet of Sri Jagannatha.  
**1.38.** I shall make my life blessed; blessed [will be] my birth and my deeds.  
**1.39.** Good people, listen and gladden your hearts: rare are the deeds of the Vaishnavas.  
**1.40.** If you would cross over samsara, listen to this profound (?) account.  
**1.41.** It was not (?) revealed before; now I (?), the servant, shall describe it.  
**1.42.** The Vaishnavas gathered here, making the name of Sri Krishna their raft.[^01-16]  
**1.43.** They expounded the rare truth and established the devotees in the name.  
**1.44.** Fixing their minds on Sri Radha and Krishna, they utter the name and lose themselves.[^01-17]  
**1.45.** Maddened by the name, they know neither night nor day.  
**1.46.** Contemplating in their hearts the Lord of Radha — Sri Jagannatha, the Lord of the world —  
**1.47.** they performed here the great lila, the great play in mahabhava.[^01-18]  
**1.48.** Rare are the deeds of the Vaishnavas; my mind will describe a little.  
**1.49.** Listen, good people, attentively: [this] will establish (?) glory in the world.  
**1.50.** Rare is the company of sadhus — where can one find it now (?)?  
**1.51.** Rare are these deeds of the sadhus, manifested in Nilachala.  
**1.52.** I shall tell a little of what is dear to me: it will destroy the torments and misfortunes of existence.  
**1.53.** May the dust of the Vaishnavas' feet fall unceasingly on my head.  
**1.54.** I am the lowly, wretched Madhava; my birth is in a shudra family.[^01-19]  
**1.55.** My father's name is Bhagavan; my mother, know, is Hiradei (?).[^01-20]  
**1.56.** Born in the Karana lineage, I bear the title Pattanayaka.[^01-21]  
**1.57.** Khandapada is my former home; I set my mind on living in Sri Kshetra.[^01-22]  
**1.58.** My guru is Gadadhara Pandita; he showed [me] boundless mercy.[^01-23]  
**1.59.** He gave [me] initiation in the Gopala mantra and delivered [me], a foolish soul.[^01-24]  
**1.60.** I am the disciple of Gadadhara; my faith is in the devotees.  
**1.61.** Constantly serving the feet of Sri Guru, I obtained knowledge and bhakti, surrendering [to him].  
**1.62.** I made service to the Vaishnavas my root, I gave my heart at the Vaishnavas' feet.  
**1.63.** My root is the company of Vaishnavas; I shall tell of the Vaishnavas.  
**1.64.** Those who are devoted in the mood of the gopis, let them keep (?) [this] in their hearts.[^01-25]  
**1.65.** All that I have heard of the former deeds of Vishnu's devotees I shall describe.  
**1.66.** All the sights I witnessed with my own eyes — how could I leave (??) [them]?[^01-26]  
**1.67.** The Vaishnava Ramananda Raya is wise, knowledgeable and learned.[^01-27]  
**1.68.** Rama Raya is my provider, a knower of the truth of the devotees' rasa.

## On the Work; the Revered Vaishnavas; End of Chapter 1


**1.69.** Foremost (?) among the Vaishnavas — having obtained grace from him,[^01-28]  
**1.70.** taking the guru's blessing as my support, I shall write the account, remembering (?).  
**1.71.** I composed the *Chaitanya-vilasa* — [on] the rasa of the Lord's sannyasa.[^01-29]  
**1.72.** In the king's fourteenth anka I composed the *Chaitanya-vilasa*.[^01-30]  
**1.73.** Now, in the forty-eighth anka, I shall write of the rasa of the Vaishnavas' lilas.  
**1.74.** [I revere] the feet of Sri Guru Chaitanya, the feet of Sri Guru Jagannatha.[^01-31]  
**1.75.** Sri Ramananda Raya, the best of all; the guru Svarupa Damodara.  
**1.76.** Sri Balarama and Achyuta, Ananta, Hari, Yashovanta;[^01-32]  
**1.77.** Sri Jivadeva, the great warrior; Sri Rudradeva, the mighty.[^01-33]  
**1.78.** And as many other devotees as there are — their names are many.  
**1.79.** All of them, gathering together, pouring their minds into Krishna's rasa,  
**1.80.** took refuge in sankirtana and always worshipped the name.  
**1.81.** Briefly I have told this rasa; good people, bear no anger.  
**1.82.** Meditating on the lotus (?) feet of Sri Jagannatha, the lowest of shudras speaks.[^01-34]  

[^01-1]: Purushartha: the goals of human life, namely dharma, artha, kama and moksha. According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 7), the highest, "fifth" goal is prema, love for Krishna.
[^01-2]: The fourfold form: Jagannatha, Baladeva (Balarama), Subhadra and Sudarshana, worshipped together in the temple at Puri.
[^01-3]: Apparently the Hare Krishna mahamantra (abbreviated by the author as "Sri Hare Rama Krishna nama"). The notion of the name "hidden" in Jagannatha's body belongs to the Odia Panchasakha tradition; it is not in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita*. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the mahamantra is the sixteen names of the *Kali-santarana Upanishad*, and Jagannatha is Krishna Himself. The reading ଗୋପ୍ୟାନ (*gopyāna*, "secretly"?) is uncertain.
[^01-4]: *Mati*: mind, intention, purpose; perhaps "nature". Two readings are conjectural: the print has କ (*ka*), probably କି (*ki*, "what") with the ି lost; ତୁର (*tura*) is taken as an archaic "Your".
[^01-5]: The pranava is the sacred syllable om. Odia Vaishnavas see the written form of om in Jagannatha's very form. According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 7), the pranava is the sound incarnation of the Lord and the chief utterance of the Vedas.
[^01-6]: The first half-line is not read. The print has ନନ୍ଦ ନିଗମ ତନ୍ଦବାହା (*nanda nigama tandabāhā?*); the first and last signs match ନ୍ଦ in ସଚ୍ଚିଦାନନ୍ଦ (1.1), but the words are unidentified. Only "nigama" is clear: the Vedas, the scriptures. In the second half-line ରଖି (*rakhi*, "protecting") is conjectural.
[^01-7]: Shunya ("void") is, for the Odia poets of the Panchasakha (Jagannatha Dasa, Achyutananda and others), the highest reality without attributes; they worship Jagannatha as the Shunya-purusha. Madhava here speaks the language of this Odia tradition. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, do not accept the Shunya as the Absolute: Jagannatha is Krishna Himself, the Supreme Person with an eternal spiritual form; the Lord's being "without qualities" means only that He has no material qualities. *Ṭhuḷa* means "gathered into one, the sum".
[^01-8]: *Rasa-ghana*, "condensed rasa": the Lord as the embodiment of the taste of love; cf. "He indeed is rasa" (*Taittiriya Upanishad* 2.7).
[^01-9]: The last word is unidentified: the print has ଅତ୍ରମିତେ (*atramite?*), rhyming with ମୋତେ (*mote*). Possibly a corruption of ଅନ୍ତିମେ (*antime*, "at the end [of life]"). The sense of the half-line is "let no sorrow remain".
[^01-10]: Odia Vaishnavas contemplate in Jagannatha the eternal lila of Radha and Krishna. According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Jagannatha is Krishna Himself, and before Him Mahaprabhu experienced the mood of Radha (Madhya 13, Ratha-yatra).
[^01-11]: Nilachala ("the Blue Mountain") is Puri, Purushottama-kshetra. Verses 18–22 speak of Puri as the eternal spiritual abode of Krishna, Goloka.
[^01-12]: ଜନମଲେ (*janamale*) is taken as "were born"; possibly ଜନମିଲେ with the ି lost. The sense: devotees sought to be born in Puri or to settle there.
[^01-13]: Goloka is the highest abode of Krishna. That pure devotees do not accept liberation devoid of service to the Lord: *Bhagavata Purana* 3.29.13; cf. v. 26.
[^01-14]: ଭଜିଗଲେ ଚଳି (*bhajigale caḷi*), literally "having worshipped, went away". Possibly "they departed [from the world] worshipping Your name", or "they worshipped unceasingly". The link with the next verse (leaf 47) is to be checked.
[^01-15]: ଥୋକେ (*thoke*): "in groups, in hosts"; below the same word (37, 52) and ଥୋକାଏ (*thokāe*, 48) mean "a little".
[^01-16]: The raft is a figure for crossing the ocean of samsara; cf. 1.11, 1.22, 1.40.
[^01-17]: ଭୋଳ ହୋନ୍ତି (*bhoḷa honti*): "they lose themselves" in the ecstasy of love, ceasing to be aware of the external.
[^01-18]: Mahabhava is the highest stage of prema. According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Adi 4), in its fullness it belongs to Sri Radha, and Mahaprabhu manifested it in Puri; here Madhava speaks of the "great play" of the Vaishnavas of Nilachala headed by the Lord.
[^01-19]: Madhava calls himself a "shudra" by birth (cf. 1.56: he belongs to the Karanas, the scribe caste). Cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 8: Ramananda Raya, also a Karana, calls himself a shudra, and Mahaprabhu replies that one who knows the science of Krishna is a guru, whatever his birth.
[^01-20]: The father's name, Bhagavan, is read with confidence. The mother's name is printed unclearly: ହୀରାଦେଈ (*hīrādeī*, Hiradei)?
[^01-21]: The Karanas are the Odia caste of scribes and officials; "Pattanayaka" is their title of honour (hence the author's name, Madhava Pattanayaka).
[^01-22]: Khandapada is a place in Odisha west of Puri (now in Nayagarh district).
[^01-23]: Gadadhara Pandita is the eternal associate of Mahaprabhu who lived in Puri and served Tota-Gopinatha there. According to Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 12; *Gaura-ganoddesha-dipika*), he is the incarnation of Sri Radha's energy.
[^01-24]: The Gopala mantra is the mantra for worshipping Krishna as Gopala; initiation in it is given in the Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition as well.
[^01-25]: The mood of the gopis is the love of the cowherd girls of Vraja for Krishna. In the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 8) Ramananda Raya, in his conversation with Mahaprabhu, calls the love of the gopis, and above all of Sri Radha, the highest stage of bhakti. The syntax of the couplet and the last word (ଧାର, *dhāra*?) are read with uncertainty.
[^01-26]: The second half-line is not read. The print has approximately ତ୍ୟଜାଇ କେମନ୍ତେ ମୁହାଁସ (*tyajāi kemante muhā̐sa?*); only କେମନ୍ତେ, "how", is clear. The translation follows the sense ("how could I not tell what I saw?").
[^01-27]: Ramananda Raya (in Madhava "Rama Raya") is the great devotee, governor under King Prataparudra, Mahaprabhu's interlocutor on the Godavari (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 8). "Provider" (*annadātā*): apparently Madhava was in Ramananda's service and lived on his support.
[^01-28]: Apparently Ramananda Raya (1.67–68), from whom Madhava received grace. The word ପଟେ (*paṭe*, "among, in the rank"?) is read with uncertainty.
[^01-29]: The *Chaitanya-vilasa* is Madhava's earlier work on Sri Chaitanya (from His appearance to His sannyasa and arrival in Puri, according to scholars).
[^01-30]: An anka is the Odia count of a king's regnal years, in which certain numbers are skipped; the anka is therefore higher than the calendar year of the reign. The king here is Prataparudra (reigned c. 1497–1540). By the accepted conversion, the 14th anka ≈ 1516/17 (the date of the *Chaitanya-vilasa*), the 48th anka ≈ 1535 (the date of the *Vaishnava-lilamrita*).
[^01-31]: Madhava reveres as gurus Sri Chaitanya and Jagannatha — judging by the list in the following verses, apparently Jagannatha Dasa of the Panchasakha (cf. ch. 4, where Chaitanya and Jagannatha Dasa call each other "swami" and "prabhu"). For Gaudiya Vaishnavas Sri Chaitanya is not only a guru but the Supreme Lord Himself, Krishna in the mood of Radha (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 1–4).
[^01-32]: Together with Jagannatha (1.74), these are the five Odia Vaishnava poets of the Panchasakha: Balarama Dasa, Jagannatha Dasa, Achyutananda, Ananta and Yashovanta. Who "Hari" is remains unclear (another devotee?).
[^01-33]: Rudradeva is apparently King Prataparudra, Mahaprabhu's patron in Puri. Jivadeva is a scholar or devotee of the same circle; who he was cannot be established from the text.
[^01-34]: This couplet is the closing signature of the chapter. We take the printed ପାଦଦ୍ମ (*pādadma*) as ପାଦପଦ୍ମ ("lotus feet"): this restores the rhyme with ଶୂଦ୍ରାଧମ.

# Madhava Pattanayaka. *Vaishnava-lilamrita*, Chapter 2
## The Rahasa in Nilachala


**2.1.** Giving my heart to Krishna's feet, I shall describe a little of the rahasa.[^02-1]  
**2.2.** The gopis (?) danced — [this dance] is worthy of the name "rasa" (?).[^02-2]  
**2.3.** This lila of the rasa has no end; this is the rasa of the eternal abode.  
**2.4.** The eternal abode is Nilachala; here the rasa dance is performed.  
**2.5.** Radha and Krishna — in this united form; to adorn (?) Jagannatha,[^02-3]  
**2.6.** the Swami arranged the gathering and gave it the name "rahasa".[^02-4]  
**2.7.** Here are japa, austerity and yoga, the devotees' bhava and anuraga:  
**2.8.** all this he brought together and called the rahasa.  
**2.9.** The rahasa is nama-sankirtana; it is not different from the name.  
**2.10.** Here are jhanja cymbals, mridanga, gini, karatalas, and also the leuda (?).[^02-5]  
**2.11.** Krishna's rasa becomes song: the *Gita-govinda* and the *Karnamrita* resound (?).[^02-6]  
**2.12.** Taking up southern padas together, they dance in rows (?) (??).[^02-7]  
**2.13.** Amid the din of cymbals and mridanga they dance, circling (?) (??).[^02-8]  
**2.14.** Sky and earth become one; the earth melts, streams of water [flow].  
**2.15.** In the middle of the rasa ground shines a jewelled mandapa.  
**2.16.** In the middle of the mandapa is a throne; on it Radha and Krishna are seated.  
**2.17.** On all four sides (??) the sakhis stand calmly.[^02-9]  

## The Rahasa at Bata-Ganesha; King Chodaganga; the Coming of Jayadeva


**2.18.** The eight sakhis, renowned by name, are always on all sides.[^02-10]  
**2.19.** In the middle is the rasa grove: such is the scene of the rasa lila.  
**2.20.** From there the sound of the flute streams forth and enchants the gopis.  
**2.21.** On all sides the devotees skilfully arranged the rahasa.  
**2.22.** Near Bata-Ganesha they raised the mandapa of the rahasa.[^02-11]  
**2.23.** Day and night they performed the rasa there — a consolation for the devotees.  
**2.24.** The din of cymbals and mridanga — a deep, sweet (?) jubilation.  
**2.25.** Kanhai built it; the Swami gave initiation into the rahasa.[^02-12]  
**2.26.** Balarama, Achyuta, Yashovanta and Ananta are renowned as the sakhas.[^02-13]  
**2.27.** Gopala Guru and Kanhai, gathering in the rasa mandapa,  
**2.28.** arrange sankirtana daily and notice neither night nor day.  
**2.29.** Here the Puranas are read; the Swami composes the *Bhagavata*.[^02-14]  
**2.30.** They hold discourses on truth — in the honey-stream of Krishna's lilas.  
**2.31.** Where did this rahasa come from? Who first arranged it?  
**2.32.** If you ask me — [I shall tell] what I heard from the mouths of sadhus.  
**2.33.** I shall expound a little here; hold no fault in your minds (?).  
**2.34.** There was formerly a king; in happiness he ruled his people.  
**2.35.** Renowned by the name Chudangadeva — brave, daring and valiant.[^02-15]  
**2.36.** He was the best of devotees and meditated on Sri Jagannatha.  
**2.37.** He renovated the dilapidated temple and left his fame on earth (??).  
**2.38.** He built the garbha-griha and spent much wealth.  
**2.39.** [Such is] the glory of Sri Jagannatha, [such] the bound of this best of kshetras.  
**2.40.** In his old age, establishing this glory, he made a vow and performed the consecration.  
**2.41.** For the time of the gumuti's consecration he makes his son crown prince:[^02-16]  
**2.42.** "When the consecration of the gumuti takes place, I shall give the throne to Kamarnava."  
**2.43.** So he thinks in his mind — and he held a splendid festival.  
**2.44.** Having clay brought quickly from the bank of the Godavari river,  
**2.45.** he made mridangas from it, giving his mind to the rasa of kirtana.  
**2.46.** Jhanja and mridanga together with gini — thus he began the kirtana.  
**2.47.** The consecration of the gumuti took place; he made Kamarnava king.  
**2.48.** In kirtana, with a voice choked with feeling, he fixed his mind at Hari's feet.  
**2.49.** He appointed mahari dancers: Hari's lila became a performance.[^02-17]  
**2.50.** Inside the gumuti of the temple — the sound of the devadasis' dancing  
**2.51.** to the praises of Hari, to mridanga, jhanja and karatalas.  
**2.52.** The maharis danced — and this was called the rahasa dance.  
**2.53.** Seeing this, the sovereign Gangeshvara obtained satisfaction of mind.  
**2.54.** In time that king went to heaven, having established jewel-like (?) fame.  
**2.55.** Meanwhile — listen, people — according to the *Gita-govinda* (?)[^02-18]  
**2.56.** a brahmana named Jayadeva came to the best of kshetras.  
**2.57.** His village is the sasana of Kenduli (?), on the bank of the Prachi river.[^02-19]  
**2.58.** Near Madhava at Niali he served with devotion in many ways.  

## Jayadeva and the *Gita-govinda* in the Temple; King Bhimadeva; Sridhara Svami


**2.59.** Learned in the shastras and Puranas, his mind was on the path of bhakti.  
**2.60.** "I shall compose a song," he said, and resolved to live in the Kshetra.  
**2.61.** Near Sri Jagannatha, with a pure, sattvic mind and feeling (?),  
**2.62.** he composed the *Sri Gita-govinda* and obtained supreme bliss.[^02-20]  
**2.63.** He sang the lila of Sri Radha and Madhava in a sweet voice.  
**2.64.** His wife Padmavati dances (?) to the rhythm of the song.[^02-21]  
**2.65.** Skilfully she performed the rahasa dance — that dancer gave the performance.  
**2.66.** The devotees exclaimed, "Blessed, blessed!" — and in devotional feeling forgot themselves.  
**2.67.** "Let the dance-service of the *Gita-govinda* take place in the temple daily" —  
**2.68.** [so] that king instituted this service and supplied saris from Kenduli.[^02-22]  
**2.69.** "The rasa of this *Sri Gita-govinda* is dear to Sri Jagannatha" —  
**2.70.** saying so, he made this arrangement; the devotees were content.  
**2.71.** In time that poet went to heaven: his passing took place in the best of kshetras.[^02-23]  
**2.72.** Then, for the *Gita-govinda* dance, he built a natamandira.[^02-24]  
**2.73.** For the rahasa dance [the dancers] danced the *Gita-govinda*.  
**2.74.** Such is the deed (?) of Bhimadeva; in time he departed, having established [his] fame.[^02-25]  
**2.75.** Meanwhile, hear the rasa — the rasa of the *Sri Bhagavad-gita* (?).[^02-26]  
**2.76.** The best of brahmanas, named Sridhara — he was born in the village of Marei.[^02-27]  
**2.77.** He composed the *Bhagavata-dipika*; his view is on the path of devotion.[^02-28]  
**2.78.** Living at Remuna-Kataka, he serves the murti of Gopinatha.[^02-29]  
**2.79.** He reads the *Gopala-tapani*, expounding [it] according to Paippalada (??).[^02-30]  
**2.80.** His bhava on the path of devotion wins over all people.  
**2.81.** Having taken initiation in Sri Kshetra, he follows the Advaita doctrine.[^02-31]  
**2.82.** He is a sannyasi of Shankara['s order]; [the second half is unclear] (??).[^02-32]  
**2.83.** His view concerns the meaning of the *Bhagavata*; he expounded (?) the shastra from many viewpoints.  
**2.84.** The path of devotion, karma and jnana — all these are not separate from one another.  
**2.85.** Bhakti exists even apart from (??) jnana; but without (?) bhakti, where is jnana?[^02-33]  

## Sridhara Swami; the Meghanada Wall; Purushottama-deva: Chhera-panhara and the Kanchi Campaign


**2.86.** Contemplate [the Lord] in the form of the *Bhagavata*, and you will take refuge in Sri Krishna.[^02-34]  
**2.87.** Sridhara expounded the *Bhagavata*, and all the people listened to it.[^02-35]  
**2.88.** The pandits of Kashi, Navadvipa and Srirangam gathered and accepted [his exposition].  
**2.89.** Uniting knowledge with bhakti, this [exposition will remain] there until … (??).[^02-36]  
**2.90.** The sannyasis understood its meaning and praised Sridhara: "Blessed, blessed!"  
**2.91.** They had all the manuscripts copied and took them away, together with the *Bhavartha-dipika*.  
**2.92.** He spread the mood of bhakti and bade farewell to [dry] knowledge.[^02-37]  
**2.93.** Whoever walks the path of bhakti surely attains Krishna.  
**2.94.** Without (?) Krishna [a person] falls into hell — thus the meaning of the *Bhagavata* unfolds.[^02-38]  
**2.95.** Rapt (?) in nama-kirtana, he spends the nights without sleep.  
**2.96.** Chanting the king of mantras of Nrisimha and serving (?) Shiva, he obtains grace.[^02-39]  
**2.97.** The Swami lived at Kapilas; he was an eminent mahant.[^02-40]  
**2.98.** Head of the Brahmachari matha (??) — his name was Sridhara Swami-pada.[^02-41]  
**2.99.** The worship of Madhava is his root; his mind rests firmly on Umadhava.[^02-42]  
**2.100.** Learning of Sridhara's rare life, Pratapa Bhanudeva [honoured him]:[^02-43]  
**2.101.** Sridhara performed divine worship; [he was given] the right of the garland and the service of the Sri Murti.[^02-44]  
**2.102.** Listen, devotees, to another tale: I shall describe it in a little more detail.  
**2.103.** The rahasa became a performance, rapt (?) in the mood (?) of the *Gita-govinda*.[^02-45]  
**2.104.** A dog appeared there; seeing it, all the people were distressed.  
**2.105.** They went and reported to the king: "A dog has entered," they said.  
**2.106.** "[The temple] is open on four sides: put up a wall quickly!"  
**2.107.** The king said, "Good, the wall will be laid" — so he said.  
**2.108.** The Meghanada wall was laid; in a year it was completed.[^02-46]  
**2.109.** The drama *Parashurama-vijaya* was presented in the temple.[^02-47]  
**2.110.** Seeing it, all were delighted; the king especially honoured [them] with words.  
**2.111.** "This is the glory of Kapileshvara-deva!" — they proclaimed day and night.  
**2.112.** Of his loins was born the good Purushottama-deva.[^02-48]  
**2.113.** The Bhoga-mandapa is his glory; the rahasa dance is his design (?).  
**2.114.** The temple servants said: "You are great — [yet] you are the servant of the Lord.  
**2.115.** Perform service to the Lord; the great service — give up (?) [to others?].[^02-49]  
**2.116.** In the great service there is the broom: ply it yourself."  
**2.117.** This service at the time of the Ratha the Lord's servant performs.  
**2.118.** Beforehand (?) they sprinkled water with camphor, sandal and aguru.  
**2.119.** The king, holding a golden broom, goes about the chariots.  
**2.120.** Seeing this, the servants praised him: "Know: this is the servant of the Lord!"  
**2.121.** Its name is chhera-panhara, an institution (?) of Purushottama.[^02-50]  
**2.122.** He gave a troupe (?) of dancing girls: "Perform the rahasa!" he said.[^02-51]  
**2.123.** Constantly he pleased the hearts of Jagannatha and Baladeva.  
**2.124.** Having become king (?), he waged war with Saluva Narasimha.[^02-52]  
**2.125.** He brought back his daughter by abduction (?); the marriage (?) took place with honour.  
**2.126.** The servants cried "Blessed!" and said, "This is the Lord's victory."  
**2.127.** For Jaga and Balia both, mounted on a black and a white horse,[^02-53]  
**2.128.** went, it is said, to the battlefield and fought (?) as the king's soldiers.  
**2.129.** Therefore he won the battle and married the beautiful one.  
**2.130.** Would he have obtained such honours without Jagannatha's grace?  
**2.131.** So says Madhava-dasa, setting his hope on Krishna's feet.[^02-54]  

[^02-1]: Rahasa (*rahāsa*): the Odia name for gatherings where the rasa-lila was enacted with song and dance; according to Madhava (2.9), it is nama-sankirtana. According to ch. 4 (pilot, 4.37), Jagannatha Dasa built a mandapa for the rahasa at Bata-Ganesha in Puri.
[^02-2]: The print has ରସ (*rasa*); OCR in vv. 2 and 4 reads ରାସ (*rāsa*, "the rasa dance"). A play on words is possible: *rāsa*, Krishna's dance with the gopis, and *rasa*, the taste of love. The form ଗୋପୀମାନନ୍ତ (*gopīmānanta*) is unclear.
[^02-3]: "United form" (*meḷa bapu*): Radha and Krishna joined in one body. In the Odia tradition Jagannatha is understood in this way. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the united form of Radha and Krishna is Sri Chaitanya (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 1.5), while Jagannatha is Krishna Himself. The syntax of the couplet and the word ସଜିବାକୁ (*sajibāku*, "to adorn"?) are read with uncertainty.
[^02-4]: The Swami is apparently Jagannatha Dasa: so Chaitanya calls him in ch. 4 (pilot, 4.49).
[^02-5]: The jhanja is a pair of large cymbals; gini are small cymbals; karatalas are hand cymbals; ଲେଉଡ଼ା (*leuṛā?*) is an instrument whose reading and meaning are unclear.
[^02-6]: Jayadeva's *Gita-govinda* and Bilvamangala's *Sri Krishna-karnamrita*. According to the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 2), in Puri Mahaprabhu relished these works day and night with Svarupa Damodara and Ramananda Raya. The last word (ଚଳେଇ, *caḷei*?) is read with uncertainty.
[^02-7]: "Southern padas": songs in the southern style; the *Krishna-karnamrita* comes from the south (according to the CC, Madhya 9, Mahaprabhu brought it back from His pilgrimage in South India). The end of the half-line is not read: ପଙ୍କ୍ତି ପ୍ରାସ୍ତେ (*paṅkti prāste?*).
[^02-8]: The last word is unidentified: the print has approximately ଶସ୍ତଳେ (*śastaḷe?*); possibly ରସସ୍ଥଳେ ("on the rasa ground", cf. 2.15).
[^02-9]: The word ≈ କରମ୍ଭିତରେ (*karambhitare?*) is unidentified; the sense of the couplet is clear: the sakhis stand around the throne.
[^02-10]: The eight principal sakhis (companions) of Sri Radha; cf. v. 2.17.
[^02-11]: Bata-Ganesha is Ganesha under the banyan (bata) in the precincts of the Jagannatha temple in Puri; according to ch. 4 (pilot, 4.36–37), Jagannatha Dasa sat there and the rahasa mandapa stood there.
[^02-12]: Kanhai is possibly Kanai Khuntia, an Odia Vaishnava poet of the Panchasakha circle; Gopala Guru (2.27) is unidentified. The "Swami" is Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 2.6).
[^02-13]: The Panchasakha ("five friends"): Balarama Dasa, Jagannatha Dasa (the Swami), Achyutananda, Ananta and Yashovanta; cf. 1.76.
[^02-14]: The *Bhagavata* of Jagannatha Dasa is the Odia rendering of the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (16th c.), the most revered work of Odia Vaishnava literature; cf. pilot, 4.38–39.
[^02-15]: Chudangadeva (Gangeshvara, 2.53) is apparently Anantavarman Chodagangadeva (reigned c. 1078–1147), the Eastern Ganga king under whom the building of the present Jagannatha temple in Puri was begun. Below is Madhava's account of how the rahasa grew out of the temple dances under this king.
[^02-16]: Gumuti: the dome, the tower of the temple. Kamarnava is Chodaganga's son and heir.
[^02-17]: The maharis are the temple dancers (devadasis) of the Jagannatha temple, who danced before the Lord during the services; later they also performed the songs of the *Gita-govinda*.
[^02-18]: Jayadeva is the author of the *Gita-govinda* (12th c.), which Mahaprabhu relished in Puri (CC, Madhya 2). The word ପରିମାଣେ (*parimāṇe*, "by the measure, according to"?) is understood with uncertainty: perhaps "as the *Gita-govinda* attests".
[^02-19]: The print has ଦେନ୍ଦୁଳି (*denduḷi*); taken as Kenduli, Jayadeva's birthplace. The Odia tradition places Kenduli Sasana on the Prachi river, near the Madhava temple at Niali; the Bengali tradition places it in Birbhum.
[^02-20]: The *Gita-govinda* of Jayadeva is a Sanskrit poem on the loving pastimes of Radha and Krishna. In Puri Mahaprabhu relished its songs day and night with Svarupa Damodara and Ramananda Raya (CC, Madhya 2). According to Madhava, the poem was composed in Puri, near Jagannatha.
[^02-21]: Padmavati is Jayadeva's wife; Jayadeva mentions her at the opening of the *Gita-govinda*. The Odia tradition regards her as a temple dancer of Jagannatha. The print has ନୃତ୍ୟତ (*nṛtyata*); the rhyme with ପଦ୍ମାବତୀ requires ନୃତ୍ୟତି (*nṛtyati*, "dances").
[^02-22]: Saris (cloth) from Kenduli, Jayadeva's home village (cf. 2.57); probably connected with the later custom of offering Jagannatha cloth woven with verses of the *Gita-govinda*. Madhava apparently ascribes the institution of the daily singing and dancing of the *Gita-govinda* in the temple to King Bhimadeva (2.74).
[^02-23]: The Bengali traditions do not speak of Jayadeva's passing in Puri; this is the Odia tradition.
[^02-24]: The natamandira is the dance hall of the Jagannatha temple, in front of the jagamohana.
[^02-25]: Bhimadeva is apparently Anangabhimadeva III (reigned c. 1211–1238) of the Eastern Ganga dynasty, to whom the temple's natamandira and the introduction of the *Gita-govinda* into the temple service are ascribed. The first letter of the word ଚରିତ ("deed") is printed like ଜ.
[^02-26]: The print has ଶ୍ରୀଭଗବତ ଗୀତ (*śrībhagabata gīta*): literally "the song of Sri Bhagavan", i.e. the *Bhagavad-gita* (Sridhara Svami also wrote a commentary on it). The reading "the *Bhagavata* [and] the *Gita*" is also possible.
[^02-27]: Sridhara Svami is the author of the *Bhavartha-dipika*, the commentary on the *Srimad-Bhagavatam*. Mahaprabhu honoured his commentary as authoritative and sternly rejected Vallabha Bhatta's attempt to refute it (CC, Antya 7). Marei is, by Odia tradition, a village near Remuna.
[^02-28]: The *Bhagavata-dipika* is apparently the *Bhavartha-dipika*.
[^02-29]: Remuna (near Balasore) is the site of the temple of Kshira-chora Gopinatha, where Madhavendra Puri served and which Mahaprabhu visited (CC, Madhya 4). Kataka means "camp, town"; ‑ଣ (*‑eṇa*) is an old locative ending (cf. pilot, ଅଙ୍ଗେଣ, କୂଳେଣ).
[^02-30]: The *Gopala-tapani* is an Upanishad of the *Atharva-veda* on Krishna as Gopala, on which the Gaudiya Vaishnavas rely (it is quoted in CC). Paippalada is a branch of the *Atharva-veda*; the Upanishad itself opens with the questions of sages. The print has ପଇପଲ ସରି (??) (*païpala sari*); the second word is unclear (the sign looks like ଶି/ଣି, with a stroke under ସ).
[^02-31]: According to the Odia tradition, Sridhara was a sannyasi of Shankara's order and a follower of Advaita (the Govardhana Matha of that order is in Puri). The Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, regard Sridhara Svami as a Vaishnava associated with the Vishnusvami line; his commentary, despite some Advaita-style expressions, places bhakti above all (cf. 2.85).
[^02-32]: The print has ପୂରଣ କରେ ?ବାନଈ (*pūraṇa kare ?abānaī*); the first letter of the last word looks like ତ/ଥ/ଉ. If read ଭବାନଈ = ଭବାନୀ, the sense may be "fulfils [a vow to?] Bhavani" or "Bhavani fulfils [his wishes]" (??); unidentified.
[^02-33]: The print has ଜ୍ଞାନ ବ୍ୟସିକେ (??) (*jñāna byasike*) — from the sense of the second half, "apart from, without jnana" (?); ଭକ୍ତି ବିନେ (?) — the sign of the second word looks like ରି/ଶି, taken as ବିନେ, "without". The superiority of bhakti over jnana is the main thought of Sridhara's commentary, especially valued by the Gaudiya Vaishnavas.
[^02-34]: The account of Sridhara Swami continues (from 2.76). The speaker is not marked: perhaps this sets out Sridhara's teaching. Cf. the Gaudiya Vaishnava view: the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* is Krishna's incarnation in word, a sun that rose after His disappearance (Bhag. 1.3.43).
[^02-35]: Sridhara Swami is the author of the *Bhavartha-dipika* commentary on the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (2.91). Mahaprabhu held his exposition to be authoritative (CC, Antya 7, the episode with Vallabha Bhatta). The "Swami" in this account is Sridhara, not Jagannatha Dasa, whom Madhava calls "the Swami" in 2.25 and 2.29.
[^02-36]: The print has ≈ କସ (*kasa*): the letter after କ resembles ସ/ଯ. Possibly କଳ୍ପ (*kaḷpa*), "until the end of the kalpa", but the print does not show this.
[^02-37]: Jalanjali is the offering of water to the departed; "to give jalanjali to knowledge" means to part with it for good. Cf. the verse *jñāne prayāsam udapāsya…* (Bhag. 10.14.3) in the talks with Ramananda Raya (CC, Madhya 8): giving up endeavour for knowledge in favour of hearing about the Lord.
[^02-38]: The print has ବହୁନେ (*bahune*); taken as ବିହୁନେ (*bihune*), "without" (the "ି" sign is often lost in this font). The meaning of ବାଢ଼େ ("grows", "unfolds") in the second half-line is uncertain.
[^02-39]: The *Bhavartha-dipika* opens with obeisances to Nrisimha. The service of Shiva is apparently at the shrine on Kapilas hill (2.97). The print has ସେବ (*seba*), taken as the participle ସେବି ("serving"), as the rhyme with ଜପି requires.
[^02-40]: Kapilas is a hill near Dhenkanal (Odisha) with a shrine of Shiva (Chandrasekhara). A mahant is the abbot of a matha.
[^02-41]: The first word is uncertain: after ବ୍ରହ୍ମ the print has two signs resembling ସ୍ୱା/ହ; read as ବ୍ରହ୍ମଚାରୀ (*brahmacārī*). "Pada" is an honorific added to the name.
[^02-42]: Madhava is a name of Vishnu; Umadhava, "Uma's lord", is Shiva. Here, as in 2.81–82 (Sridhara follows the advaita teaching and is a sannyasi of Shankara's tradition), Madhava's Odia tradition diverges from the Gaudiya Vaishnava one: according to Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, Sridhara Swami was a Vaishnava following Vishnusvami, not a mayavadi; Mahaprabhu accepted his exposition of the *Bhagavatam* as true (CC, Antya 7).
[^02-43]: Pratapa Bhanudeva is apparently Bhanudeva III of the Eastern Ganga dynasty (reigned c. 1352–1378).
[^02-44]: Malyadhikara is the right to receive a garland taken from the Lord (a prasadi garland), a high honour in the Jagannatha temple. The syntax of 2.100–101 is understood from the sense: the king granted Sridhara this right and the service of the Sri Murti.
[^02-45]: The rahasa is a performance of Krishna's lilas, here in the temple, to the songs of the *Gita-govinda* (cf. 2.49–55). There is a dot above ଭାବେ (a chandrabindu or a blot); the last word is ≈ ଭୋଳା (*bhoḷā*, "rapt"), possibly ଖେଳା (*kheḷā*, "play"). A dog entering the temple is a defilement that requires purification.
[^02-46]: The Meghanada pacheri is the outer wall of the Jagannatha temple in Puri; tradition ascribes it to Kapilendra-deva (Kapileshvara, 2.111; reigned c. 1435–1467), founder of the Suryavamsha (Gajapati) dynasty.
[^02-47]: *Parashurama-vijaya* is a Sanskrit drama ascribed to Kapilendra-deva himself.
[^02-48]: Purushottama-deva (reigned c. 1467–1497) was Kapilendra's son and the father of Prataparudra (Rudradeva); the building of the temple's Bhoga-mandapa is ascribed to him.
[^02-49]: The print has ପରିହର (*parihara*), "give up, reject", which contradicts 2.116 ("ply it yourself"). The sense expects ପରିଚର (*paricara*), "perform"; or else: "give up [the pride of royal greatness]".
[^02-50]: Chhera-panhara is the sweeping of the chariots by the king with a golden broom and their sprinkling with sandal water before the Ratha-yatra. CC (Madhya 13) describes Prataparudra performing this service and Mahaprabhu being pleased by his humility. The meaning of ଉଆଣ (*uāṇa*) here and in 2.70, 2.72 is unclear; taken as "institution".
[^02-51]: Nachunis are temple dancers (maharis, cf. 2.49). ସଂପରଦା (*saṁparadā*) is taken as "troupe" or "grant" (Skt. *samprada*?).
[^02-52]: The Odia legend of Purushottama-deva's campaign against Kanchi (Kanchi-Kaveri): the king of Kanchi insulted him as a "sweeper" because of the chhera-panhara; Jagannatha and Baladeva rode ahead of the army on a black and a white horse, and the king won and took the princess as his wife (Padmavati, according to the legend). Madhava names the opponent Saluva Narasimha, a ruler of Vijayanagara in the second half of the 15th century.
[^02-53]: Jaga and Balia are affectionate Odia names of Jagannatha and Baladeva (Balabhadra).
[^02-54]: The closing formula with the author's name. Chapter 2 ends with this verse; Chapter 3 ("ତୃତୀୟ ଅଧ୍ୟାୟ") begins on leaf 57.

# Madhava Pattanayaka. *Vaishnava-lilamrita*, Chapter 3
## The Two *Gita-govindas*: the Test before Jagannatha and Rudradeva's Edict


**3.1.** O sadhus, hear with faith (?) of the sweetness of the *Gita-govinda*.[^03-1]  
**3.2.** [To] the song that Jayadeva composed, the rahasa was performed in the gumuti (?).[^03-2]  
**3.3.** For a long time this dance was performed in the gumuti.  
**3.4.** [There was] Divakara, the best of brahmanas, foremost in learning.[^03-3]  
**3.5.** Taking his seat in the assembly (?), he composes the *Gita-govinda* anew.  
**3.6.** He is the king's favourite and chief (?) [at his court]; he composed a song-dance.[^03-4]  
**3.7.** He put the king's name into the signature and received many rewards (?).[^03-5]  
**3.8.** This dance of the *Sri Gita-govinda* is called "the New Song".[^03-6]  
**3.9.** On the former *Gita-govinda* dance a royal prohibition was proclaimed:  
**3.10.** "Let the king's song-dance be performed; the former padas shall not be sung."  
**3.11.** In time that king went to heaven, and an obstacle arose for the king's song.[^03-7]  
**3.12.** Jivadeva, the best of brahmanas — his mind [was given] to learned disputation —[^03-8]  
**3.13.** reported to Rudradeva: "This dance is not good."[^03-9]  
**3.14.** The temple servants said: "How so? This dance and that one are not the same" (?).[^03-10]  
**3.15.** They placed the two manuscripts in the gumuti, saying: "Choose which is good" (?).[^03-11]  
**3.16.** Having placed the manuscripts in the holy gumuti, they bolted (?) the doors there.  
**3.17.** The next day at dawn the servants saw with their own eyes:  
**3.18.** the Bada Thakura's song-manuscript lies beneath,[^03-12]  
**3.19.** and Jayadeva's song-dance lies plainly on top.  
**3.20.** The servants went and reported; Sri Rudradeva commanded:  
**3.21.** "Let both this dance and that one be performed; set neither aside (?).  
**3.22.** The Bada Thakura's song-dance is performed at the time of the Lord's bhoga (?):[^03-13]  
**3.23.** [beginning] from (??) the evening dhupa, it ends at the Bada-singhara.[^03-14]  
**3.24.** At other times, performing song, let them chant (?) the *Sri Gita-govinda*.  
**3.25.** The troupe of the Bada Thakura, the troupe of Kapileshvara Thakura,[^03-15]  
**3.26.** the troupe of the old dancers, the troupe (?) of the Telangi dancers —  
**3.27.** these dancers shall sing, they shall perform the Bada Thakura's songs.  
**3.28.** Know: this dance is for the others (?); but the singing of the *Sri Gita-govinda*,[^03-16]  
**3.29.** the sweet song of Jayadeva, the Vaishnava singers honour (?).[^03-17]  
**3.30.** They shall sing this song of rasa, and all the untrained shall learn [it].  
**3.31.** Apart from this there shall be no other song; let the Padichha (?) be vigilant."[^03-18]  
**3.32.** Such was the king's ordinance; and the servants proclaimed (?) it.  
**3.33.** At the Jaya-Vijaya gate (?) they had the inscription carved in stone (?):[^03-19]  
**3.34.** Jayadeva's song of rasa, excluding other songs from it,  
**3.35.** they shall sing in the daytime; the Vaishnava servants shall relish [its] rasa.  
**3.36.** Know: this arrangement was made in the … (??) anka of the king.[^03-20]  
**3.37.** Kavichandra Raya Divakara quickly (?) says to the king:[^03-21]  

## Kavichandra's Departure; Madhavendra Puri and Kshirachora-Gopinatha at Remuna


**3.38.** "O (?) king, a muddle has been made here: the servants did not obey [your] word."[^03-22]  
**3.39.** The king, learning of it, kept silent; Kavichandra [took] it as an insult.  
**3.40.** He left the king's court (?) and went away to foreign lands.[^03-23]  
**3.41.** Jivadeva was greatly delighted; the servants were satisfied.[^03-24]  
**3.42.** After some time (?), Bada-thakura's *Gita-govinda* —[^03-25]  
**3.43.** its manuscript was tied up with a string: no one found any taste in it.  
**3.44.** All the Vaishnava troupes [sang] the nectarean song of Jayadeva.  
**3.45.** In the temple this very dance was performed; the other song did not take hold.[^03-26]  
**3.46.** All were satisfied; the devotees rejoiced in bhava.  
**3.47.** Now hear a sweet [tale], if you long for the treasure of prema.  
**3.48.** [There was] a Vaishnava named Madhavendra; his life is rare.[^03-27]  
**3.49.** A king named Nrisimha … to (?) Govardhana … (??) —[^03-28]  
**3.50.** brought the murti of Gopala and settled [Him] at Remuna-kataka.[^03-29]  
**3.51.** He installed the murti of Gopala and offered abundant bhoga.  
**3.52.** In time (?) Madhavendra came, renowned among the saintly Vaishnavas.[^03-30]  
**3.53.** Before the murti of Gopala he swooned in rapture.  
**3.54.** So lovely [was] the form — a twofold (?) [stream] of love flowed.[^03-31]  
**3.55.** Gazing at the murti of Gopala, he passes the night without sleep.  
**3.56.** The servants did not give [him] bhoga; but Madhava there (?) [longed] for Him.[^03-32]  
**3.57.** Bolting the doors, they went home; Madhava remains without food.  
**3.58.** At midnight (?) [the servant] had a dream; the servant opened the doors  
**3.59.** and saw, hidden under the hem [of Gopala's cloth], a little pot of kshira-bhoga that had not been served (?).[^03-33]  
**3.60.** Seeing Gopala's mercy, the servant was astonished.  
**3.61.** Humbly (?) he prostrated himself and proclaimed the glory of Gopala.  
**3.62.** He called out loudly: "Who here is the devotee?"  
**3.63.** He calls, uttering the name of Gopala; Madhava rises with love.  
**3.64.** The servant, setting the vessel down, falls at Madhava's feet:  
**3.65.** "You are dear to Gopala, best of devotees: take the bhoga.  
**3.66.** I have offended you; I have received Gopala's command.  
**3.67.** When you taste this prasada, [you will know]: it is not (?) [mere] prasada — it is nectar."[^03-34]  
**3.68.** Madhava eats with a joyful heart, remembering the name of Gopala within.  
**3.69.** At dawn the servant rose and fell at Madhava's feet.  
**3.70.** All the people (?) heard of this and at once came to Madhava:  
**3.71.** "Where was such a devotee? How much merit he must have done!"  
**3.72.** For His devotee's sake Gopala [came] to be called "Thief of Kshira" (?).  
**3.73.** All gathered together and surrendered (?) their hearts at Madhava's feet.  
**3.74.** They performed kirtana there, and Madhava thrilled all over.[^03-35]  
**3.75.** This Gopinatha, Kshirachora, became famous among the people.[^03-36]  
**3.76.** Many years [Madhavendra] spent [there], meditating on Kshirachora.[^03-37]  
**3.77.** His disciples Raghava and Ishvara came and joined him at Remuna.[^03-38]  

## Madhavendra Puri at Bentapur; the Author's Own Story: the Road from Khandapada and the Meeting with Ramananda


**3.78.** Bhavananda invited [him]: "Come to my village," he said.[^03-39]  
**3.79.** Going (?) by the road to Purushottama (?), [they] halted to rest in the village of Bentapur.[^03-40]  
**3.80.** Madhava, Ishvara [and] Raghava — guru and disciples — all [became] guests.[^03-41]  
**3.81.** They stayed in Bhavananda's home and performed kirtan in Bentapur.  
**3.82.** Keeping the beat on mridanga and gini, they led the kirtan of Hari's name.[^03-42]  
**3.83.** They stayed there one paksha, in the month of Phalguna of the … (??) anka.[^03-43]  
**3.84.** Meanwhile, hear the sweetness of [another] small, particular tale.[^03-44]  
**3.85.** My village is Khandapada; I had reached twenty-one years of age.[^03-45]  
**3.86.** My father and mother passed away: how was I to live decently (?)?[^03-46]  
**3.87.** In this world I am a poor man: I have no kin, no brothers.  
**3.88.** "What shall I do?" I pondered (?); and I placed (?) this resolve (?) in my mind:[^03-47]  
**3.89.** I set my mind on going to the Kshetra and gathered courage in my heart.[^03-48]  
**3.90.** At dawn I went out of the village and became a wayfarer (?) on the road.[^03-49]  
**3.91.** How many villages, hills, fields (?) and forests I then passed!  
**3.92.** Whatever anyone gave, that I ate — I refused (?) nothing.[^03-50]  
**3.93.** In ten days I arrived [there] and halted to rest in the village of Bentapur.  
**3.94.** Evening time came; Madhava together with Raghava  
**3.95.** at (?) Bhavananda's gate joyfully (?) performs kirtan.[^03-51]  
**3.96.** When I saw the kirtan, boundless attachment (?) [arose in me], and my mind grew firm.  
**3.97.** At dawn I went gently and fell at the Gosai's feet.[^03-52]  
**3.98.** I said: "Prabhu, protect [me]: I am the servant of your feet.  
**3.99.** Take me into [your] kirtan (?); teach me Hari's name."[^03-53]  
**3.100.** Madhava Gosai, hearing this, said: "Rise, rise."  
**3.101.** He placed his hand on my head and stroked my back with his hand.  
**3.102.** He said: "You are still young in years — what business have you with kirtan?  
**3.103.** Go to Bhavananda: he will look after you."[^03-54]  
**3.104.** I went to Bhavananda and prostrated myself at his feet.  
**3.105.** Bhavananda … (??) [me] and spoke to Ramananda.[^03-55]  
**3.106.** Ramananda, his eldest son, sits (?) with a book in his hand.[^03-56]  
**3.107.** I went up and told [him] my sorrow; he looked at me with affection.  
**3.108.** He said: "Stay with me; besides, you are a karana lad.[^03-57]  
**3.109.** Where will you go? Stay here: one can see from your face (?) that you have studied."[^03-58]  
**3.110.** [I replied:] "I have studied nothing." [And he] diligently (?) taught me.[^03-59]  

## The Departure of Madhavendra Puri; *Jagannatha-vallabha* and Ramananda Raya at Rajamandri


**3.111.** I learned a few lessons, and for a little while (?) I took up the palm leaf (?).[^03-60]  
**3.112.** [Ramananda] said: "You will stay by me and manage my affairs."  
**3.113.** I stayed, following him, [taking] him (?) as my sole refuge (??).[^03-61]  
**3.114.** Madhava Puri left the Kshetra (?) and stayed at Remuna-kataka.[^03-62]  
**3.115.** Near the murti of Gopinatha he performed kirtana every day.  
**3.116.** Intoxicated with prema, overwhelmed — streams of tears flowed from his eyes.  
**3.117.** In time he departed to heaven — in the month of Phalguna of the fifth anka.[^03-63]  
**3.118.** The king grieved greatly and summoned Bhavananda.[^03-64]  
**3.119.** He said: "Madhava Gosai has departed to heaven singing the Name.[^03-65]  
**3.120.** Blessed is the life of this Vaishnava, blessed the fame he established (?)."  
**3.121.** He grieved much and said: "May he attain a good destiny."  
**3.122.** I shall describe (?) Rama Raya's books: in learning he was accomplished.[^03-66]  
**3.123.** He composed many song-dramas — [one] by the name of *Jagannatha-vallabha*.[^03-67]  
**3.124.** He offered it into the king's hands, and the king praised it: "Excellent, excellent!"  
**3.125.** The king gave his consent and said: "Stage the song-drama."  
**3.126.** In the month of Phalguna of the eighth anka — the festival (?) of *Jagannatha-vallabha*:[^03-68]  
**3.127.** at the Dola festival, in the temple, for five days in the Nata-mandira[^03-69]  
**3.128.** this song-drama was performed, and the king obtained joy.  
**3.129.** With affection the king gave Ramananda the title "Raya"[^03-70]  
**3.130.** and presented a golden staff (?). The devotees enact Radha and Krishna.[^03-71]  
**3.131.** Seeing Raya's drama, the servants praised it: "Excellent, excellent!"  
**3.132.** In the ninth anka [Raya] received a command (?): "Go to Rajamandri," [the king] said,[^03-72]  
**3.133.** "govern the realm of Rajamahendri: the people of that realm are discontented."  
**3.134.** Rama Raya became the ruler and managed the affairs of Rajamandri.  
**3.135.** He showed kindness to me as well: "Come with me," he said.  
**3.136.** In the king's thirteenth anka a famine brought suffering.[^03-73]  
**3.137.** Many people perished; the soldiers were left without food.  
**3.138.** Through Rama Raya's care the people … (??) survived.[^03-74]  
**3.139.** Bhavananda departed to heaven; Ramananda … (?) … (??).[^03-75]  
**3.140.** Gopinatha and Vaninatha were worthy persons for affairs of state;[^03-76]  
**3.141.** Kalanidhi and Sudhanidhi gave their minds to the … (??) science.[^03-77]  
**3.142.** Ramananda, [filled with] the mood (?) of a devotee, [lived] day and night in the joy of kirtana.[^03-78]  
**3.143.** Bathing at a ghat of the Godavari, he devotes himself to the bhajana of the Name.[^03-79]  
**3.144.** In the company of sadhus and Vaishnavas he reads songs and stages dramas.  
**3.145.** He turned over (?) the manuscript of Jayadeva's *Gita* — the ways of rasa.[^03-80]  
**3.146.** Constantly pondering (?) on it (?), he gives his mind to the bhajana of the Name.[^03-81]  
**3.147.** He is immersed in the prema of Radha and Krishna and knows nothing else.  
**3.148.** The king came (?) and rebuked him: "Gather (?) the army," he said.[^03-82]  
**3.149.** "The noose has already tightened (?) round our neck, and now you talk of Krishna-rasa![^03-83]  
**3.150.** At such a time this is not good: equip the army and set [it] in a ring (?)."[^03-84]  
**3.151.** Says Madhava the servant: listen, devotees (?), to another rasa.[^03-85]  

[^03-1]: Printed ଅସ୍ଥ (*astha*), without the long vowels; taken as ଆସ୍ଥା କରି, "with faith, attentively" (a usual formula addressing listeners). The last sign of the half-line looks like ଶ — the usual look of ରି in this font (*kari*), as the rhyme with ମାଧୁରୀ confirms.
[^03-2]: Gumuti: the dome, the inner space of Jagannatha's temple (cf. 2.41–2.50: the devadasis' dance "inside the gumuti of the temple"). Here ଗୁମୁରେ is printed three times (3.2, 3.3, 3.15) and ଶ୍ରୀଗୁମୁରେ in 3.16, without ଟି; taken as ଗୁମୁଟିରେ, "in the gumuti" (?). Rahasa: the temple dance (see 2.1, 2.65).
[^03-3]: Divakara is Kavichandra Raya Divakara (see 3.37), a court poet of the king. The *Abhinava Gita-govinda* ("New Gita-govinda") is usually ascribed to King Purushottama-deva himself; according to Madhava, Divakara composed it and signed it with the king's name (3.7).
[^03-4]: ପାଟ (*pāṭa*) is taken as "chief, principal" (cf. ପାଟମହାଦେଈ, "chief queen"); perhaps "the king's dear and chief [poet]". The reading is sure (rhyme with ନାଟ); the sense is doubtful.
[^03-5]: The bhanita is the closing line of a song naming its author. ମେଲଣି (*melaṇi*) is taken as "rewards, gifts" (?); perhaps ମେଲାଣି, "a parting gift".
[^03-6]: The *Abhinava Gita-govinda*, "New Gita-govinda": an Odishan Sanskrit poem in imitation of Jayadeva.
[^03-7]: The king is Purushottama-deva (r. c. 1467–1497), father of Prataparudra. "Went to heaven" means he passed away.
[^03-8]: Jivadeva is named together with Rudradeva in 1.77 as well; who he was cannot be established from the text.
[^03-9]: Rudradeva is King Prataparudra (r. c. 1497–1540), son of Purushottama, devotee and patron of Mahaprabhu in Puri (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 11–14).
[^03-10]: ନୋହନ୍ତା (*nohantā*) is taken as "are not [the same]"; the ‑ା in ନୋହନ୍ତା and କେମନ୍ତା is apparently for the rhyme. The sense "this dance is not [inferior to] that one" is also possible.
[^03-11]: Printed ଭଲ ଭେଲ ବାଛ (*bhala bhela bācha*): taken as "choose which [turned out] good" (ଭେଲ — an old form "became, was"?). The story of the two books placed before Jagannatha, of which Jayadeva's *Gita-govinda* was found on top the next morning, is known in Odisha; a similar story is also told in Priyadasa's commentary on Nabhaji's *Bhaktamala*.
[^03-12]: Bada Thakura ("the elder lord") here is the late King Purushottama, author of "the New Song"; "the Bada Thakura's songs" are the *Abhinava Gita-govinda*.
[^03-13]: Printed ହେଗବେଳେ; the sign looks like ହ (as in ହେବ, 3.23); taken as ଭୋଗବେଳେ, "at the time of bhoga", the food offering to Jagannatha.
[^03-14]: Sandhya-dhupa (ସଞ୍ଜ ଧୂପ) is the evening food offering; the Bada-singhara is Jagannatha's last, night-time dress before His rest; by temple custom the *Gita-govinda* is sung at it. The first word, ସକଳଠାରୁ (*sakaḷaṭhāru*), is unclear: "from all"? "from morning" (ସକାଳ)? — the second sign is blotted; from the context (vv. 22–24) the Bada Thakura's songs are sung from the evening dhupa until the Bada-singhara.
[^03-15]: Samprada (ସପରଦା, ସପ୍ରଦା): a troupe of temple dancers. Kapileshvara Thakura is King Kapilendra-deva (r. c. 1434/35–1467), founder of the Suryavamsha dynasty and father of Purushottama. The Telangi dancers came from the Telugu-speaking lands.
[^03-16]: ଇତରକେ (*itarake*): the first sign is blotted; taken as "for the others" (ଇତର, "other") — i.e. the Bada Thakura's songs are left to the dancers, and the *Gita-govinda* to the Vaishnava singers (?).
[^03-17]: ମାନନ୍ତ (*mānanta*) is taken as "honour"; it may be a plural ending of ଗାଆଣ, "singers". Mahaprabhu Himself in Puri relished the songs of the *Gita-govinda* day and night with Svarupa Damodara and Ramananda Raya (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 2).
[^03-18]: The Padichha (ପଡ଼ିଛା) is an official who supervises the worship in Jagannatha's temple. The reading is doubtful: printed ପ + an unclear ligature + ଛା.
[^03-19]: The Jaya-Vijaya gate leads into the jagamohana of Jagannatha's temple. An inscription of Prataparudra (usually dated 1499) survives on it, directing the temple dancers and singers to perform Jayadeva's *Gita-govinda* and listing the same troupes as 3.25–3.26. Printed ଦ୍ୱାରସ୍ଥରେ (?) (the ligature after ର is unclear) and ପଥରେ (?) — "on stone".
[^03-20]: Anka: the Odia count of regnal years (see 1.72). The word before ଅଙ୍କେ is unclear: ରୃ/ତୃ/ବୃ + ଶ/ଣ; possibly ବୃଷ, the solar month Vrisha (April–May), usual in Odia dates, or a numeral. The date is fixed by the gate inscription (c. 1499).
[^03-21]: Kavichandra Raya is an honorific title of Divakara (Divakara Mishra). Printed ସତ୍ତର, taken as ସତ୍ୱର, "quickly"; ଉତ୍ତର, "reply", is also possible. His speech continues on leaf 59.
[^03-22]: The speaker is apparently Kavichandra Raya Divakara (3.37), author of Bada-thakura's "New Song": he complains that the servants are not carrying out the royal order on the songs (3.21–3.31). The first sign of the line is a separate «େ» with the ligature this font uses for ଭା; read as ଭୋ, the vocative "O!".
[^03-23]: ମୁହାଁସ (*muhā̃sa*) is taken as "presence, court" (cf. ମୁହାଁ, "facing"); not checked in dictionaries.
[^03-24]: Jivadeva — the opponent of the "New Song" who asked Rudradeva to set it aside (3.12–3.13). The servants are the temple servants who sang Jayadeva's *Gita-govinda*.
[^03-25]: Bada-thakura is the late King Purushottama-deva; his *Gita-govinda* is the *Abhinava Gita-govinda* (see 3.8, 3.18). To tie a manuscript with a string is to lay it aside, to stop reading it. Printed ≈ କଛୁଦନ; read as କିଛୁଦିନ, "some days".
[^03-26]: Jayadeva's *Gita-govinda* is sung in the Jagannatha temple to this day. Mahaprabhu especially loved its songs (cf. 3.29).
[^03-27]: Madhavendra Puri — a sannyasi, guru of Ishvara Puri (3.77) and thus parama-guru of Sri Chaitanya. Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour him as the first sprout of the tree of prema (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 9). His story with Gopala and Kshirachora-Gopinatha is told by Mahaprabhu in CC, Madhya 4.
[^03-28]: Nrisimha — apparently one of the Eastern Ganga kings named Narasimha-deva (13th–14th c.). The second half-line is unclear: ତରେ (*tare*) — "to, for"?; ଚରସ୍ଥ (*carastha*) is unidentified (ଚର "messenger"? "pasture"?). From 3.50, the king brought the murti of Gopala from Govardhana. CC (Madhya 4) differs: Madhavendra himself found Gopala on Govardhana Hill and installed Him there, while the Deity of Remuna is Gopinatha; Madhava calls the Deity of Remuna both Gopala and Gopinatha (3.75).
[^03-29]: Remuna — a village near Balasore in northern Odisha; kataka — a fort, royal camp.
[^03-30]: Printed କ'ଲେ: the apostrophe marks elision in this edition; read as କାଲେ / କାଳେ, "in time, once" (?).
[^03-31]: ଯୁଗଳ (*yugaḷa*), "pair": possibly tears of love from both of Madhavendra's eyes; or the love of the Divine Couple, Radha and Krishna (?). ଲବଣ୍ୟ is printed without the long "a" (= ଲାବଣ୍ୟ, "loveliness").
[^03-32]: Madhava here and below is Madhavendra (the name shortened for the metre), not the author of the poem. The second half-line is understood tentatively: ତହିଁ (?), "there"; ତା ସରାଗ, "with love for Him".
[^03-33]: Kshira — sweet rice cooked in milk, the famous offering to Gopinatha at Remuna. In CC (Madhya 4), Gopinatha hid a pot of kshira behind His cloth and in a dream told the pujari to give it to Madhavendra; there Madhavendra asks for nothing and waits not at bolted doors but in the empty market, chanting the holy name. The word ନବାଡ଼େ (*nabāṛe*) is unclear: "not served" (ନ ବାଢ଼େ)?
[^03-34]: ନୁହଇ (*nuhaï*): the first sign resembles both ନୁ and ଲୁ; taken as "is not": "it is not [mere] prasada — it is nectar" (?).
[^03-35]: କରମ୍ବିତ (Skt. *karambita*, "studded") — apparently as in *pulaka-karambita*, "covered with hairs standing on end": one of the symptoms of ecstasy (sattvika-bhava).
[^03-36]: Kshirachora-Gopinatha ("Gopinatha, the Thief of Kshira") — the Deity of the temple at Remuna; He received this name from the lila just told (CC, Madhya 4). On His way from Bengal to Puri, Mahaprabhu visited Remuna and retold this story to His companions.
[^03-37]: According to Madhava, Madhavendra spent many years at Remuna. According to CC (Madhya 4), fearing fame, he left Remuna that same night and returned later, on his journey with sandalwood for Gopala; Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour his samadhi at Remuna. ଧାନ (*dhāna*) is a simplified spelling of ଧ୍ୟାନ, "meditation".
[^03-38]: Ishvara Puri — disciple of Madhavendra Puri and guru of Sri Chaitanya; CC (Antya 8) describes his service to Madhavendra in his teacher's last days. Raghava — apparently Raghavendra Puri, another disciple of Madhavendra (?).
[^03-39]: Bhavananda Raya — father of Ramananda Raya; Mahaprabhu received him and his sons in Puri, calling him Pandu and his sons the five Pandavas (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 10; cf. Antya 9). According to Madhava, his village was Bentapur. The guest is Madhavendra Puri (see 3.80): after the wonders of Gopala and Kshirachora-Gopinatha at Remuna (leaves 59–60), Bhavananda invites him home.
[^03-40]: Purushottama — Puri (Purushottama-kshetra). Printed ପୁରସ୍ତମ — apparently a colloquial form of ପୁରୁଷୋତ୍ତମ (?); the second word is printed ନଲ, taken as ଗଲ, "went" (possibly ନେଲ "took [the road]" or ଚଲ "walked"). Bentapur — a village in Orissa on the way to Puri.
[^03-41]: Madhava — Madhavendra Puri; Ishvara — Ishvara Puri, his disciple, who later initiated Sri Chaitanya (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Adi 9 and 17); Raghava — another disciple of Madhavendra, unidentified. The *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 4) describes Madhavendra's journey to Puri via Remuna but has no visit to Bhavananda at Bentapur.
[^03-42]: Gini — small hand cymbals keeping the beat in kirtan.
[^03-43]: Anka — the Odia count of regnal years (see 1.72, 3.36). The word before ଅଙ୍କ is the same unclear word as in 3.36 (ରୃ/ତୃ + a sign like ଶ); if it is ତୃ‑ "third", then Phalguna (February–March) of Prataparudra's third anka falls in early 1499 or so — the same time as the edict in 3.33–3.36. A paksha is half a lunar month.
[^03-44]: From this verse the author, Madhava Pattanayaka, tells his own story: orphanhood, the road to Puri, the meeting with Madhavendra Puri, and entering the household of Ramananda Raya.
[^03-45]: Khandapada (Khandapara) — a village in Orissa, Madhava's birthplace. If he was twenty-one around 1499 (see 3.83), he was born around 1478.
[^03-46]: "Passed away" — lit. "became [the prey] of Kala, time". ଭଲେ (*bhale*) taken as "well, decently"; possibly ଭାଳେ "[I] pondered" (printed without ା).
[^03-47]: Printed ଭ + ଳ + ଲି + ଇଁ — taken as ଭାଳିଲିଁ, "I pondered". In the last word the signs ଦ, େ, ଇ stand in an odd order; taken as ଦେଇ, "placing [in the mind]"; possibly ବଢ଼ଇ, "[the thought] grows". The first word of the second half-line is ବୁଦ୍ଧି "resolve" or ବୁଝି "having understood".
[^03-48]: The Kshetra — Sri Kshetra, Puri, the abode of Jagannatha.
[^03-49]: ପଥିକା (?) — printed like ପଥ୍‌କା / ପଥୁକା; taken as ପଥିକ, "wayfarer". ପ୍ରଭାତୁ (here and in 3.97) — "from dawn" (‑ତୁ = ‑ରୁ after ତ).
[^03-50]: Printed ବାର, then the sign େ before ନ; taken as "refusal, prohibition" (= ବାରଣ?): "I refused nothing".
[^03-51]: Printed ଦ୍ୱାର ଭଗେ — taken as ଦ୍ୱାରଭାଗେ, "at the gate" (?), or ଦ୍ୱାର ଆଗେ, "before the gate". ସରଗେ taken as ସରାଗେ, "joyfully, lovingly".
[^03-52]: Gosai (gosvami) — here Madhavendra Puri.
[^03-53]: ତୁଲେ (*tule*) taken as "along with, into [the circle of]"; the first sign is ତୁ or ରୁ. The sense is clear from the context: the youth asks to be admitted to the kirtan.
[^03-54]: Lit. "he will look into your circumstances" (ଉଦନ୍ତ — "situation, news").
[^03-55]: The verb ବରଗିଲ (*baragila*) is not identified; the second sign resembles ଋ. Possibly ବରଣିଲ, "received, welcomed", or ବର୍ଜିଲ, "sent away".
[^03-56]: Ramananda — Ramananda Raya, Bhavananda's eldest son, later a governor under Prataparudra and an intimate associate of Mahaprabhu (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 8). "With a book in his hand" apparently means: engaged in learned work. ବସଇ, "sits", is possibly ବସିଣ, "seated".
[^03-57]: Karana — the Odia caste of scribes and officials; both Ramananda Raya and Madhava himself belonged to it (Pattanayaka is a karana title).
[^03-58]: ମୁହିଁ (*muhi̐*) taken as ମୁହଁ, "face": "one can see from your face that you have studied". If read as "I", the half-line becomes Madhava's reply, but it would then contradict 3.110.
[^03-59]: ନଠାଇ (*naṭhāi*) taken as ନିଠାଇ / ନିଠେଇ, "diligently, attentively" (?). The change of speakers (Madhava — Ramananda) is not marked in the original.
[^03-60]: The author's account of himself (from 3.84) continues: Ramananda Raya took the young Madhava into his house and taught him (3.106–3.110). The palm leaf is the writing material; "took up the palm leaf" apparently means he learned to write. ଖେଦାଏ (*khedāe*) is not identified; understood as "a little" by parallel with ଥୋକାଏ in the first half-line. Ramananda's words in 3.112 mark the beginning of Madhava's service as Ramananda's personal assistant (cf. 1.67).
[^03-61]: The second half-line is unclear: ତାୟାକୁ (*tāẏāku*) is taken as ତାହାକୁ, "him"; ଏକ ଶ୍ରଇ (*eka śraï*) is perhaps ଏକାଶ୍ରୟ, "sole refuge"; ପର (*para*), "as, like".
[^03-62]: The Kshetra is Sri Kshetra, Puri (cf. 3.89). Printed ≈ ଷେ + a sign like ତ୍ର + ‑ୁ; taken as କ୍ଷେତ୍ରୁ, "from the Kshetra" (?). According to Madhava, Madhavendra Puri (the author's "Madhava Puri", "Madhava Gosai") returned from Puri to Remuna, to Kshirachora-Gopinatha (3.48–3.77).
[^03-63]: The fifth anka of Prataparudra is, by conversion, roughly early 1500 (Phalguna is February–March; cf. 3.83). "Departed to heaven" (*swargabāsa*, "took up abode in heaven") is Madhava's usual expression for a departure (cf. 3.11). Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour Madhavendra Puri's departure as his entrance into the Lord's eternal pastimes, not a passage to the heavenly planets; his samadhi is at Remuna, beside the temple of Kshirachora-Gopinatha.
[^03-64]: The king is Prataparudra. Bhavananda is the father of Ramananda Raya (see 3.78).
[^03-65]: Cf. *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Antya 8: in his last days Madhavendra Puri was served by his disciple Ishvara Puri, and he left the world reciting the verse *ayi dīna-dayārdra nātha*, full of longing for Krishna.
[^03-66]: ବର୍ଣ୍ଣିବ (*barṇṇiba*) is the future of the verb "to describe"; taken as the author's words, "I shall describe" (?). ପୋଥା (*pothā*) is a variant of ପୋଥି, "book, manuscript"; ସାମରଥା (*sāmarathā*) is ସାମର୍ଥ୍ୟ, "capability, power".
[^03-67]: *Jagannatha-vallabha* is Ramananda Raya's Sanskrit drama about Radha and Krishna. Mahaprabhu relished its songs (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 2), and Ramananda himself trained two devadasis to perform it (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Antya 5).
[^03-68]: The eighth anka is, by conversion, roughly early 1502. The last word is printed ≈ ଉତ୍ସର (the last sign is like ର); taken as ଉତ୍ସବ, "festival" (cf. ଦୋଳଉତ୍ସବ in 3.127).
[^03-69]: Dola (Dola-yatra, Dola-purnima) is the spring swing festival on the full moon of Phalguna, one of the main festivals of the year in Puri. The Nata-mandira is the hall for dance and performances in the Jagannatha temple. In ପାଞ୍ଚଦିନ and ମନ୍ଦିରେ the «ି» is not visible (printed ≈ ପାଞ୍ଚଦନ, ମନ୍ଦରେ).
[^03-70]: "Raya" (*rāẏa*) is an honorific title; it is the name by which Ramananda is known in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* ("Ramananda Raya", "Raya").
[^03-71]: The word ଛଡ଼ି (*chaṛi*, "staff, rod") is read uncertainly (the first sign looks like ନ with flourishes); a golden staff is a mark of honour. The second half-line apparently concerns the performance: devotees play the roles of Radha and Krishna.
[^03-72]: Rajamandri / Rajamahendri (today Rajahmundry) is a town on the Godavari, then a southern province of the Gajapati realm. In the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 7–8) Ramananda Raya is governor at Vidyanagara on the Godavari; there Mahaprabhu met him on His way south (in Madhava, chapter 4). The ninth anka is, by conversion, about 1503. ଅଜ୍ଞା (*ajñā*) = ଆଜ୍ଞା, "command".
[^03-73]: The thirteenth anka is, by conversion, about 1506–1507. ଦୁର୍ଭିକ୍ଷ, "famine", is printed ≈ ଦୁଭକ୍ଷ.
[^03-74]: The last word (printed ≈ ଶ + ୟ + ଲେ; in this font ଶ may also be ରି) is not identified; by the sense perhaps "all, everywhere" (cf. ସକଳେ?).
[^03-75]: According to Madhava, Bhavananda departed about 1507. In the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* Bhavananda Raya with his sons meets Mahaprabhu after His return from the south (Madhya 10) and is mentioned in the story of Gopinatha Pattanayaka (Antya 9), i.e. he is alive in the 1510s. The second half-line is unclear: printed ≈ ଦୁବଳ କ୍ରୟା; if ଦୁର୍ବଳ କାୟା, "wasted his body [with grief]"; if କ୍ରିୟା, "performed the [funeral] rites".
[^03-76]: Gopinatha (Pattanayaka), Vaninatha, Kalanidhi and Sudhanidhi are Ramananda's brothers, the sons of Bhavananda; the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 10) calls them, together with Ramananda, the five Pandavas. Vaninatha served Mahaprabhu in Puri; the story of Gopinatha Pattanayaka is in CC, Antya 9.
[^03-77]: The first word of the second half-line is «େ» + ଷ (= କ୍ଷ) + the sign seen in 3.114 (≈ ତ୍ର); taken as କ୍ଷେତ୍ର (??). Possibly a misprint for କ୍ଷାତ୍ର / କ୍ଷତ୍ର, "martial [science]": then Kalanidhi and Sudhanidhi took up warfare, while Gopinatha and Vaninatha took up administration.
[^03-78]: The second word is printed with a ligature that resembles both ରା and ଭା, + ବ; taken as ଭାବ, "mood" (?).
[^03-79]: The Godavari is the river on whose bank Mahaprabhu met and conversed with Ramananda Raya (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 8; in Madhava, chapter 4). ସ୍ନାହାନ (*snāhāna*) is ସ୍ନାନ, "bathing".
[^03-80]: Jayadeva's *Gita* is the *Gita-govinda* (cf. 3.44). The verb is printed ≈ «େ» + ଲ with a hook + ଟ + ଇ + ଲ; taken as ଲେଉଟଇଲ, "turned over, reread" (?). ରସରୀତି, "the ways (customs) of rasa", is apparently what he read.
[^03-81]: The first sign of the second word is broken; taken as ଏଥି, "on this" (i.e. on the *Gita-govinda*) (?). ଗୁଣଇ, "ponders, recites" (?). ନିରନ୍ତର is printed without «ି».
[^03-82]: The king is Prataparudra. Apparently the threat of war on the southern frontier is meant (perhaps with Vijayanagara, whose army occupied the southern possessions of the Gajapati in the 1510s) (?). ଭେଲାଅ (*bhelāa*) is taken as ମେଳାଅ, "gather" (?); the first sign looks like ଭ.
[^03-83]: The verb is printed ≈ ଲଗିଲଟି; taken as ଲଗିଲଣି (= ଲାଗିଲାଣି), "has already tightened" (?).
[^03-84]: ବେଢ଼ାଏ (*beṛhāe*), from ବେଢ଼ା, "enclosure, ring"; understood as "in a ring, around" (?).
[^03-85]: The closing couplet of the chapter with the author's name; chapter 4 begins on the next leaf. ଭକତେ, "devotees", is printed indistinctly (the second sign looks like ବ/ର); ଆନ ରସ, "another rasa", apparently means "another tale", i.e. the next chapter.

---

# Madhava Pattanayaka. *Vaishnava-lilamrita*, Chapter 4
## Gajapati Prataparudra: the Southern War and the Raid on Puri


**4.1.** Rudradeva is that Gajapati whose might (?) [even] the gods do not know.[^04-1]  
**4.2.** He is born of a portion of Vishnu; he is of the Solar dynasty, renowned by name.[^04-2]  
**4.3.** In all three worlds he is the one king; his greatness is beyond measure.  
**4.4.** He subdued all kings, overawing [them] with campaigns that cut roads [through the forests].[^04-3]  
**4.5.** Great in might, great in valour, he rules his people and subjects in prosperity.  
**4.6.** With thousands of warriors, with a campaigning (?) army, he again marched south.[^04-4]  
**4.7.** At Udayagiri [and] Chandragiri he valiantly made his camp.[^04-5]  
**4.8.** In the fifteenth anka the uparaya (??) fights with Krishnaraya.[^04-6]  
**4.9.** From the north Ghazi (?) Ismail marched (?) against the Kshetra.[^04-7]  
**4.10.** The servitors said to the Lord: "A great peril has come."[^04-8]  
**4.11.** The servitors carried off and hid [the Deities] and came to the fort of Kenkal.[^04-9]  
**4.12.** Seating the Deities on elephants, Nrisimha Uparaya (?) leads [Them] away.[^04-10]  
**4.13.** In the midst of Lake Chilika, inside the … (??) of Chadhei[^04-11]  
**4.14.** they hid the Deities. A Pana (?) went to the south[^04-12]  
**4.15.** [and] on the bank of the Krishnaveni tells the king the news.[^04-13]  
**4.16.** Hearing it, Pratapa, best of kings, spoke in anger:  
**4.17.** "While I live — what deed (?) is this: the Lord handed over (?) to the Yavanas!"[^04-14]  
**4.18.** [The messenger] brought word: "Chadhei-guha" — and hearing it, the jewel among kings grew calm.[^04-15]  
**4.19.** He arrives at the fort of Kenkal and, contrite, beholds [the Deities].  
**4.20.** He said: "O Lord, have mercy: this kingdom is Yours.  
**4.21.** By Your strength I am strong: I will slay the Yavanas — what are they, the wretches (?)!"[^04-16]  
**4.22.** Making Jagannatha's name his rosary, the king marches in haste (?).[^04-17]  
**4.23.** He drove off the mlecchas and went as far as [the place] called Mandaragada.[^04-18]  
**4.24.** He quickly came back; but when he went [again] to the south,  
**4.25.** a most terrible battle broke out, and the king was sorely distressed.  
**4.26.** He said: "O Lord, protect: this kingdom is Yours."[^04-19]  
**4.27.** He constantly remembers the Name, fixing his mind on the Lord's feet.[^04-20]  

## Sri Chaitanya's arrival in Puri and meeting with Ramananda Raya


**4.28.** At that time there came to the Kshetra a new, young yati, a sannyasi, together with a company of devotees.  
**4.29.** His name was Krishna Chaitanya: He was as the Lord Himself, manifest before the eyes.[^04-21]  
**4.30.** Beholding Jagannatha, He falls senseless in prema.[^04-22]  
**4.31.** Sarvabhauma, who saw [this], had Him carefully tended (??).[^04-23]  
**4.32.** He took Him to his own house, and the devotees too came there.  
**4.33.** They went to the temple for darshan, and He Himself partook of prasad.  
**4.34.** For a whole month in the Kshetra their discussion of the shastras grew ever richer.[^04-24]  
**4.35.** For about a month He stayed in the Kshetra, frequenting the temple.  
**4.36.** At the foot of Bata-Ganesha, in plain view, Jagannatha Dasa would sit.[^04-25]  
**4.37.** He had built a pavilion there for rahasa and installed the murti of Radha-Krishna.[^04-26]  
**4.38.** He was composing the Bhagavata, and the devotees listened attentively.  
**4.39.** All the bhava of the Bhagavata he sang day after day in a sweet voice.  
**4.40.** One day, in the leisure of evening, before a host of devotees, he was in particular  
**4.41.** expounding the meaning of the Bhagavata — and Chaitanya came and joined them.  
**4.42.** Hearing the exposition of the Bhagavata, He understood the swami's bhava.  
**4.43.** Tears trickle from the eyes; boundless thrills run through the body.[^04-27]  
**4.44.** Such great bhava in Jagannatha [Dasa] Chaitanya discerned.[^04-28]  
**4.45.** He said: 'You are the best of devotees; your understanding is nourished by the bhava of devotion.  
**4.46.** Where could I obtain your bhava? I will not let go of your company!'[^04-29]  
**4.47.** For two and a half praharas the two remained in each other's embrace.[^04-30]  
**4.48.** Then, sitting together, they praised one another.  
**4.49.** Chaitanya called him 'Swami', and he himself called Him 'Prabhu'.[^04-31]  
**4.50.** As a river merging with the ocean — the waves of bhava rose high.[^04-32]  
**4.51.** He met with Sarvabhauma and studied the shastras with him.  
**4.52.** Sarvabhauma, a great logician, versed in nyaya, deeply learned,[^04-33]  
**4.53.** held many learned disputations with the Lord in the Kshetra.  
**4.54.** He saw the Lord's devotion: the Lord's heart was by its very nature full of prema.  
**4.55.** Whenever the talk turned to devotion, sattvika bhavas appeared on the Lord's body:[^04-34]  
**4.56.** His lips trembled, His body sweated; there was shaking, bhava, and rapturous dance.  
**4.57.** Seeing this, Sarvabhauma said: 'Are You not the Lord Himself?[^04-35]  
**4.58.** I do not know the bhava of devotion: with a dry heart I [deal only] in logical proofs.'[^04-36]  
**4.59.** [Sarvabhauma] spent his time with the Lord. [Then] the Lord said: 'I shall go on pilgrimage.'  
**4.60.** [Sarvabhauma:] 'When You go to the southern land, seek the company of Ramaraya.[^04-37]  
**4.61.** He knows the truth of devotion; his mind is set on the path of raga.[^04-38]  
**4.62.** I do not know the talk of Krishna; Rama Raya knows it all.  
**4.63.** When You go to the southern land, be sure to meet Rama Raya.'  
**4.64.** He set out on His southern journey and came to the bank of the Godavari.[^04-39]  
**4.65.** Having bathed and purified Himself, He had spread His seat at the foot of a tree.[^04-40]  
**4.66.** His lustre was by nature golden — one might mistake Him for the rising sun.  
**4.67.** As if fashioned of gold, His body shone fair.  
**4.68.** His arms reached to His knees; His wide eyes were lifted up (?).[^04-41]  
**4.69.** A brahminical radiance flashed from Him; a sweet smile flowed from His lips.  
**4.70.** At this time Ramaraya came in state [to the river] and was bathing at the ghat,[^04-42]
