**4.71.** with his retinue; herald-drums and kettledrums resounded.[^04-43]  
**4.72.** Ramaraya rose from the ghat, and the Raya set off in his palanquin.[^04-44]  
**4.73.** Rising from the foot of the tree, Chaitanya ran up to meet him:  
**4.74.** 'Are you Ramaraya? You look like a king.'[^04-45]  
**4.75.** At once they embraced — as if lightning had merged with a cloud.[^04-46]  
**4.76.** [Chaitanya:] 'I have come and met you — behold my good fortune!'  
**4.77.** Then Ramaraya said: 'Come, pray, to my home;  
**4.78.** stay here as my guest, and we shall converse on the shastras.  
**4.79.** By my good fortune You have come and granted me the company of a sadhu.'  
**4.80.** The two became absorbed in conversation and spent ten days so.[^04-47]  
**4.81.** In verses (?), joining many meanings, Ramaraya told his account.[^04-48]  

## The Conversation on the Godavari: Radha's Prema and Mahabhava


**4.82.** [He set forth] the order of the path of bhakti and the summit of the path of raga.[^04-49]  
**4.83.** He expounded many truths and imparted much knowledge (?):  
**4.84.** all the bhavas of the gopi devotees — through the meaning of the ślokas of the *Bhagavata*.[^04-50]  
**4.85.** He determined the sadhya [— the highest goal] and set forth (?) bhajana and the One who is worshipped.  
**4.86.** The *Brahma-samhita*, the *Karnamrita*, Jayadeva's *Gita-govinda* —[^04-51]  
**4.87.** all the bhavas of the path of raga he expounded at once (?).  
**4.88.** "The devotee's bhava is higher (?)," he says, praising (?) prema.  
**4.89.** "Prema alone is the goal of life, the limit of the path of bhakti."[^04-52]  
**4.90.** Raya says: "Kanta-prema — this path of bhajana is the highest.  
**4.91.** Worship Krishna in the mood of a beloved — this is the summit of the path of sadhana."[^04-53]  
**4.92.** Still Gora was not satisfied: "Tell [Me] the way further," He said.[^04-54]  
**4.93.** At last Ramaraya says: "The highest goal is Radha's prema."  
**4.94.** Radha's sweet bhava Raya told in full.  
**4.95.** "Krishna's energies are three: sandhini (?), sambandhini and hladini.[^04-55]  
**4.96.** Hladini is Krishna's svarupa-shakti: such is its name (?).  
**4.97.** Hladini is bliss: she gives bliss to Krishna.  
**4.98.** The very energy hladini is Radhika; in prema she knows no hesitation.[^04-56]  
**4.99.** The boundless (?) hladini is Radha: she always enchants Krishna.  
**4.100.** The essence of hladini is prema; she is Krishna's life.  
**4.101.** [Radha is] the crown jewel of all beloveds, the embodiment of mahabhava.[^04-57]  
**4.102.** Krishna is within her and without; she sees Krishna in everything.  
**4.103.** Mahabhava is of two [kinds]: they are called rudha and adhirudha.[^04-58]  
**4.104.** The highest [stage] of adhirudha mahabhava is called (?) madana.  
**4.105.** Radha is madana: such is the definition of mahabhava.  
**4.106.** Mahabhava is the cintamani," [so he said,] describing Radha's own nature.[^04-59]  
**4.107.** "The lila is one — [yet] in two parts (?); Radha's bhava is causeless.[^04-60]  
**4.108.** Whoever becomes a follower in Radha's bhava will certainly attain Krishna.[^04-61]  
**4.109.** Apart from this there is no other way — so it is made manifest in the shastras (?).  
**4.110.** In separation from Krishna Radha pines; tears flow from her eyes.  
**4.111.** The torment of separation from Govinda — apart from it there is no other path.  
**4.112.** Whoever worships Krishna in that bhava certainly attains Krishna."  
**4.113.** He told this supreme truth — the limit of the path of bhakti.  
**4.114.** "Is there still any doubt in You? Ask without hesitation.[^04-62]  
**4.115.** Are You not the Lord Himself? Come and set my heart at rest (?).  
**4.116.** You are the avatara of Radha; [here] You taste Your own nature."[^04-63]  
**4.117.** Prabhu says: "Speak, speak! Radha's bhava is unbearable.  
**4.118.** Hearing it from your lips, I, My doubt dispelled, come to Myself (??)."[^04-64]  
**4.119.** Ramaraya recites the śloka: "At first raga arose from the play of glances…"[^04-65]  
**4.120.** Prabhu's whole body trembles; tears bathe (??) His chest;  
**4.121.** from bhava — trembling, the body flushes (?), [the hairs stand on end] like kadamba filaments.  
**4.122.** Placing [His] hand on Ramaraya's lips, [Prabhu] said: "Enough, enough (?) — [do not] speak."[^04-66]  
**4.123.** He embraced Raya; in time [He] came to Himself (?).[^04-67]  
**4.124.** He told the supreme truth and dispelled all doubts without remainder.  

## "You Are My Guru": The Southern Pilgrimage and Ramananda's Move to Nilachala


**4.125.** Prabhu said: "You are My guru: from your lips I have learned the truth.[^04-68]  
**4.126.** This position of yours as guru I shall keep remembering and never for a moment let go from My mind."  
**4.127.** Raya said: "[I am] the lowest of shudras; I am [only] a repository (??) of the evidence of the shastras.[^04-69]  
**4.128.** Counting (?) [me] as a guru, [You are only] making fun of me (?): I am a lowly one."[^04-70]  
**4.129.** Prabhu said: "You gave instruction; you taught a rare truth.  
**4.130.** I heard of your greatness, and therefore (?) I came to you.  
**4.131.** You made all truth plainly visible: the treasure of prema, that whirlpool of rasa (?),[^04-71]  
**4.132.** the teaching on the hladini energy — where [else] would I have found these?  
**4.133.** From today you are My guru. [To take] a twice-born for a shudra is an error."[^04-72]  
**4.134.** So [He] stayed (?) [there] ten days, and then went on to the south.[^04-73]  
**4.135.** Setubandha and Rameshvara, Sri Ranga, Udupi, Shankara (?) —[^04-74]  
**4.136.** how many holy places (?) there were! [At last] He came and reached Prayaga.[^04-75]  
**4.137.** Wandering through many tirthas, [visiting them] one after another (?) with Hari-nama,  
**4.138.** [spreading] Krishna-katha — the essence of rasa — and the kirtana of the Name,  
**4.139.** He arrived at Rajamandri (?) and says to Ramaraya: "Come!"[^04-76]  
**4.140.** Do not stay (?) here even a moment; leave the royal service at once.[^04-77]  
**4.141.** To spend My days in Nilachala without you, guru, is pointless."  
**4.142.** Raya said: "So be it (?)," and he said: "Go on ahead."[^04-78]  
**4.143.** Prabhu returned (?) — He arrived in Nilachala.[^04-79]  
**4.144.** In the nineteenth anka of the king, Raya arrived in Nilachala.[^04-80]  
**4.145.** The king said: "Good," and entrusted him with the charge of the temple:[^04-81]  
**4.146.** "Look after the loss and gain of the temple — this is your task."  
**4.147.** Ramaraya agreed: [now] he manages the affairs of the temple.[^04-82]  
**4.148.** Summoning Sri Jivadeva, [the king said]: "You go to Rajamahendri (?)."[^04-83]  

## Jivadeva Kavi-dindima in Rajamahendri; Ramananda and the Vaishnavas in Shri Kshetra


**4.149.** ["]There manage the affairs of state and keep the army with you.["],[^04-84]  
**4.150.** [The king] gave Jivadeva the post and named him "Bahinipati" (?).[^04-85]  
**4.151.** Jivadeva was mighty, and in the devotee's bhava great in understanding.[^04-86]  
**4.152.** He is the rajaguru; he is called "Kavi-dindima".[^04-87]  
**4.153.** He composed many poems and plays and displayed a poet's gift.  
**4.154.** He composed the *Bhagata-vaibhava* and the drama *Utsahavati* (?).[^04-88]  
**4.155.** [People] watched these plays — so it was. [Then] he received the king's leave to depart,[^04-89]  
**4.156.** stayed in Rajamahendri and gathered an army.  
**4.157.** Ramaraya, living in the Kshetra, looks after (??) the sadhu Vaishnavas.[^04-90]  
**4.158.** He built a mandapa near the northern side (?) [of the temple].[^04-91]  
**4.159.** There the Vaishnavas gathered as a community and gave their minds to the rasa of Krishna.  
**4.160.** The days flowed by (?) in the rasa of Krishna, says Madhava joyfully.[^04-92]  

[^04-1]: Rudradeva is King Prataparudra (r. c. 1497–1540), Mahaprabhu's patron in Puri (*Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 11–14); Gajapati, 'lord of elephants', is the title of the kings of Orissa. The second half-line is taken as *pratāpa debe na jāṇanti*, 'the gods do not know [his] might', i.e. it cannot be measured; read as one word, 'Pratapa-deva', the sense is unclear.
[^04-2]: The Gajapatis of Kapilendra's line styled themselves the Solar dynasty (Surya-vamsha). 'A portion of Vishnu' is courtly praise of the king. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas something else matters more: in the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* Prataparudra is a great devotee who swept the road before Jagannatha's chariot (Madhya 13).
[^04-3]: Benta-bhiana: clearing forest and making a road for an advancing army; here, campaigns of conquest in general.
[^04-4]: The word before *sena* ('army') is read as ଦଣ୍ଡ, *daṇḍa*, 'chastisement, military expedition'; the first sign also resembles ଘ. The rhyme *sena* / *puṇi* is imperfect.
[^04-5]: Udayagiri and Chandragiri are fortresses in the south (now Andhra Pradesh), the frontier of the Gajapati realm in the wars with Vijayanagara. 'Made his camp': lit. 'made a *kaṭaka*' (army, war camp).
[^04-6]: Krishnaraya is Krishnadeva Raya, king of Vijayanagara (r. 1509–1529), Prataparudra's adversary in the south. Anka: the Odia count of regnal years (see 1.72); Prataparudra's fifteenth anka falls about 1509–1510. The word ଉପରାୟ (*uparāẏa*) is unclear: a title ('sub-king, deputy'? cf. 'Nrisimha Uparaya' in 4.12) or 'falling upon [him]' (ଉପରେ 'above' + ଆଏ?). Who fights whom is not entirely clear from the line.
[^04-7]: Ismail Ghazi was a general of the Bengal sultan Alauddin Husain Shah; about 1509–1510, while Prataparudra was at war in the south, he raided Orissa and Puri. Printed ଗ ଜ (spaced, without ା and ି), taken as ଗାଜି, 'Ghazi'. The verb ଚଢ଼ଟି (?) is taken as 'marching in force' (ଚଢ଼ି). The *Chaitanya-bhagavata* (Antya 4) calls Husain Shah the destroyer of Orissa's temples and murtis; on the hostility at the Orissa–Bengal border in Mahaprabhu's time, see *Chaitanya-charitamrita*, Madhya 16.
[^04-8]: The servitors (sevakas) of Jagannatha's temple address the Lord Himself.
[^04-9]: Gada-Kenkal, the fort of Kenkal: its location cannot be established from the text.
[^04-10]: The Deities of Puri were taken away from the raid on elephants. Nrisimha Uparaya is apparently the official in charge of this; *uparāẏa* is probably his title (cf. 4.8).
[^04-11]: Chilika is the great lagoon south-west of Puri. Chadhei (*caṛhei*, 'bird') is Chadhei-guha, 'the Bird Cave' (4.18), an island hill in Chilika where, by Puri tradition, the Deities were sheltered from raids. The word କୁଁମାକ (*ku̐māka*) is not identified: an unclear sign (ଁ? ୀ?) stands over ମ; by sense, 'of the cave, shelter'.
[^04-12]: Printed ପାଣେକ, taken as ପାଣ ଏକ, 'one Pana' (the Pana are an Odia caste from which runners and drummers were drawn); the third sign also resembles ଫ.
[^04-13]: The Krishnaveni is the Krishna river in the south, where the king was with his army.
[^04-14]: The last word is read as ଅର୍ପିଲ, 'handed over, delivered': the king apparently took the news to mean that the Lord had fallen into the Yavanas' power. The last word of the first half-line is ବେଭର (?) = ବେଭାର, 'deed, conduct'. The rhyme *bebhara* / *arpila* is imperfect; one of the words may be corrupt.
[^04-15]: Understood thus: the messenger reported that the Deities were sheltered in Chadhei-guha. Another reading is possible: '[they] brought [the Deities] from [the place] called Chadhei-guha'; this would explain why in 4.19 the king beholds Them at the fort of Kenkal.
[^04-16]: The last word is printed ଛ + a sign like ୁ + ର; taken as ଛାର, 'worthless, contemptible'.
[^04-17]: That is, the king repeats Jagannatha's name unceasingly, as on a rosary. The last word is printed like ଝରତରେ, taken as ତ୍ୱରିତରେ, 'in haste'.
[^04-18]: Mandaragada is Garh Mandaran, a fortress on the Orissa–Bengal frontier (now Hooghly district, West Bengal). The mlecchas here are the Bengal sultan's army.
[^04-19]: The second half-line repeats 4.20 word for word.
[^04-20]: The next verse (4.28) begins the account of Sri Chaitanya's arrival in Puri, 'at this time': Madhava thus places His coming in the period of the southern war and the raid. By the *Chaitanya-charitamrita* (Madhya 6), Mahaprabhu came to Puri soon after accepting sannyasa (1510).
[^04-21]: Sākṣāt īśvara samāna — lit. 'equal to Ishvara, manifest'. The author conveys how the Lord appeared to the people of Puri; below (v. 57) Sarvabhauma asks outright whether He is not the Lord Himself. According to the Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching expounded by Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati after the Chaitanya-charitamrita (Adi 1–4), Sri Krishna Chaitanya is Svayam Bhagavan Krishna Himself, who has assumed the mood and lustre of Sri Radha; samāna does not mean 'merely like' here.
[^04-22]: Cf. CC Madhya 6.1–14 and CB Antya 2: on seeing Jagannatha, Mahaprabhu falls senseless in ecstasy; Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya has Him carried to his house. Madhava keeps the same order ('first Jagannatha, then Sarvabhauma's house') in his Chaitanya-vilasa (according to B. B. Majumdar).
[^04-23]: Uncertain reading: the printed ସାଷ୍ଟାମ ଯତକ (sāṣṭāma yataka) is not found in the dictionaries. From the sense and from CC (Madhya 6), Sarvabhauma took care of the unconscious sannyasi (had Him carried, revived). Possible corruptions of sāṣṭāṅga (full prostration) and yatana (care). Question for a specialist — QUESTIONS.md.
[^04-24]: According to CC (Madhya 7.3–5), Mahaprabhu came to Puri in Phalguna and left for the south in Vaishakha, i.e. about two months later; Madhava says 'a month'. The 'discussion of the shastras' corresponds in CC (Madhya 6) to Sarvabhauma's seven-day exposition of the Vedanta-sutra and the debate that followed.
[^04-25]: Jagannatha Dasa — Odia Vaishnava poet, author of the Odia Bhagavata, first of the 'five friends' (Panchasakha). Bata-Ganesha — the shrine of Ganesha by the Kalpa-vata banyan in the Jagannatha temple compound. The meeting of Sri Chaitanya with Jagannatha Dasa belongs to the Odia tradition (Divakara Dasa and others); the Gaudiya biographies (CC, CB) do not have it. We translate the author's text as it stands.
[^04-26]: Rahasa (Odia rahāsa, from rāsa) — the enactment of the pastimes of Radha and Krishna in song and dance; Madhava describes the rahasa custom of Puri in ch. 2. The murti of Radha-Krishna is an installed Deity, not an image.
[^04-27]: The subject is not named: the tears and thrills may belong to Chaitanya hearing the Bhagavata or to Jagannatha Dasa himself; the next couplet ('such bhava in Jagannatha Chaitanya discerned') points rather to the latter.
[^04-28]: Printed ଠାନ (ṭhāna), but the rhyme with ଭାବ (bhāba) requires ଠାବ (ṭhāba); ṭhāba karibā — 'to locate, discern' (Brooks 1874, p. 103: ṭhāba — 'a place').
[^04-29]: Mahaprabhu's words that He cannot obtain Jagannatha Dasa's bhava show the humility with which the Lord, playing the part of a devotee, extols His devotees (cf. His praise of Ramananda Raya in CC Madhya 8). According to the Gaudiya teaching followed by Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself in the form and mood of a devotee; in exalting a devotee He teaches humility, not that anyone is above Him.
[^04-30]: A prahara is a 'watch', an eighth of a day (≈3 hours); two and a half praharas ≈ seven and a half hours. The reading କୋଲାଗ୍ରତେ is uncertain (one would expect କୋଳାଗତେ, 'in embrace').
[^04-31]: That is, Chaitanya respectfully called Jagannatha Dasa 'swami', and he called Him 'Prabhu', 'Lord'. So the Odia tradition expresses their mutual regard.
[^04-32]: The reading ଉଭରିଲ (ubharila) rests on the agreement of passes A and B and on the same verb in v. 68 (ଉଭରି). Cf. Purnachandra (vol. 1, PDF leaf 1045): ଉଭା 'standing, erect', ଉଭାରିବା 'to raise, lift; to stand up' (with a quotation from Jagannatha Dasa's Bhagavata); hence 'the waves of bhava rose up'. Not in Brooks.
[^04-33]: Vasudeva Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya — the famous Naiyayika (logician of the Nyaya school), court pandit of Prataparudra (CC Madhya 6).
[^04-34]: Sattvika bhavas — the eight bodily signs of ecstatic love: stupor, perspiration, horripilation, faltering voice, trembling, change of colour, tears and fainting (Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu 2.3).
[^04-35]: In CC (Madhya 6) Sarvabhauma recognises Mahaprabhu as the Lord when He shows him His six-armed form, and composes verses in His praise; in Madhava, Sarvabhauma's awakening comes from the sight of the Lord's sattvika bhavas.
[^04-36]: Paramāṇe = pramāṇe — 'in proofs': Sarvabhauma confesses that his learning is dry logic without bhakti; cf. CC Madhya 6, where before his conversion he expounds Vedanta in the spirit of advaita.
[^04-37]: The change of speaker is not marked; by sense vv. 60–63 are Sarvabhauma's reply (cf. CC Madhya 7.62–67: Sarvabhauma begs Mahaprabhu to meet Ramananda Raya on the Godavari and not to slight him as a shudra and a worldly man). The future forms yiba, labhiba, bheṭiba are rendered here as addressed to the second person.
[^04-38]: Raga-marga — the path of spontaneous love (raganuga bhakti) in the mood of the residents of Vraja, as distinct from the path of injunctions (vidhi). In CC (Madhya 8) Ramananda Raya is the master of rasa through whom Mahaprabhu reveals the highest truths of bhakti.
[^04-39]: The southern pilgrimage — CC Madhya 7–9. Madhava passes straight to the meeting on the Godavari, omitting the way through Kurmakshetra and Jiyada-Nrisimha.
[^04-40]: The meeting with Ramananda takes place on the bank of the Godavari near Rajamahendri (Kovvur/Rajahmundry); cf. CC Madhya 8.11–23. The forms hoiachu, pārachu (ending -chu) are unusual (in literary Odia this is the 2nd person singular) — question for a specialist.
[^04-41]: 'Arms reaching to the knees' (ājānu-lambita-bhuja) is one of the marks of a mahapurusha; Madhava describes the Lord as Ramananda sees Him, cf. the description in CC Madhya 8 (effulgence like many suns, lotus eyes). The meaning of ଉଭରି (ubhari) is from context.
[^04-42]: Ramananda Raya — son of Bhavananda Raya, governor of Rajamahendri under Prataparudra; Madhava served under him (ch. 3). ବଜେ (baje) is taken as ବିଜେ (bije) — the honorific 'deigns to go, proceeds' (of kings and Deities). The literal sense of v. 70 is not wholly clear.
[^04-43]: Ṭamaka — the herald's drum, niśāṇa — great kettledrums: as governor of the region Ramananda goes out with music and retinue; cf. CC Madhya 8 (he comes to bathe in a palanquin, with music and brahmanas).
[^04-44]: ହାଣ୍ଡୋଳା (hāṇḍoḷā?) — the conjunct is uncertain. In Purnachandra (vol. 7, PDF leaf 1171) ହାଣ୍ଡୋଳା is 'a small burnt earthen temple for village Deities', which does not fit here; by context a palanquin or litter is expected (cf. CC Madhya 8: dolāya caḍi', 'seated in a palanquin'; Odia ଦୋଳା, ହିନ୍ଦୋଳା). The rendering 'palanquin' is provisional. ବଜେକ = ବିଜେ with the particle -କ (cf. କଲେକ, ହେଲେକ in this same text).
[^04-45]: Cf. CC Madhya 8: Mahaprabhu asks, 'Are you Raya Ramananda?' and, hearing 'yes', embraces him.
[^04-46]: Golden Gauranga and dark Ramananda — 'lightning and a cloud', a traditional image (in CC both weep with love at this meeting and fall to the ground).
[^04-47]: According to CC (Madhya 8), Mahaprabhu spent about ten days with Ramananda in talks about Krishna.
[^04-48]: There follows in Madhava the dialogue of Ramananda and Chaitanya (cf. the 'Ramananda-samvada', CC Madhya 8.57 ff., where Ramananda answers with verses of the shastras). The first word is printed ପଦ୍ମରେ (padmare, 'in the lotus'), which makes no sense; we read ପଦ୍ୟରେ (padyare, 'in verses') — question for a specialist. The pilot ends here.
[^04-49]: Continuation of Ramananda's account (4.81). Vidhi-marga is the path of rules; raga-marga is the path of spontaneous love (cf. pilot, 4.62). Printed «ବଧି» (*badhi*), read ବିଧି (*bidhi*, "rule"): the ି sign, as often in this edition, did not print; the rhyme with ଅବଧି confirms.
[^04-50]: Cf. CC, Madhya 8: Ramananda answers Mahaprabhu's questions with ślokas of the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* and other shastras; the bhava of the gopis is at the centre of his answers.
[^04-51]: The sources Ramananda draws on in Madhava: the *Brahma-samhita*, Bilvamangala's *Krishna-karnamrita* and Jayadeva's *Gita-govinda* (on Jayadeva see ch. 2). According to CC (Madhya 9), Mahaprabhu found the *Brahma-samhita* and the *Krishna-karnamrita* on His southern pilgrimage and brought them to Puri; the *Gita-govinda* He relished in Puri with Ramananda and Svarupa Damodara (CC, Madhya 2). Printed «ବ୍ରହ୍ମସହିତା» (without ଂ) = *Brahma-samhita*.
[^04-52]: Prema is the "fifth purushartha", the highest goal of life, surpassing dharma, artha, kama and moksha (CC, Adi 7).
[^04-53]: In CC (Madhya 8) Ramananda, rising from varnashrama, names the stages — dasya, sakhya, vatsalya and finally kanta-prema ("this is the limit of sadhya"), and then Radha's prema as the crown of all sadhyas. Madhava gives this ladder in brief.
[^04-54]: Cf. CC, Madhya 8: to each answer Mahaprabhu says, "This is external; tell Me further." Literally "Gora did not understand" — i.e. did not accept the answer as final.
[^04-55]: The three internal energies of the Lord — sandhini (existence), samvit (knowledge) and hladini (bliss); cf. CC, Adi 4 and Madhya 8. Printed «ସମ୍ବନ୍ଧିନୀ» (*sambandhinī*, "the connecting one") where the doctrine expects samvit; the first sign of «ସନ୍ଧିନୀ» is unclearly printed. "Hladini" is printed with an abbreviated ligature (≈ ଲଦନୀ), below as «ଆହ୍ଲାଦିନୀ».
[^04-56]: The particle ‑ଟି (*‑ṭi*: ଶକ୍ତିଟି, ଅଟଇଟି 4.100, 4.105, ଏହୁଟି 4.109) is taken as emphatic ("the very, precisely"); a question for a specialist.
[^04-57]: Cf. CC, Madhya 8: the essence of hladini is prema, the essence of prema is bhava, the highest stage of bhava is mahabhava; Sri Radha is the embodiment of mahabhava and the crown jewel of all Krishna's beloveds.
[^04-58]: The division of mahabhava into rudha and adhirudha, and the highest stage of adhirudha — madana, found only in Sri Radha — follow Srila Rupa Gosvami's *Ujjvala-nilamani*. The word «ବେଭାର» (*bebhāra*, 4.96, 4.104) is taken as "usage, designation" (= *byabahāra*) — uncertain.
[^04-59]: Cf. CC, Madhya 8: mahabhava is the cintamani; such is Radha's own nature.
[^04-60]: Cf. CC, Adi 4: Radha and Krishna are one self who assumed two forms to relish the rasa of lila. "Causeless" (*ahetuka*) — unmotivated and unconditioned (cf. *Bhagavatam* 1.2.6). The reading «ବେନିଭାଗ» (*beni* "two" + *bhāga* "part"; printed ≈ ବେନଭାଗ) is uncertain.
[^04-61]: Cf. CC, Madhya 8: no one enters this lila without the sakhis; whoever follows the sakhis in their mood attains the service of Radha and Krishna in the kunjas.
[^04-62]: The change of speaker is not marked. By sense 4.114–116 are Ramananda's words to Mahaprabhu; the intimate "you" (*tora, tui, tu*) is usual in Odia bhakti, where Jagannatha too is addressed so. It is possible that 4.114 is Mahaprabhu speaking to Ramananda.
[^04-63]: In CC (Madhya 8) Ramananda sees Mahaprabhu as a dark cowherd boy covered by a golden radiance, and Mahaprabhu reveals to him the form of Rasaraja-Mahabhava — Krishna and Radha in one. According to the Gaudiya Vaishnava teaching (CC, Adi 1 and 4; Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati), Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself, who accepted Radha's bhava and radiance to taste the sweetness of His own love; Madhava's words "the avatara of Radha" express this idea in his own way. The second half-line ("the tasting of [Your] own nature is Yours") is rendered close to literally.
[^04-64]: The last word is printed ≈ «ଚତୋଇ» (*catoi*); read as a transposition for ଚେତାଇ (*cetāi*, "regaining consciousness / awakening") — unclear. The sense of the half-line is conjectural.
[^04-65]: The opening of Ramananda's Brajabuli song "pahilehi rāga nayana-bhaṅge bhela" ("at first attraction arose from the play of glances"), CC, Madhya 8; Madhava calls it a "śloka", and it is printed with distortions («ପହିଲଇ … ଭଙ୍ଗ ଭେଲେ»).
[^04-66]: Cf. CC, Madhya 8: on hearing this song Mahaprabhu covered Ramananda's mouth with His own hand. The words «ହେଉ ହେଲ କହ» are understood tentatively.
[^04-67]: The same unclear word «ସାଷ୍ଟାମ» (*sāṣṭāma*) as in 4.31 (pilot: "sāṣṭāma yataka karāile" of the unconscious Mahaprabhu). Here "in time he became *sāṣṭāma*" after ecstasy — which supports the sense "come to oneself, conscious" for 4.31 as well. Whether Prabhu or Ramananda comes to himself is not clear.
[^04-68]: Mahaprabhu speaks. Cf. CC, Madhya 8: at the end of the conversation Ramananda confesses that the Lord Himself spoke through his mouth, while Mahaprabhu, in humility, listens to him as a disciple. According to the Gaudiya Vaishnavas (Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati), Sri Chaitanya, Krishna Himself, plays the part of a student to glorify His devotee and to show the world that the truth about Krishna is learned from a Vaishnava. The change of speaker in 4.125–133 is unmarked; the assignment of lines follows the sense.
[^04-69]: By birth Ramananda was a karana (a scribe-kayastha), ranked with the shudras in the varna order; in CC (Madhya 8) he likewise calls himself low-born. The second word of the second half is printed ≈ ଘର (*ghara*, "house"); taken as "repository" — uncertain. A corruption (ଧର, "I hold to [the evidence of the shastras]") is possible. The sense of the half-line — "I only repeat the shastras" — follows from the context, not from this word.
[^04-70]: The readings ଗଣି (*gaṇi*, "counting") and ଭାଣ୍ଡି (*bhāṇḍi*, "deceiving, making fun") are uncertain: the second sign of the first word and the conjunct of the second are unclearly printed (possibly ଭାଙ୍ଗି).
[^04-71]: The last word is printed ≈ ରସାବର୍ତ୍ତ (*rasābartta*); taken as *rasa-āvarta*, "whirlpool of rasa". The tail of the final ligature also allows ‑ବର୍ତ୍ତି. The list in 4.131–132 (prema, rasa, hladini) repeats the topics of the conversation in 4.95–112.
[^04-72]: Cf. CC, Madhya 8.128: "Whether a brahmana, a sannyasi or a shudra — whoever knows the science of Krishna is a guru." In Madhava, Mahaprabhu calls Ramananda a twice-born (*dwija*) outright and his "shudra" status an error. The Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, teach that a true Vaishnava stands above all varnas and that brahminical status is determined by qualities and initiation, not by birth.
[^04-73]: Ten days — as in 4.80 and in CC (Madhya 8), where after ten days of conversation with Ramananda Mahaprabhu continues south. The last word of the first half is printed ≈ ରଲ (or ରଳ); read ରହିଲ (*rahila*, "stayed"), which the metre also requires (as printed the half-line has 8 syllables).
[^04-74]: Places of the southern pilgrimage — CC, Madhya 9: Sri Ranga-kshetra (where Mahaprabhu spent the four months of the rains), Setubandha-Rameshvara, Udupi (the seat of Madhvacharya) and others. Madhava's order differs from CC. "Shankara" is probably Shiva (Shiva shrines) or Shringeri, the seat of Shankaracharya, which CC (Madhya 9) mentions next to Udupi; what the author meant is unclear.
[^04-75]: According to CC (Madhya 9), Mahaprabhu returned from the south to Puri by the same road, through Vidyanagara on the Godavari; He was at Prayaga later, on the way to Vrindavana and back (Madhya 17–19). In Madhava, Prayaga stands between the southern shrines and Rajamandri — a divergence from CC (or some other place called "Prayaga"?). The first word of 4.136a is printed ≈ ପୀଠମାନନ୍ତ: "pithas" + an unclear ‑ନ୍ତ (*ananta*, "countless"?).
[^04-76]: Rajamandri (Rajamahendri, modern Rajahmundry) — Ramananda's province (see 3.132). In CC (Madhya 9), on the way back Mahaprabhu again meets Ramananda at Vidyanagara and tells him to leave his service and come to Puri; Ramananda arrives later (Madhya 10–11), as in Madhava (4.142–144). The ending ‑ର in ରାଜମନ୍ଦ୍ରୀର is taken as locative ("at Rajamandri"); the subscript ୍ର is unclearly printed.
[^04-77]: Printed ≈ ରୂ: read ତୁ (*tu*, "you") — in this edition the second form of ତ looks like ର; this gives 9 syllables and the usual imperative "do not you stay".
[^04-78]: ହେଉ ହେଲ (*heu hela*) — lit. "let it be, it has become"; here, by sense, assent: "very well, so be it". The same phrase occurs in 4.122, where Mahaprabhu covers Ramananda's mouth; there it probably means "enough".
[^04-79]: The word is unclearly printed (≈ େ + a sign like ନ + ଉ? + ଟି); read ଲେଉଟି (*leuṭi*, "having returned").
[^04-80]: Anka — the Odia count of regnal years, which skips anka 1 and the ankas ending in 0 and 6. The nineteenth anka of Prataparudra (who came to the throne ≈1497) is his fifteenth regnal year, i.e. ≈1511/12. According to CC (Madhya 9–11), Ramananda came to Puri soon after Mahaprabhu's return from the south.
[^04-81]: According to CC (Madhya 11), Prataparudra released Ramananda from governing his province while keeping his salary, so that he could be with Mahaprabhu; CC says nothing about entrusting him with temple affairs. Madhava, who served under Ramananda (ch. 3), reports that the king gave him charge of Jagannatha's temple. ମନ୍ଦିର is everywhere printed ≈ ମନ୍ଦର.
[^04-82]: ବେଭାର (*bebhāra* = *byabahāra*) here clearly means "affairs, management"; this supports the understanding of the same word in 4.96 and 4.104 ("usage, designation").
[^04-83]: Jivadeva — Ramananda's successor at Rajamahendri (his appointment and title follow in 4.149–151). The name is printed ≈ ଶ୍ରୀଜବଦେବ (without ୀ); below it is ଜୀବଦେବ. The last word of 4.148b is printed ≈ ରାଜମହେନ୍ଦଶ: the final sign looks like ଶ, read as «ରି» (*rājamahendari*), which gives the rhyme with ହକାରି.
[^04-84]: Continues 4.148, where the king sends Jivadeva to Rajamahendri (Rajamundry on the Godavari; cf. 3.132). We take 4.149 as the king's words (the gerunds *bujhi*, *rakhi* — "managing", "keeping"); it may also be narrative: "there he managed affairs and kept the army with him."
[^04-85]: Printed ≈ ବାହନପତି; we read ବାହିନୀପତି (*bāhinīpati*), "commander of the army", a well-known Odia military title (later also a family name). The metre (9 syllables) fits both readings.
[^04-86]: In 1.77 Jivadeva is called a "great warrior" (*mahārathā*) beside Rudradeva, and in 3.12 "the best of brahmanas"; here he is a military commander, rajaguru and poet. "Mighty" renders *sāmarathā* (= *samartha*), the same word as in 1.77.
[^04-87]: "Kavi-dindima" ("kettledrum of poets") is an honorific title for a poet. A Sanskrit poet of Prataparudra's court, Jivadeva Acharya, is known by this title; Madhava apparently means him (to be verified). The particle ‑ଟି (*aṭaïṭi, bolaïṭi*) is the same as in 4.98–109.
[^04-88]: *Bhagata-vaibhava* means "The Glory of the Devotees"; apparently the drama known as *Bhakti-vaibhava* (*Bhakti-vaibhava-nāṭaka*) of Jivadeva Acharya, to whom the poem *Bhakti-bhagavata* is also ascribed. The title *Utsahavati* is an uncertain reading (second sign ≈ ତ୍ସ/ସ୍ତ୍ର); the work is not identified. *Rupaka* is the general term for Sanskrit drama.
[^04-89]: The first half-line (lit. "[they] saw the plays, this became") is uncertain: probably Jivadeva's plays were staged and watched, after which he received *melāṇi* — leave to depart — and went to Rajamahendri.
[^04-90]: The Kshetra is Shri Kshetra, Puri. According to 4.144–147, in the nineteenth anka (≈1511/12) Ramananda returned to Nilachala and at the king's wish took over the management of the temple's affairs. The CC (Madhya 11) likewise tells that Ramananda, leaving his post in the south, settled in Puri to be with Mahaprabhu. The verb is printed ≈ ସମଜଇ (*samajaï*) and is not identified; the sense seems to be "looks after, cares for" (cf. ସମ୍ଭାଳଇ).
[^04-91]: Apparently a mandapa for gatherings (goshthi) of Vaishnavas by the northern side of Jagannatha's temple; the temple itself is not named in the couplet. ଉତ୍ତର ପାର୍ଶ୍ୱଣ ("of the northern side") is an uncertain reading (the second sign of the second word is indistinct; = *pārśweṇa*?). As far as we know, the CC does not mention such a mandapa.
[^04-92]: The closing couplet of the chapter with the author's name (cf. 3.151); sheet 75 opens with the heading "ପଞ୍ଚମ ଅଧ୍ୟାୟ" — Chapter 5. Printed ≈ "ଦନ ବହୁଲେ … ମଧବ": we read ଦିନ ବହିଲେ "the days flowed by" and ମାଧବ (lost ‑ି and ‑ା).

---

# Madhava Pattanayak. *Vaishnava-lilamrita*, Chapter 5
## Prataparudra at the Ratha-yatra: the Chhera-panhara and Gora's Kirtan


**5.1.** Of [the events of] the nineteenth anka hear, good people, attentively (??).[^05-1]  
**5.2.** Prataparudra Gajapati graciously comes to the Ratha-yatra.[^05-2]  
**5.3.** The story of the young ascetic (?) he hears from Ramaraya.[^05-3]  
**5.4.** "A young sannyasi has come," Budha Lenka related.[^05-4]  
**5.5.** The Tadhau, bringing the panji, reads: "The young ascetic has come.[^05-5]  
**5.6.** He is here, in this very place, having completed His journey through the south."[^05-6]  
**5.7.** The king said, "Very well (?)," and went to the chhera-panhara.[^05-7]  
**5.8.** Performing the chhera-panhara of the chariot, he saw Gora's kirtan.  
**5.9.** Seeing it, he was amazed — and met with Chaitanya (?).[^05-8]  
**5.10.** Ramaraya, who was in the company, is beside Prabhu.  
**5.11.** Kanhai, Jagannatha Dasa, Balarama and Yashovanta,[^05-9]  
**5.12.** Svarupa, Achyuta, Ananta, Gadadhara and Shivananda,  
**5.13.** Bhainsa, Raghu Arakshita — many are with Him.[^05-10]  

## Talk with the King, the Lodging at Kashi Mishra's; the Journeys to Ramakeli and Prayaga, Rupa's Arrival


**5.14.** The king addressed Gora, gladdened at seeing the kirtan.[^05-11]  
**5.15.** [The king] said, "This is a good practice: the name of Hari is the essence.[^05-12]  
**5.16.** Keep performing kirtan here; do nothing other than this."  
**5.17.** Chaitanya said, "O king, you are a servant of Jagannatha.  
**5.18.** What could be greater than this? Seeing [it], I am blessed."  
**5.19.** The king charged (?) Raya: "Look well after the ascetic's affairs."[^05-13]  
**5.20.** Summoning Kashi Mishra, [he said:] "House Chaitanya with care (?)."[^05-14]  
**5.21.** So the king commanded — and lodged (?) [Him] in Kashi Mishra's house.[^05-15]  
**5.22.** This place is near the temple, a fitting abode for the ascetic.  
**5.23.** To relish the bhava of Radha, He appeared (lit. "took birth") in the world of mortals.[^05-16]  
**5.24.** At His waist He ties a cord and ties knots (?) on the cord.[^05-17]  
**5.25.** Daily (?) He chants a hundred thousand names; from dawn He performs kirtan.[^05-18]  
**5.26.** In bliss He lives in the rasa of Krishna, in the mandapa on the northern side.[^05-19]  
**5.27.** Meanwhile — listen, people — after some days Chaitanya says:  
**5.28.** "I shall go from here to the north and, returning, come back to the Kshetra."[^05-20]  
**5.29.** Raya went with Him and, parting [from Him] at Cuttack, came back.[^05-21]  
**5.30.** By way of Cuttack and Remuna (?), Chaitanya went to Ramakeli,[^05-22]  
**5.31.** met Rupa and Sanatana, and made them give up royal service:[^05-23]  
**5.32.** "You two brothers are learned, worthy vessels for the rasa of Krishna."  
**5.33.** From there He turned back and arrived in the Kshetra.[^05-24]  
**5.34.** All were gladdened — Rama Raya and Kashi Mishra.  
**5.35.** Some days after this, He made ready to go to Prayaga.  
**5.36.** He went by the forest road and, going on, reached Prayaga.[^05-25]  
**5.37.** In that place He met Rupa and stayed [there] ten days.  
**5.38.** He said to Rupa, "You come — appear in Nilachala."[^05-26]  
**5.39.** Chaitanya turned back and arrived in Nilachala.  
**5.40.** Rupa came after [some] days, by the road through Remuna and Bhadrak.[^05-27]  
**5.41.** In Nilachala Rupa lives; his life in Krishna (?) … (??).[^05-28]  
**5.42.** His love is for the shastras; he is full of affection for sadhus and Vaishnavas.  

## Rupa with Ramananda and Svami; Kabir in Puri; Sanatana's Arrival


**5.43.** Chaitanya brought [Rupa] to Raya and said, introducing him: "This is Rupa.[^05-29]  
**5.44.** Teach him tattva, show him the path (?) of rasa."  
**5.45.** Raya conversed with Rupa and taught him the truths of rasa:  
**5.46.** shanta, sakhya and vatsalya, dasya, madhura, the mingling of bhavas (?),[^05-30]  
**5.47.** mottayita (?), kilakinchita and all the other truths, as many as there are.[^05-31]  
**5.48.** Deep is this supreme truth — in the world it is hidden.  
**5.49.** Raya is a knower (?) of the truths of rasa, and Rupa grasped (?) all of it.  
**5.50.** Having known the inner (?), supreme (?) bhava, he became fixed (?) in a humble disposition (?).[^05-32]  
**5.51.** For ten months Rupa stayed [in Nilachala] and heard the *Bhagavata* from Svami.[^05-33]  
**5.52.** All Svami's bhavas — trembling, hair standing on end — [were] natural [to him].  
**5.53.** He reads the verses of the *Bhagavata* — tears stream from his eyes.  
**5.54.** Having learned from Svami the truth of bhakti, [Rupa] was supremely glad.  
**5.55.** He had all the manuscripts copied — the *Brahma-samhita* and the *Karnamrita*.[^05-34]  
**5.56.** But Kashi Mishra could not bear it and said: "Send Rupa away!"[^05-35]  
**5.57.** "In everything his conduct is that of a mleccha: he does not go for darshan of Jagannatha.  
**5.58.** He is a minister of a mleccha king, yet [he] is brought (?) here, called a devotee."[^05-36]  
**5.59.** Chaitanya says: "Rupa, go; do not stay here any longer (?).  
**5.60.** Build a holy seat in Vrindavana; write books, firm of mind."[^05-37]  
**5.61.** Rupa set out for Vrindavana — he did not linger here even for a moment.  
**5.62.** In the king's twenty-fourth anka a sannyasi comes from the north.[^05-38]  
**5.63.** His name is Jholaa Kabir; on his body is saffron cloth.[^05-39]  
**5.64.** Holding a tumbura in his hand, he sings songs in a sweet voice.[^05-40]  
**5.65.** His love is for Jagannatha; his mind is great (?) and generous.  
**5.66.** Svami, hearing [his] bhajan, embraced him with love.[^05-41]  
**5.67.** He said: "All human beings are equal in this (??) [world].[^05-42]  
**5.68.** Be it a chandala or a twice-born — know: in creation [all] are equal.[^05-43]  
**5.69.** If the mind abides in the Name alone, Krishna is attained with ease.  
**5.70.** The desire for liberation is like hell; service to one's chosen Deity — that is the root."[^05-44]  
**5.71.** Kabir found satisfaction and gave his mind to Jagannatha.  
**5.72.** After a month he departed — in the king's twenty-fourth anka.  
**5.73.** From the north comes (?) Sanatana — know that he is very dear to Prabhu.[^05-45]  

## Sanatana under the Chariot; the Instruction of Sanatana


**5.74.** He had been minister to the mleccha king, and in time he became a Vaishnava.[^05-46]  
**5.75.** On his body were evil sores; he came (?) at the time of Gundicha (?).[^05-47]  
**5.76.** He threw himself under the chariot, saying that he would give up his life.[^05-48]  
**5.77.** Svarupa, Tulasi, Achyuta, Ananta, Kanhai and Yashovanta[^05-49]  
**5.78.** lifted him, raised him up (?) and held him, saying: "What a deed you are doing!"  
**5.79.** In the din (?) of the kirtana no one learned of this matter.[^05-50]  
**5.80.** Prabhu came to know of it and reproached (?) [him]: "What is this? Is this conduct of yours good?[^05-51]  
**5.81.** Where is (?) a pandit more learned than you? — and you would take (??) the life from your body!"[^05-52]  
**5.82.** He stayed [in Nilachala] for a year and understood many truths.[^05-53]  
**5.83.** From Svami he learned the immeasurable truths of devotion:[^05-54]  
**5.84.** the truth of Krishna and the truth of creation, the truth of the jiva and the truth of lila.  
**5.85.** The jiva and Brahman are not one: the jiva, he says, is Krishna's servant.[^05-55]  
**5.86.** He expounds the truth of difference-and-non-difference (bheda-abheda), and Sanatana listens in silence.[^05-56]  
**5.87.** Speaking of Krishna's energies, he names chit-shakti and jiva-shakti,  
**5.88.** and there is one more — maya-shakti. Svami explained much.[^05-57]  
**5.89.** The lila-avataras, the guna-avataras, the yuga-avataras and their workings (?);[^05-58]  
**5.90.** Krishna's aishvarya is one bhava (?), but the bhava of madhurya is [His] own nature.[^05-59]  
**5.91.** Svami expounded bhajan in the madhura-bhava.  
**5.92.** Madhura is the sadhana, and he calls (?) it the best as well.  
**5.93.** In the highest way Krishna manifests Himself in His humanlike lila — it alone is full of rasa.[^05-60]  
**5.94.** "Recite the kama-gayatri mantra, making your mind firm in Krishna.[^05-61]  
**5.95.** Abandoning (?) the vaidhi (?) path, perform bhajan by the path of raga.[^05-62]  
**5.96.** A Vaishnava does not desire (??) liberation — he longs for service to Krishna.[^05-63]  
**5.97.** Thus Krishna is attained, thus the doubts of the mind are dispelled."  
**5.98.** Prabhu said: "Go from here and establish a holy seat in Vrindavana."[^05-64]  
**5.99.** And Sanatana set out after a year had passed.  

## Vallabha in Nilachala; Svami Receives the Name "Atibada"; Balarama Dasa


**5.100.** From the north came Vallabha; his devotional feeling is for Gopala.[^05-65]  
**5.101.** Together (?) with Svami he has a meeting: they read the *Bhagavata*.[^05-66]  
**5.102.** His mind is on Sri Jagannatha: at darshan he finds joy.  
**5.103.** "This [Jagannatha] is Gopijana-vallabha Himself, Who has taken the form of Radha-Krishna" —[^05-67]  
**5.104.** [so] the sannyasi Vallabha understood, and the devotee made [Him his] offering, saying:  
**5.105.** "My faith is this: know that Jagannatha is Gopijana-vallabha."  
**5.106.** Having come for the Ratha [festival], Vallabha Gosai stayed [in Nilachala] two months.[^05-68]  
**5.107.** Seeing Prabhu's kirtana, he gains boundless joy.  
**5.108.** He came to the ishta-goshthi and grasped (?) many truths.[^05-69]  
**5.109.** Meanwhile — listen with relish — in the king's twenty-seventh anka,[^05-70]  
**5.110.** one day, sitting in seclusion, Prabhu, beside (?) Svami, [said]:  
**5.111.** "Tell Me the account of [our former] embodiment (?): from you [I] differ only in body."[^05-71]  
**5.112.** Svami said: "Very well." Prabhu says: "Tell it fully."  
**5.113.** Svami relates in private: "Radha was beside (?) [Krishna].  
**5.114.** Radha is not different from Krishna — She abides in wholehearted bhava.[^05-72]  
**5.115.** That form is sac-cid-ananda: Radha's svarupa [and His] are one body.  
**5.116.** Looking at Radha, [Krishna] laughed — the nectar of Krishna's laughter flowed.  
**5.117.** From Krishna's laughter is Your birth; from Radha's laughter is my birth."[^05-73]  
**5.118.** [Thus] he told of the former form, and this is not otherwise (?) [than it was].  
**5.119.** Prabhu embraced him with love (prema) and said: "This one is truly very great (ati-bada)!"[^05-74]  
**5.120.** "Yours and Mine is one life; only the body is different.  
**5.121.** You tell this very great story — from today you have become Atibada."  
**5.122.** He took the uttariya (?) from His shoulder and tied it on Svami's head.[^05-75]  
**5.123.** He says: "This is true greatness! Svami, you are My very life."  
**5.124.** Prabhu told Svarupa to call him Atibada.[^05-76]  
**5.125.** And before the gathering (?) of all the devotees Prabhu proclaims [him] Atibada.  
**5.126.** Kashi Mishra and Rama Raya, Balarama, Achyuta, surrounding (??) [Him],[^05-77]  
**5.127.** together (?) with Ananta and Yashovanta — [before them] Prabhu proclaimed this news.  
**5.128.** All accepted this usage (?) — "Atibada."  
**5.129.** He told a very great story — from today he became Atibada.  
**5.130.** Meanwhile, another tale full of rasa: hearing it, good people, rejoice.  
**5.131.** [He who] is called (?) Balarama Dasa — he is a mahapurusha.[^05-78]  
**5.132.** He is the son of Somanatha; he reveals the truth of Vedanta.[^05-79]  

## Balarama Dasa and the Sand Chariot; End of Chapter 5


**5.133.** He composed the song of the *Ramayana*; he is called a yoga-purusha (?).[^05-80]  
**5.134.** [Once] he climbed onto the chariot; in his hand there was (?) a flower garland:  
**5.135.** "On the chariot I shall give [it] to Vamana; I shall offer [Him my] devotional feeling" —[^05-81]  
**5.136.** [saying] so, he climbed onto the chariot. The servants say: "Get away from here!"[^05-82]  
**5.137.** "You have no right to offer a garland to [the Lord of] the chariot (?)."  
**5.138.** The servants reviled [him] much and said: "Get away from this place, you!"  
**5.139.** He came down from the chariot and said nothing to anyone.  
**5.140.** He went to the seashore to worship Jagannatha.  
**5.141.** Out of sand he made a chariot and called: "[O] our (?) son of Nanda (?)!"[^05-83]  
**5.142.** "Come, O brother Balarama, bringing sister Subhadra!"  
**5.143.** The three Deities took Their seat [on it] and accepted worship on the sand chariot.[^05-84]  
**5.144.** On the Danda the chariot did not move; the crowd of people stood stunned (?).[^05-85]  
**5.145.** The devotees stood looking on; however much the servants strove (?),  
**5.146.** [however much] the king, having come, raged (?) — still the chariot does not move.[^05-86]  
**5.147.** The day passed, night came — and yet the chariot did not move.  
**5.148.** A hundred thousand devotees gathered — no one could move [it] (??).[^05-87]  
**5.149.** At night the king had a dream: "I am on the seashore," [the Lord] said.[^05-88]  
**5.150.** "Go, king, and bring Me together with the devotee Balarama."  
**5.151.** The king planned a procession (?): Prabhu and Svami — [in his] retinue (?);[^05-89]  
**5.152.** Kashi Mishra, Tapana Acharya (?), Kanhai, Ananta Acharya (?),[^05-90]  
**5.153.** Achyuta, Ananta, Yashovanta, Tulasi Mishra, Damodara Pandita.[^05-91]  
**5.154.** With the din of cymbals and mridangas the seashore seemed to brim over.  
**5.155.** On the sand all (?) assembled and offered dandavat prostrations.  
**5.156.** In the festivity (?) — the bhava and longing (?) [of all]; the joy of the devotees … (??).[^05-92]  
**5.157.** They glorified Balarama, worshipped [him] with flowers and sandal,  
**5.158.** and said: "Come, Balarama; your name is 'best of devotees'.  
**5.159.** With devotion and bhava you have bound [Him]; you have brought Prabhu under your control.[^05-93]  
**5.160.** Where is there a devotee [like] you? Come quickly — we are swooning (??)."[^05-94]  
**5.161.** Balarama Dasa came and appeared upon the chariot.  
**5.162.** He offered the garland to Prabhu and covered [His] body with sandal.  
**5.163.** The din of bells and mridangas — the Bada-danda then seemed to brim over.  
**5.164.** The chariot rolled (??) rumbling (?), says Madhava, falling at the feet.[^05-95]  

[^05-1]: Prataparudra's nineteenth anka is, by the usual count (anka 1 and ankas ending in 0 and 6 are skipped), the 15th regnal year, ≈1511/12; the Ratha-yatra of this anka falls ≈ summer 1512. According to the CC (Madhya 9–11), Mahaprabhu returned to Puri from the south shortly before the Ratha-yatra, to which the Bengali devotees came. Printed ≈ ବଷୟେ — read ବିଷୟେ ("concerning, of"); the last word of the second half-line (≈ ଅୟେ) is not identified, and "attentively" follows the sense.
[^05-2]: Gajapati ("lord of elephants") is the title of the Odia kings. *Bije* is the honorific verb for the movement of the king or the Deity.
[^05-3]: Printed ≈ ସତ ("a true [story]"); we read ଯତି, "ascetic, sannyasi", as in 5.5 (ସ and ଯ are almost indistinguishable in this type). The king learns of Mahaprabhu from Ramananda Raya — cf. CC, Madhya 11, where Ramananda, on his return, tells the king about the Lord.
[^05-4]: Budha Lenka — "old Lenka"; *lenka* is an Odia designation of a servant or attendant. The person is not identified.
[^05-5]: The Tadhau is apparently a temple scribe (karana) who keeps the records (*pañji*; cf. the *Madala-panji*, the chronicle of Jagannatha's temple). Where his words end is not marked; we take 5.5b–5.6 as what he reads out.
[^05-6]: Mahaprabhu's southern pilgrimage (≈1510–1512) — CC, Madhya 7–9; in Madhava, Chapter 4 (the meeting with Ramananda on the Godavari, 4.81–124).
[^05-7]: The chhera-panhara is the king's sweeping of the chariots with a golden broom (cf. 2.121). According to the CC (Madhya 13), Mahaprabhu was pleased by this humble service of Prataparudra. "ହେଉ ହେଲ" (cf. 4.122) is taken as "very well, all right" — uncertain.
[^05-8]: CC (Madhya 13–14): during the procession the king watched Mahaprabhu's kirtan and was amazed; the meeting itself took place later, in the garden, when the chariot halted: Prataparudra, in the plain dress of a Vaishnava, recited verses of the *Gopi-gita*, and Mahaprabhu embraced him. Madhava compresses the account into one line. The word before "met" is printed ≈ ରୂଲେ/ତୁଲେ and is taken as "together with" (?).
[^05-9]: Prabhu's companions in the kirtan according to Madhava: Kanhai (cf. 2.25); the Panchasakha — Jagannatha Dasa, Balarama Dasa, Yashovanta, Achyutananda, Ananta (cf. 1.76); and Svarupa Damodara, Gadadhara Pandita and Shivananda (Sena?). In the CC (Madhya 13) the Ratha-yatra is led by seven kirtan groups of Bengali devotees (Svarupa, Shrivasa, Mukunda, Advaita, Nityananda and others); the participation of the Odia Panchasakha is a tradition of the Odia school, not attested in the CC/CB.
[^05-10]: Bhainsa and Raghu Arakshita are not identified.
[^05-11]: Gora (Gaura) is Mahaprabhu, as in 5.8. According to the CC (Madhya 12–14), Mahaprabhu as a sannyasi avoided meeting the king; Prataparudra's humble service at the chhera-panhara pleased Him, but the meeting took place later, in the garden, when the king came in the plain dress of a Vaishnava. In Madhava the king and Mahaprabhu converse at once, at the Ratha-yatra.
[^05-12]: The change of speaker is not marked. We take 5.15–16 as the king's words (the honorific verb *boile*; Chaitanya's reply begins in 5.17). "ଏ ଭଲ ବେଭାର" (*e bhala bebhāra*) — "this is a good practice [conduct]" (*bebhāra* = *byabahāra*); it could also be understood as an inquiry after His welfare.
[^05-13]: Raya is Ramananda Raya. The verb ବରଗିଲେ (*baragile*) is the same as the unidentified ବରଗିଲ in 3.105; there and here a superior gives an instruction, so we assume the sense "charged, enjoined" (?). "Ascetic" — printed ≈ ଯତର, read ଯତିର (‑ି lost; cf. ଯତ = *yati* in 5.5); likewise in 5.22.
[^05-14]: Kashi Mishra was a temple priest and the king's preceptor; in his house near the temple (later called the "Gambhira") Mahaprabhu lived in Puri. According to the CC (Madhya 10), the lodging at Kashi Mishra's was arranged, at the king's wish, by Sarvabhauma immediately after the Lord's return from the south, before the Ratha-yatra; in Madhava the king entrusts it to Ramananda and to Kashi Mishra himself after the Ratha-yatra. The form ମିଶ୍ରକଇ (*miśrakaï*) is taken as a dative-accusative (= ‑କୁ?). "With care" — printed ≈ ଯ + ନ with a subscript sign, read ଯତ୍ନ (?).
[^05-15]: The last word is printed ≈ ର + a small sign like "େ" + ା + ଇଲେ; we read ରଖାଇଲେ, "lodged, had [Him] housed" (?), from the rhyme and from 5.20 (*rakha*).
[^05-16]: This agrees with the Gaudiya Vaishnava understanding of the confidential reason for Mahaprabhu's descent — to relish Shri Radha's love for Krishna (CC, Adi 4). The link with the preceding verse is not marked: 5.23–26 describe Mahaprabhu in Puri.
[^05-17]: Understood as counting the names chanted by knots on a cord. The word after "knots" is printed ≈ ଆଲେ (or ଥାଲେ) and is not identified (ଗଣ୍ଠିଆଳ, "knotted"?). "Cord" — printed ≈ ସ[ୂ]ତ୍ରକୁ, "on the cord" (?).
[^05-18]: The first sign is blotted with ink; we read ନିତ୍ୟ, "daily", from the sense (?). A hundred thousand names is a "lakh" (*lakṣa*).
[^05-19]: Cf. 4.158: Ramananda built a mandapa on the northern side, where the Vaishnavas gathered (there the unclear ପାର୍ଶ୍ୱଣ (?); here ପାରୁଶ, *pāruśa* = *pārśva*, "side"). Exactly where this mandapa stood (on the northern side of the temple?) is unclear.
[^05-20]: The journey north is the attempt to go to Vrindavan through Bengal (CC, Madhya 16; ≈1514 by the Gaudiya Vaishnava chronology). Madhava reduces the account to a few lines.
[^05-21]: According to the CC (Madhya 16), Ramananda accompanied the Lord further — to the border of Odisha. Printed ≈ ଛଡ଼ଣ, read ଛଡ଼ିଣ, "having parted" (or ଛାଡ଼ିଣ, "having left [Him]").
[^05-22]: Remuna (near Balasore) is the place of the Deity Kshira-chora Gopinatha (CC, Madhya 4). The syntax of ରେମୁଣା କଟକେଣ ହୋଇ (*remuṇā kaṭakeṇa hoi*) is unclear: "via Remuna [and] Cuttack" or "from Cuttack via Remuna"; the same phrase occurs elsewhere in the poem.
[^05-23]: Ramakeli is a village near Gauda where Mahaprabhu met the brothers Rupa and Sanatana, ministers of Sultan Husain Shah (CC, Madhya 1 and 16). According to the CC (Madhya 19), they gave up their service later, after this meeting; Madhava compresses this into one line.
[^05-24]: According to the CC (Madhya 16), Mahaprabhu turned back from Kanai-natashala and returned to Puri.
[^05-25]: According to the CC (Madhya 17–19), Mahaprabhu went through the Jharikhanda forest to Vrindavan (≈1515) and on the way back met Rupa at Prayaga and instructed him for ten days (Madhya 19). Madhava mentions neither Vrindavan nor the instruction of Sanatana in Varanasi (Madhya 20–25).
[^05-26]: According to the CC (Madhya 19), Mahaprabhu sent Rupa to Vrindavan and afterwards allowed him to come to Him in Nilachala by way of Gauda; Rupa's arrival in Puri is in CC, Antya 1. *Parakāśa* (= *prakāśa*) is taken as the imperative "appear".
[^05-27]: Remuna and Bhadrak lie on the road from Bengal to Puri. According to the CC (Antya 1), in Puri Rupa stayed with Haridasa Thakura for about ten months and read his plays *Vidagdha-madhava* and *Lalita-madhava* to Mahaprabhu, Ramananda and Svarupa.
[^05-28]: The last word is printed ≈ a sign like ଡ଼ or ଙ with a dot + ଟ + କା + େ + ସ with a subscript sign (*ṭekāste*?) and is not identified. *Kṛṣṇabartti* is taken as "a life [devoted] to Krishna" (*kṛṣṇa-bṛtti*?) or "talk of Krishna" (*kṛṣṇa-bārttā*?).
[^05-29]: Raya is Ramananda Raya (in Madhava also "Ramaraya"). The account of Rupa's coming to Puri begins on sheet 77 (5.38–42). According to the CC (Antya 1), in Puri Rupa's dramas were examined in the presence of Ramananda Raya and Sarvabhauma, and Ramananda glorified his knowledge of rasa; the instruction in rasa-tattva itself Rupa had received earlier from Mahaprabhu at Prayaga (CC, Madhya 19). In Madhava, Chaitanya entrusts Rupa's teaching to Raya himself. In 5.43b ପ୍ରକାଶେ is taken as "openly, by way of introduction" — uncertain.
[^05-30]: The five principal rasas (relationships with the Lord) — shanta, dasya, sakhya, vatsalya, madhurya. *Bhāva-śābalya*, "the mingling of bhavas", is the simultaneous combination of several emotions, described in Rupa Gosvami's *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu*. The first sign of the word is printed unclearly; the reading ଶାବଲ୍ୟ is suggested by the rhyme with ବାତ୍ସଲ୍ୟ.
[^05-31]: Mottayita (the display of hidden love at the mention of the beloved) and kilakinchita (a mixture of emotions — laughter, tears, anger and others) are "ornaments" (alankaras) of Sri Radha, later described by Rupa Gosvami himself in the *Ujjvala-nilamani*. The first word is printed unclearly (≈ ମୋ + ଟ୍ଟା/ଷ୍ଠା + ୟି + ତ); in the second the ‑i signs are lost.
[^05-32]: The couplet is understood tentatively: three words are read with uncertainty (ଭିତର? ଶ୍ରେଷ୍ଠ? ବୃତ୍ତି?), and ପରମାଣି is taken as "taking as the standard" (from *pramāṇa*). The humility of Rupa and Sanatana is glorified many times in the CC (Antya 1, 4).
[^05-33]: Svami is Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 2.6), author of the Odia *Bhagavata*. Rupa's study of the *Bhagavata* under Jagannatha Dasa is a tradition of the Odia school; it is not mentioned in the CC/CB. According to the CC (Antya 1), in Puri Rupa lived together with Haridasa Thakura and stayed until the Dola-yatra; for Gaudiya Vaishnavas Rupa Gosvami is the acharya of rasa-tattva, who received it directly from Mahaprabhu.
[^05-34]: The *Brahma-samhita* (its fifth chapter) Mahaprabhu found in the temple of Adi-Keshava, and Bilvamangala's *Krishna-karnamrita* on the bank of the Krishna-vena river, during His southern pilgrimage; He brought both books to Puri, where the devotees copied them (CC, Madhya 9, 11). Printed ସହିତା (without anusvara) and ≈ କର୍ଣ୍ଣମୃତେ.
[^05-35]: Kashi Mishra was Mahaprabhu's host in Puri (the Gambhira is in his house). The CC has no such displeasure on Kashi Mishra's part: according to the CC (Antya 1, 4), Rupa and Sanatana themselves, out of humility, considering themselves defiled by their service at the Muslim court, did not approach the temple of Jagannatha, and Mahaprabhu visited them daily at Haridasa's place and praised their humility. The accusation of Rupa (5.56–58) is an account of the Odia tradition.
[^05-36]: The mleccha king is Husain Shah, sultan of Bengal; Rupa (Dabir Khas) and Sanatana (Sakara Mallik) were his ministers (CC, Madhya 1, 19). Who "brings" Rupa (3rd person ଆଣଇ) is not stated.
[^05-37]: According to the CC (Antya 1), after the Dola-yatra Mahaprabhu sent Rupa to Vrindavana, charging him to write books on rasa and to restore the lost holy places; from Puri Rupa went first to Bengal and from there to Vrindavana. In the edition the numbers of 5.60 and 5.61 are printed transposed (।୬୧।, ।୬୦।); here they follow the order of the lines.
[^05-38]: Prataparudra's twenty-fourth anka is, by the usual count (anka 1 and ankas ending in 0 and 6 are skipped), the 19th regnal year, ≈1515/16 (accession ≈1497).
[^05-39]: Kabir — the poet-saint of Varanasi (15th – early 16th c.), a weaver by birth. *Jholaā* is apparently an Odia rendering of *julāhā*, "weaver". Madhava calls him a sannyasi in saffron cloth. Kabir's coming to Puri and his meeting with Jagannatha Dasa are a tradition of the Odia school (the "Kabir-chaura" math is known in Puri); Kabir is not mentioned in the CC/CB.
[^05-40]: The tumbura is a stringed drone instrument (cf. the tanpura).
[^05-41]: Svami is Jagannatha Dasa. Who speaks in 5.67–70 is not marked; by the sense it is Svami instructing Kabir (in 5.71 Kabir "found satisfaction").
[^05-42]: The last word (printed ≈ ଶସ୍ତଳ) is the same unclear word as in 2.13 and 2.37, where by the sense it means "on earth, in the world".
[^05-43]: Cf. the CC (Antya 4): for the service of Krishna low birth is no obstacle and high birth no qualification; according to the *Srimad-Bhagavatam* (3.33.7), even a dog-eater who chants the holy name is worthy of honour.
[^05-44]: Cf. *Srimad-Bhagavatam* 3.29.13: pure devotees do not accept any kind of liberation if it lacks service to the Lord. Gaudiya Vaishnavas likewise regard the striving for impersonal liberation as an obstacle to bhakti.
[^05-45]: Sanatana Gosvami, Rupa's elder brother. According to the CC (Antya 4), he came to Puri from Vrindavana through the Jharikhanda forest when Rupa had already left; Mahaprabhu lodged him with Haridasa. The account continues on sheet 80.
[^05-46]: Sanatana (Gosvami) — before his renunciation Sakara Mallik, minister of Husain Shah, the sultan of Bengal (the "mleccha king", cf. 5.58). According to the CC (Madhya 19–20), he left state service, was imprisoned by the sultan, escaped and met Mahaprabhu in Varanasi, where he received His detailed instruction (Madhya 20–24); from there Mahaprabhu sent him to Vrindavana. Madhava says it briefly: "in time he became a Vaishnava".
[^05-47]: Gundicha — the chariot festival (Ratha-yatra, "Gundicha-yatra"), when the Deities ride in chariots to the Gundicha temple. The reading of the couplet is uncertain: the first word is printed ≈ ଆସତ (taken as ଆସନ୍ତ "having come"), and the last sign of the second is unclear. According to the CC (Antya 4), on the way to Puri through the Jharikhanda forest Sanatana's whole body broke out in itching sores from bad water and fasting, and he resolved to give up his body under the wheel of Jagannatha's chariot during Ratha-yatra.
[^05-48]: In the CC (Antya 4) Sanatana only intended this; Mahaprabhu, knowing his intention, told him that giving up the body is an act in the mode of ignorance and that Krishna is not attained that way, but only by bhajan; moreover, Sanatana's body had already been surrendered to Mahaprabhu and belonged to Him. In Madhava, Sanatana actually throws himself under the chariot and the companions lift him up (5.77–78) — an account of the Odia tradition.
[^05-49]: Svarupa — Svarupa Damodara. Tulasi — possibly Tulasi the parichha (superintendent of the Jagannatha temple) mentioned in the CC. Achyuta (Achyutananda), Ananta and Yashovanta belong to the Panchasakha; Kanhai — cf. 2.25 (possibly Kanai Khuntia).
[^05-50]: *Gaha gaha* is apparently onomatopoeic (a hum, the thick noise of a crowd); reading and sense are uncertain.
[^05-51]: Cf. the CC (Antya 4): Mahaprabhu reproaches Sanatana — if Krishna could be obtained by giving up the body, He Himself would give up millions of bodies; Krishna is not attained by giving up the body. The first unclear word (printed ≈ ତରକଲ) is taken as *tarakila* "began to reproach" (or *tarajila* "threatened"?); "what is this" — *kisa* (printed ≈ କସ).
[^05-52]: The last words of both half-lines are read uncertainly (printed ≈ କାହିଁଛ and ≈ ମୂଚୁଛ); the translation is conjectural. In the CC Sanatana is glorified as a great knower of the scriptures.
[^05-53]: According to the CC (Antya 4), Sanatana lived with Mahaprabhu in Puri for about a year — from Ratha-yatra to the Dola-yatra of the next spring; then Mahaprabhu let him go to Vrindavana (cf. 5.99).
[^05-54]: Svami — Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 2.6, 5.51). According to the CC (Madhya 20–24), all this teaching — on Krishna, on the jiva as Krishna's eternal servant, on the three energies, on the avataras, on sadhana-bhakti (vaidhi and raganuga) and on prema — was given to Sanatana by Mahaprabhu Himself, in Varanasi, over two months. Madhava ascribes the instruction of Sanatana in Puri to Jagannatha Dasa — a tradition of Odisha; for Gaudiya Vaishnavas Sanatana Gosvami is a disciple of Mahaprabhu Himself. The speaker in 5.85–97 is by sense Svami (named outright in 5.88 and 5.91).
[^05-55]: Cf. CC, Madhya 20.108: the constitutional nature of the jiva is to be Krishna's eternal servant. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, as here, reject the identification of the jiva with Brahman.
[^05-56]: Gaudiya Vaishnavas call this teaching *achintya-bheda-abheda* — "inconceivable oneness and difference"; it was proclaimed by Mahaprabhu Himself (CC, Adi 7; Madhya 20) and systematically set out by Srila Jiva Gosvami. In Madhava, bheda-abheda is expounded by Jagannatha Dasa.
[^05-57]: Cf. CC, Madhya 20 (and *Vishnu Purana* 6.7.61): Krishna has three energies — internal (chit), marginal (jiva) and external (maya). Couplets 5.87–88 form one sentence.
[^05-58]: Mahaprabhu instructs Sanatana on the lila-, guna-, yuga- and other avataras in CC, Madhya 20. The last word (*bebhāra* "conduct, usage", as in 5.80b) is not fully clear here.
[^05-59]: Cf. CC, Madhya 21: Krishna's sweetness (madhurya) is the essence of His nature, and His majesty (aishvarya) is subordinate to it. The last word of the first half-line is printed incompletely (≈ ଭା…) and restored by rhyme as ଭାବ.
[^05-60]: Cf. CC, Madhya 21.101: of all Krishna's pastimes the highest is His humanlike lila (*nara-lila*).
[^05-61]: The kama-gayatri is the mantra for worshipping Krishna as the transcendental Cupid (Madana) of Vrindavana (cf. CC, Madhya 8); Gaudiya Vaishnavas receive it from the guru at initiation. Verses 5.94–97 are an instruction in the imperative; by sense the speaker is Svami.
[^05-62]: Vaidhi-bhakti (following the injunctions of scripture) and raganuga-bhakti (following the spontaneous love of the residents of Vraja) are described in CC, Madhya 22, and in Rupa Gosvami's *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu*. Gaudiya Vaishnavas teach that raganuga awakens from genuine eagerness (*lobha*) and also follows the scriptures, rather than simply setting vaidhi-bhakti aside. Both unclear words (printed ≈ ବଇଧ…, ≈ ଛଡ଼ିଣ) are understood by the sense.
[^05-63]: Cf. 5.70 and the note to it. The verb of the first half-line is not identified (printed ≈ ବୁହିଁ); the sense suggests ଚାହିଁ "wants".
[^05-64]: According to the CC (Antya 4), Mahaprabhu let Sanatana go to Vrindavana after the Dola-yatra, charging him to write books on bhakti and to restore the lost holy places. "Pitha" — a holy seat; cf. 5.60 (the same charge to Rupa).
[^05-65]: Vallabha is Vallabha Bhatta (Vallabhacharya, 1479–1531), founder of the Pushti-marga, a worshipper of Bala-Gopala (the child Krishna). According to the CC (Madhya 19), Mahaprabhu met him at Adaila near Prayaga — that is, north of Puri; later Vallabha Bhatta came to Puri himself, Mahaprabhu humbled his pride in his commentary on the *Bhagavata*, and afterwards, with His permission, Vallabha received the Kishora-Gopala mantra from Gadadhara Pandita (CC, Antya 7). Vallabha's meetings with Jagannatha Dasa belong to the Odia tradition; they are not in the CC/CB. Printed ≈ ଅଇଲ ("came"), ‑ā lost.
[^05-66]: Svami is Jagannatha Dasa, author of the Odia *Bhagavata* (cf. 5.51). The word ତୁଳେଣ ("together with"?) is read and understood tentatively.
[^05-67]: Gopijana-vallabha, "the beloved of the gopis," is the youthful Krishna, Kishora-Gopala. According to Madhava, Vallabha, who had worshipped Gopala, recognises in Jagannatha Gopijana-vallabha Himself, "Who has taken the form of Radha-Krishna" (an Odia conception of Jagannatha). Cf. CC, Antya 7: Vallabha Bhatta moves from the service of Bala-Gopala to that of Kishora-Gopala. According to his followers' tradition, Vallabha became a sannyasi only at the end of his life; what the devotee "offered" in 5.104 is not stated.
[^05-68]: Ratha-yatra is the chariot festival of Jagannatha in Puri (month of Ashadha); Mahaprabhu's kirtana and dancing before the chariot are described in CC, Madhya 13. According to the CC (Antya 7), Vallabha Bhatta spent some time in Puri and saw Mahaprabhu's kirtana.
[^05-69]: Ishta-goshthi is a gathering of devotees for discourse on the chosen Deity and on spiritual truths; the expression also occurs in the CC. The last word (printed ≈ ସମଜଲେ) is taken as "grasped, understood" (cf. ସମଝିବା) — tentatively.
[^05-70]: The twenty-seventh anka of Prataparudra is, by the usual count (anka 1 and ankas ending in 0 and 6 are skipped), the 21st regnal year, ≈1517/18 (accession ≈1497).
[^05-71]: The first word (printed ≈ ପିଣ୍ଡା + ଙ୍ଗ) is read as *piṇḍāṅga*, "body, embodiment"; cf. "former form" (*pūrba aṅga*) in 5.118. The idea that Mahaprabhu and Jagannatha Dasa differ "only in body" (5.111, 5.120) belongs to the Odia tradition; it is not in the CC/CB.
[^05-72]: Cf. CC (Adi 4): Radha and Krishna are one svarupa, yet for the taste of lila they eternally exist in two forms; Radha is Krishna's hladini-shakti, not different from Him.
[^05-73]: The account of Mahaprabhu's birth from Krishna's laughter and Jagannatha Dasa's from Radha's laughter belongs to the Odia Atibadi tradition. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, recognise Sri Chaitanya as Krishna Himself, Who accepted the mood and golden complexion of Radha (CC, Adi 1, 4); Jagannatha Dasa's claim to origin from Radha does not occur in the CC/CB and is not accepted by Gaudiya Vaishnavas.
[^05-74]: "Atibada" (Odia *ati baṛa*, "very great") is the epithet of Jagannatha Dasa; according to Odia tradition it was given by Mahaprabhu Himself, hence the name of his followers, the Atibadis. This episode is not mentioned in the CC/CB. The list of apasampradayas (deviant groups) given by the Gaudiya acharyas includes the "ativadi"; Gaudiya Vaishnavas do not accept this line's teaching of Jagannatha Dasa's identity with Radha.
[^05-75]: The uttariya is the upper cloth worn over the shoulders. To tie on someone's head a cloth from one's own shoulder is a mark of the highest honour (cf. the siropa, a turban of honour). The reading ଉତ୍ତରୀ is uncertain (the signs are blurred).
[^05-76]: Svarupa Damodara, Mahaprabhu's closest companion in Nilachala.
[^05-77]: Kashi Mishra; Rama Raya is Ramananda Raya; Balarama (Dasa), Achyuta (Achyutananda), Ananta and Yashovanta (5.127), together with Jagannatha Dasa, make up the Panchasakha (cf. 1.76). The last word of 5.126 (printed ≈ ବେ + ତ/ଢ + ା + ୟ) is not identified; "surrounding" (cf. ବେଢ଼ିବା) is a guess prompted by the rhyme with ରାୟ. The word read in 5.125 and 5.127 as ମେଳେ ("in the gathering, together") is printed with an extra small sign.
[^05-78]: Balarama Dasa is the eldest of the Panchasakha, author of the Odia *Jagamohana Ramayana* (*Dandi Ramayana*) and the *Lakshmi Purana*; by tradition the son of Somanatha Mahapatra, an official at Prataparudra's court. Mahapurusha, "great person," is an honorific for saints. The second word of the half-line (printed ≈ ବେଲଇ?) is taken as ବୋଲଇ "called" — tentatively. The account of Balarama Dasa continues on sheet 84.
[^05-79]: Works on spiritual philosophy are also attributed to Balarama Dasa (e.g. the *Gupta Gita*). *Sūta* (printed with long ū) is taken as *suta*, "son."
[^05-80]: Balarama Dasa (cf. 5.131–132) is the author of the Odia *Jagamohana Ramayana* (*Dandi Ramayana*). A yoga-purusha is "a man of yoga", an ascetic endowed with yogic power. The last sign of the half-line (ତ) is taken as an emphatic particle ("indeed"); this is uncertain.
[^05-81]: Vamana here is Jagannatha on the chariot: in Puri the Lord seated on the chariot during the Ratha-yatra is called Vamana (Ratha-vamana). A well-known verse says that one who sees Vamana on the chariot is not born again.
[^05-82]: The servants (*sebaka*) are Jagannatha's temple servants, who stay on the chariots during the Ratha-yatra and decide who may climb up. The story of Balarama Dasa, the sand chariot and Jagannatha's halted chariot (5.134–164) is a well-known Odia tradition; it is not in the CC/CB.
[^05-83]: The second half-line is read uncertainly. Printed ≈ ଡାକିଲ ଆମ ନନ୍ଦବଳା: the third word ends in ମ (not ସ, as in ଆସ "come" in 5.142), hence taken as "our"; the last word is "child of Nanda" (*nanda-bāḷā*, Krishna, i.e. Jagannatha), printed without ‑ā. [ଆସ] "come" is also possible.
[^05-84]: The three Deities are Jagannatha, Balarama (Balabhadra) and Subhadra; *pratimā* means murti, Deity. *Bije hele* is the honorific "took Their seat, graciously came".
[^05-85]: The Danda, Bada-danda, is Puri's great road, along which the chariots are drawn from the temple to Gundicha (cf. 5.163). The word read as ଚିତ୍ର "[became] as if painted", i.e. "stood stunned", is printed indistinctly.
[^05-86]: The king is not named in this story; from the context of the chapter (cf. 5.109) he is Prataparudra. Cf. CC (Madhya 14): at the Ratha-yatra Jagannatha's chariot stopped and would not move even when elephants pulled it; it moved when Mahaprabhu pushed it with His head. That account has no story of Balarama Dasa. "Raged" renders *ugra hoi*, "becoming fierce"; the first word is indistinct.
[^05-87]: Printed ≈ ଘାଇ with a mark under ଘ. The sense requires "move" (ଘୁଞ୍ଚାଇ? in that case the print has lost ଞ୍ଚ); ଘାଇ "blow" fits worse. The word is not identified.
[^05-88]: The Lord appears to the king in a dream. "I am on the seashore" means that Jagannatha is on Balarama Dasa's sand chariot.
[^05-89]: The couplet is understood uncertainly: ଗସ୍ତ (*gasta*, Pers. "tour, procession") is a guess (perhaps ରାସ୍ତ…); ସ୍ୱାମୀକଇ is "and Svami" (cf. ≈ ସ୍ୱାମୀଙ୍କଇ in 5.110); ସମ୍ଭାର is "retinue, equipage". Prabhu is Sri Chaitanya; Svami is Jagannatha Dasa. The CC/CB say nothing of Mahaprabhu taking part in this event.
[^05-90]: Kashi Mishra was Mahaprabhu's host in Puri (CC, Madhya 10). We have not identified Tapana Acharya. Kanhai: cf. 2.25 and 5.77 (perhaps Kanai Khuntia). Ananta Acharya is perhaps the disciple of Gadadhara Pandita mentioned in the CC; this needs checking. Ananta of the Panchasakha is named separately in 5.153. The final word of both half-lines is printed ≈ ଆ + ଚ/ଗ + ‑u? + ର୍ଯେ; the reading ଆଚାର୍ଯ୍ୟେ ("Acharya") is a guess.
[^05-91]: Achyuta (Achyutananda), Ananta and Yashovanta belong to the Panchasakha (cf. 5.126–127). Tulasi Mishra: cf. Tulasi in 5.77 (perhaps Tulasi the paricha, the temple superintendent mentioned in the CC). Damodara Pandita was a companion of Mahaprabhu in Puri, known for the strictness of his judgements (CC, Antya 3).
[^05-92]: The couplet is not understood. Printed ≈ ଉ + ତ୍ସ + ବେ (ଉତ୍ସବେ "in the festivity"?), ଭାବ ("bhava") and ମନୋ + a sign ≈ ଢ/ର + ଥା ("longing"?); the second half-line has ଭକ୍ତରଙ୍ଗ ("the joy of the devotees") + ଅନୁସ + the same sign + ଥା (not identified).
[^05-93]: Prabhu here is apparently Jagannatha seated on the chariot (cf. 5.162). For the Lord yielding to a devotee's love, cf. *Bhagavata* 9.4.63 (*ahaṁ bhakta-parādhīnaḥ*, "I am subject to My devotees"). The speakers of 5.158–160 are not named; apparently the king and those assembled.
[^05-94]: The last word (ମୁରୁଛୁ) is not identified; "we are swooning" is a guess from *mūrcchā* "fainting". The ending ‑ଛୁ in ଅଛୁ (cf. 5.134) and ମୁରୁଛୁ is unclear.
[^05-95]: The closing couplet of the chapter, with the author's name (cf. 4.160). Below it sheet 85 has only a dot, and sheet 86 opens with the heading «ଷଷ୍ଠ ଅଧ୍ୟାୟ» — Chapter 6. The verb of the first half-line is printed ≈ ଗ/ରା + ‑u? + ଳ + ଇ; the sense requires "moves, rolls" (ଚଳଇ, ଗଡ଼ଇ). *Ghiṛi ghiṛi* is onomatopoeia (the rumbling, creaking of wheels); the second word is printed without the final ‑i.

---

# Madhava Pattanayak. *Vaishnava-lilamrita*, Chapter 6
## The Dispute over the Beginning of the Mahamantra; Shivananda, Kanhai and the Pilgrims; Madhavi Dasi


**6.1.** Listen, sadhus, to this sweet account — [of] what the secret of the mahamantra is.[^06-1]  
**6.2.** "Hare Rama" or "Hare Krishna"? — over this a quarrel arose (?).  
**6.3.** Shivananda says: "Hare Krishna"; Kanhai says: "Hare Rama."[^06-2]  
**6.4.** "What should stand at the beginning of the mantra?" — over this a dispute arises.  
**6.5.** They wrangled at length — and went to Svarupa.[^06-3]  
**6.6.** Svarupa says: "Come, let us go and learn from Svami."[^06-4]  
**6.7.** Svami said: "Very well," — and sent word to call Prabhu.  
**6.8.** Prabhu summons Rama Raya, Sarvabhauma, Kashi Mishra.[^06-5]  
**6.9.** In the northern (??) side mandapa all gathered:[^06-6]  
**6.10.** Ananta, Achyuta, Kanhai, Shivananda and Govinda (?).[^06-7]  
**6.11.** They made Prabhu head of the assembly: "Do judge, [O Lord] (?)," they said.  
**6.12.** Sarvabhauma — [for] "Hare Rama"; Gadadhara — [for] "Hare Krishna."[^06-8]  
**6.13.** Kashi Mishra says: "In the Kshetra the 'Hare Rama' mantra is current."  
**6.14.** There was much reasoned (?) debate; at last Prabhu proclaimed:  
**6.15.** "The 'Hare Rama' mantra is the essence; in Nilachala it is this that is in use.[^06-9]  
**6.16.** What else should we do about this? Give up (?) the initial 'Hare Krishna'?"[^06-10]  
**6.17.** Shivananda says: "In Gauda [the mantra] beginning with 'Hare Krishna' is current."  
**6.18.** Prabhu said: "You speak rightly: in Gauda [let it] be 'Hare Krishna.'"[^06-11]  
**6.19.** Meanwhile — listen to the account: Shivananda used to bring pilgrims (?) —[^06-12]  
**6.20.** to bring those who come for darshan to the Ratha [festival] and to stay a month in the Kshetra.  
**6.21.** With pilgrims from everywhere (?) he did business for cowries (?).[^06-13]  
**6.22.** Kanhai did not tolerate this; he says: "[He] took the pilgrims and went off (?)."[^06-14]  
**6.23.** Kanhai brought about a hundred [people] and struck fear (?) into the pilgrims.[^06-15]  
**6.24.** Because of this (?) a quarrel began; Kashi Mishra becomes the mediator.[^06-16]  
**6.25.** Kashi Mishra restrained (?) [them]: "Make no trouble — this way [it is] well."[^06-17]  
**6.26.** The sister of Shikhi Mahanti — her name was Madhavi Dasi.[^06-18]  

## Madhavi Dasi in Mahaprabhu's Gathering; Junior Haridasa


**6.27.** Great was her devotion; her heart [was set] on sankirtana.[^06-19]  
**6.28.** In the Name she lost herself; she chanted the Name unceasingly.[^06-20]  
**6.29.** She composed many songs, measureless in the taste [rasa] of Krishna.  
**6.30.** Before Svami and Prabhu she sings songs, lost in bhava.[^06-21]  
**6.31.** Prabhu showed her mercy: "Sit in the goshthi," [He] said.[^06-22]  
**6.32.** [Then] the devotees left the ishta-goshthi: "We shall come no more," they said.  
**6.33.** "A woman is beside Him; even if she is a devotee — [what] trust (?) [is there]?"[^06-23]  
**6.34.** "We (?) shall not be here," so they declared in Prabhu's presence.[^06-24]  
**6.35.** Svami said: "… (??): Madhavi Dasi is your equal.[^06-25]  
**6.36.** Is there anywhere (?) another such devotee? And you say: let her go away!"  
**6.37.** [But] Vaishnavas, carried away by feeling, become [like] beasts (?) near a woman.[^06-26]  
**6.38.** Even a woman's figure of wood or stone Vaishnavas [should] not look at.  
**6.39.** And Madhavi is a body of blood and flesh: that is the true point.[^06-27]  
**6.40.** This word is sure proof (?): her coming [must be] forbidden.  
**6.41.** Madhavi came no more; she gave up the company of the ishta-goshthi.  
**6.42.** Among Prabhu's companions are Gaudiyas, [but] not all [of them] are Chaitanya.[^06-28]  
**6.43.** Not in the navel of every musk deer, indeed, is (?) musk found,[^06-29]  
**6.44.** not in every forest [grows] sandalwood — know this for certain;  
**6.45.** [so too] not all Gaudiya Vaishnavas are equal to Svarupa.[^06-30]  
**6.46.** [There was] one called Junior Haridasa; he was a companion (?) [of Prabhu] in Puri-dhama.[^06-31]  
**6.47.** His intimacy with Madhavi grew day by day.  
**6.48.** He goes to Madhavi, [making up] many idle pretexts.  
**6.49.** For the sake of rice and salt he hastens (?) to meet her at her place.[^06-32]  
**6.50.** Shikhi Mahanti forbade [him], yet he did not stop going.[^06-33]  
**6.51.** The neighbours (?) grew suspicious: "This is not proper," they said.[^06-34]  
**6.52.** One day, at night-time, seeing [him], spotting Junior Haridasa,  
**6.53.** Shikhi Mahanti beat [him]: foolish Haridasa got a beating from his hand.[^06-35]  
**6.54.** The Gaudiyas gathered and went to report to Prabhu.  
**6.55.** Prabhu said: "[He] did well. Has (??) Haridasa [behaved] well?[^06-36]  
**6.56.** It is not fitting to look at his face," — and He rebuked him much.  

## Haridasa's Departure; the Gaudiyas Leave Nilachala; Svami's Eight Shlokas; the Gaudiyas in Vrindavana


**6.57.** Haridasa [felt] great grief; in a watch of the night he went straight away.[^06-37]  
**6.58.** He did not stay in Nilachala — he went and threw himself into the Ganga.[^06-38]  
**6.59.** On receiving the news the Gaudiyas gathered and lamented (?):[^06-39]  
**6.60.** "Shall we go or stay in this place? Prabhu's heart has become estranged [from us].  
**6.61.** Since (?) Prabhu so extols Jaga Dasa[^06-40]  
**6.62.** and keeps silent (?) with us — we are leaving: [our] honour (??) is lost.[^06-41]  
**6.63.** Jagadasa, who has received no instruction, He proclaims by the name 'Svami.'[^06-42]  
**6.64.** He has neglected us — come, let us all go from here."  
**6.65.** In a watch of the night they all went away — and did not come back.[^06-43]  
**6.66.** Svami, Svarupa Damodara, Shivananda and Gadadhara —  
**6.67.** these sat in Prabhu's presence and deliberated over the trouble (?).[^06-44]  
**6.68.** Shivananda says, calling out: "Your dealing ought not to be like this!  
**6.69.** For Your sake (?) they lived here — and now they have all gone.[^06-45]  
**6.70.** This is not good for You: bring them back to the Kshetra (?)."[^06-46]  
**6.71.** Prabhu looked (?) at Svami's face — Svami turned away.  
**6.72.** Prabhu said: "Will you not look [at Me] (??)? If you go along with Me (?), [then] we shall go."[^06-47]
