**6.73.** [Svami:] "A fool does not go with You (?). If You call, they will come; not (??) done…"[^06-48]  
**6.74.** Prabhu said: "Write a letter — they will turn back and return."  
**6.75.** Svami wrote eight shlokas — all of Prabhu's bhava, as much as there is.[^06-49]  
**6.76.** He recited the shlokas to Prabhu; in joy Prabhu embraced [him].  
**6.77.** Shivananda took [the letter] and went; "Prabhu wrote this," he said.[^06-50]  
**6.78.** As far as Yajanagara — there, in that place, they all were.[^06-51]  
**6.79.** No one came back — Shivananda along [with them].[^06-52]  
**6.80.** All went by the road to Gauda; from there a large part [of them]  
**6.81.** went to Vrindavana — they met Rupa and Sanatana.[^06-53]  
**6.82.** Rupa settled [them] … (??) as far as Mount Govardhana.[^06-54]  
**6.83.** Vallabha's disciples were [there]; on seeing (?) the Gaudiyas, at once —[^06-55]  
**6.84.** a most fierce quarrel begins; the crowd of Gaudiyas is beaten.[^06-56]  
**6.85.** The Gaudiyas said: "All the income from the temple (?) we shall eat."[^06-57]  

## The Fray on Govardhana and Prabhu's Word; "The Swami's Eight Shlokas"


**6.86.** They did not get to enjoy (?) the service of Gopala — and so a fierce quarrel broke out.[^06-58]  
**6.87.** The servants of Sri Vallabha gave some of them a beating.[^06-59]  
**6.88.** Some of the Gaudiyas survived (?), and some departed from the body (??).[^06-60]  
**6.89.** Some (?) remained in that place, near Rupa Gosai.[^06-61]  
**6.90.** By the [festival of the] Ratha (?) we heard the news: Kanhai came and told it.[^06-62]  
**6.91.** Kanhai reported to Prabhu. Prabhu said: "It has turned out well.[^06-63]  
**6.92.** Whoever is 'beaten' by nature will be beaten constantly.[^06-64]  
**6.93.** And still sense will not enter them: as each one's nature is, [so he is]."  

*The Swami's Eight Shlokas*[^06-65]

**6.Sh1.** *[Skt.]* Supremely victorious is the sankirtana of Sri Krishna: it cleanses the mirror of the heart, extinguishes the great forest fire of worldly existence, sheds moonlight on the white lily of welfare, and is the life of the bride, knowledge; it swells the ocean of bliss, gives at every step a taste of full nectar, and bathes the whole self.  
**6.Sh1a.** In glorifying Krishna's qualities the mirror of the mind is cleansed.  
**6.Sh1b.** The stain of the soul goes away, and an exalted bhava streams forth.  
**6.Sh1c.** Sankirtana is the giver of teaching; it awakens bhakti.  
**6.Sh1d.** Pure prema grows; [sankirtana] gives a taste of the honey of prema for Krishna.  
**6.Sh1e.** All the senses are satisfied — such is the sankirtana of Krishna's name.  
**6.Sh2.** *[Skt.]* You have manifested many names and placed all Your potencies in them, and set no [special] time for remembering [them]. Such is Your mercy, O Lord — and such is my misfortune that no attachment [to them] has arisen in me.  
**6.Sh2a.** Your names are endless; many names You bear.  
**6.Sh2b.** In all the names is Your power; it is manifestly seen (??), [the sages] say.[^06-66]  
**6.Sh2c.** In remembering and chanting the Name there is no distinction of proper or improper time.  
**6.Sh2d.** O Prabhu! Such is Your mercy, but (?) I am unfortunate.  
**6.Sh2e.** In my (?) heart there is no faith, and love (?) for the Name does not arise (?).[^06-67]  
**6.Sh3.** *[Skt.]* Humbler than a blade of grass, more tolerant than a tree, seeking no honour and giving honour [to others] — [thus] should Hari always be glorified.  
**6.Sh3a.** Deem [yourself] lower than grass; be more tolerant than a tree.  
**6.Sh3b.** Giving up your own happiness and sorrow, pour out love upon others.  
**6.Sh3c.** Always repeat the name of Hari — there is no other refuge than this.  
**6.Sh4.** *[Skt.]* Neither wealth, nor followers, nor a beautiful woman, nor poetry do I desire, O Lord of the world. May there be in every birth of mine causeless devotion to You, O Ishvara.  
**6.Sh4a.** I want neither wealth nor followers, nor the jewel of a beautiful wife.  
**6.Sh4b.** [Nor] to become a poet by composing (?) verses. O Prabhu! Why should I desire [these]?  
**6.Sh4c.** Piteously I pray: O Prabhu! Birth after birth,  
**6.Sh4d.** becoming Your follower, may I go on performing bhakti.  
**6.Sh5.** *[Skt.]* O son of Nanda! Me, Your servant, fallen into the terrible ocean of existence, consider in Your mercy a particle of dust at Your lotus feet.[^06-68]  
**6.Sh5a.** O Krishna, son of the cowherd Nanda! Have mercy, O Gopinatha!  
**6.Sh5b.** A fallen fool in this world, I am sinking and perishing in the ocean of existence.  
**6.Sh5c.** At Your lotus feet hold [me] as a speck of dust.  

## "The Swami's Eight Shlokas" (conclusion); the End of Chapter Six


**6.Sh6.** *[Skt.]* When, as I take Your name, will my eyes [run] with streams of tears, my mouth [falter] with a voice choked with emotion, and my body be covered with hairs standing on end?[^06-69]  
**6.Sh6a.** O Jagannatha, round-eyed One! When will that day come[^06-70]  
**6.Sh6b.** when, as Your name is uttered, [my] eyes will shed tears,  
**6.Sh6c.** when, as soon as I utter Your name, my throat will be choked entirely,  
**6.Sh6d.** and my whole body will tremble with joy, its hairs standing on end?  
**6.Sh7.** *[Skt.]* A moment has become for me like a yuga, my eyes like the rainy season, the whole world has become empty — in separation from Govinda.[^06-71]  
**6.Sh7a.** In separation from Hari my heart burns beyond measure;  
**6.Sh7b.** a moment is like a yuga, water streams from my eyes in torrents;  
**6.Sh7c.** the whole world (?) seems empty — heavy is the pain of separation from Hari.  
**6.Sh8.** *[Skt.]* Let Him embrace [me], who am devoted to His feet, or trample me; or let Him wound my heart by not showing Himself — let that rake do as He likes: still the Lord of my life is He, and no other.[^06-72]  
**6.Sh8a.** O Krishna! Give me pain — or employ [me in service] at Your feet;[^06-73]  
**6.Sh8b.** embrace me — or do not give [me] darshana;  
**6.Sh8c.** [though] You sport, absorbed in love and intoxicated with it, … (??) —[^06-74]  
**6.Sh8d.** still You are my life: apart from You [I] have no other refuge.  
**6.Sh8e.** All this bhava of Radha (?) the Swami described in eight shlokas.[^06-75]  

*Thus ends the sixth chapter.*

[^06-1]: The mahamantra is "Hare Krishna Hare Krishna Krishna Krishna Hare Hare / Hare Rama Hare Rama Rama Rama Hare Hare" (*Kali-santarana Upanishad*). In the common recensions of the Upanishad the mantra begins with "Hare Rama"; Gaudiya Vaishnavas chant it beginning with "Hare Krishna," as Mahaprabhu Himself taught (CB, Madhya 23). The dispute over the order of the two halves of the mantra and Mahaprabhu's judgement (6.2–18) are an Odia tradition; they are not in the CC/CB. Printed ସାଧବେ is taken as a vocative plural, "O sadhus."
[^06-2]: Shivananda is Shivananda Sena, the Bengali devotee who every year led the Gaudiyas to Puri for the Ratha-yatra (CC, Madhya 16; Antya 1, 12). Kanhai is apparently Kanai Khuntia, an Odia Vaishnava of the Panchasakha circle (cf. 5.11).
[^06-3]: Svarupa Damodara, Mahaprabhu's closest companion in Nilachala; according to the CC (Antya 5), every composition or question meant for Mahaprabhu first passed through him.
[^06-4]: Svami is Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 5.101, 5.119–124). In Madhava the dispute is settled through him, with Svarupa's mediation; there is no such episode in the CC/CB.
[^06-5]: Rama Raya is Ramananda Raya; Sarvabhauma is Sarvabhauma Bhattacharya; Kashi Mishra is the one in whose house Mahaprabhu lived in Puri (CC, Madhya 10).
[^06-6]: The first word is printed unclearly (≈ ଭ + a blotted sign) and is read as "northern" only from the sense and the metre (2 syllables, ≈ ଉତ୍ତ). *Pāruśa maṇḍapa* is a "side mandapa" (a pillared hall); which building is meant is not said.
[^06-7]: Ananta and Achyuta (Achyutananda) belong to the Panchasakha. "Govindai" is probably Govinda, Mahaprabhu's servant (CC, Madhya 10), with the affectionate ending ‑ai; the reading is uncertain.
[^06-8]: Gadadhara is Gadadhara Pandita, Madhava's teacher. In Madhava the Bengalis (Shivananda, Gadadhara) stand for "Hare Krishna," the Odias and residents of Puri (Kanhai, Kashi Mishra, and also Sarvabhauma, though he came from Navadvipa) for "Hare Rama."
[^06-9]: Mahaprabhu's decision — "Hare Rama" first for Nilachala, "Hare Krishna" for Gauda (6.15–18) — is an Odia tradition. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, chant the mahamantra beginning with "Hare Krishna" (cf. the note to 6.1).
[^06-10]: The last word (printed ≈ ଗ୍ର/ଛ + ଡ଼ + ବା) is read as ଛାଡ଼ିବା "shall we give up" — a guess; if it is ଗଢ଼ିବା "shall we set," the sense is: "What else should we do — put 'Hare Krishna' at the beginning?" Printed ଏଥକୁ, କ, କଶବା are normalised as ଏଥିକୁ, କି, କରିବା.
[^06-11]: Gauda is Bengal. *Kahu bhala* is taken as "you speak rightly" (addressed to Shivananda).
[^06-12]: According to the CC (Madhya 16; Antya 12), Shivananda Sena every year led the Bengali devotees to Puri for the Ratha-yatra, bore the travel expenses and tolls, and arranged their lodging. The reading "pilgrims" (printed ≈ ଯତ୍ରି) is uncertain.
[^06-13]: Cowries are shells used as small coin. If the reading is right, Madhava speaks of income from bringing pilgrims. In the CC, by contrast, Shivananda Sena himself maintains the pilgrims (Madhya 16; Antya 12), and Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour him as an eternal associate of the Lord. The word ଅଖିଳର ("from everywhere, of all") is printed ≈ ଆଖିଳର.
[^06-14]: Who "took the pilgrims" is not said; understood as Kanhai's complaint against Shivananda. ଘେନ ପଳା — "taking [them], went off" (?).
[^06-15]: Whom Kanhai brought ("about a hundred," *śaheka*) — his own pilgrims or his own men — is not said. ତାସ is taken as *trāsa* "fear" — uncertain.
[^06-16]: Printed ≈ ହେତ — taken as ହେତୁ "because of [this]" (rhyming with ମଧ୍ୟସ୍ଥ).
[^06-17]: Printed ≈ ସମ୍ବାଶଲେ ("ri" as ଶ) — read ସମ୍ବାରିଲେ "restrained, calmed" (cf. ସମ୍ବରିବା); possibly ସମ୍ଭାଳିଲେ "settled."
[^06-18]: Madhavi is Madhavi-devi, the sister of Shikhi Mahiti; in the CC (Antya 2) Mahaprabhu counts among Radha's associates "three and a half" persons in the world — Svarupa Damodara, Ramananda Raya, Shikhi Mahiti and, as the "half," his sister Madhavi. In Madhava the brother is called ମହାନ୍ତର ("Mahanti"). The account of Madhavi continues on sheet 88 (6.27 ff.).
[^06-19]: Madhavi Dasi (cf. 6.26) — in the CC (Antya 2), Madhavi-devi, the sister of Shikhi Mahiti: an elderly, strict ascetic whom Mahaprabhu counted among the associates of Srimati Radharani. The CC says nothing of her songs or of her taking part in Mahaprabhu's gatherings; this is Odia tradition. *Bhagata* is taken as "devotion" (= *bhakti*); "[she was] a great devotee" is also possible.
[^06-20]: *Bhoḷa* — "lost, enraptured"; in this font ଭା is set as a ligature, so ଭୋଳ, ଭାବ, ଭାବେ (6.28, 6.30, 6.37, 6.47) look like ଭେଳ, ଭବ, ଭେବ. Cf. "ମତ ଭୋଳେ" (3.53), "ଭୋଳ ହୋନ୍ତି" (1.44).
[^06-21]: Svami — Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 6.6–7); Prabhu — Mahaprabhu.
[^06-22]: Goshthi, ishta-goshthi — a gathering of devotees for talk about the Lord (cf. the note to 5.108). Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following the CC (Antya 2), stress that Mahaprabhu as a sannyasi most strictly avoided the company of women and required the same of His renounced companions; the account of His seating Madhavi in the gathering is Odia tradition, not found in the CC/CB.
[^06-23]: The last word (printed ≈ ବଶ୍ୱ:ସ) is read as ବିଶ୍ୱାସ "trust, faith"; the understanding of the half-line — "even if she is a devotee, [what] trust [is there]?" — is uncertain. "Even if she is a devotee [worthy of] trust" is also possible. Printed ଅଛ is taken as ଅଛି "is".
[^06-24]: The first word (printed ≈ ଆମ୍ଭ + a sign like ନ୍ତ with a tail) is taken as "we" (cf. ଆମ୍ଭେ); the form is not identified. *Jaṇāi* — "making known, declaring".
[^06-25]: The end of the first half-line (printed ≈ ମଟ ସଲ / ମଡ ସଲ) is not identified. Svami (Jagannatha Dasa) here defends Madhavi; the understanding of 6.36a ("is there anywhere another [such] devotee?", printed ≈ କାହିଁଛ କ) is from the sense.
[^06-26]: The speaker of 6.37–40 is not marked: it may be Svami continuing (having acknowledged Madhavi's devotion, he nevertheless grants the concern) or the devotees' reply; the conclusion in 6.40 ("her coming [must be] forbidden") and 6.41 show that this argument prevailed. Cf. in the CC (Madhya 11) the saying cited by Mahaprabhu: a renunciant should be wary even of the form of a woman, as even the form of a snake disturbs the mind; in the CC (Antya 2), the *Bhagavata* verse that the senses drag away even the learned. *Paśu yāi* is taken as "become [like] beasts" — uncertain (printed ≈ ପବୁ/ପଶୁ ଯାଇ); possibly ପଶି ଯାଇ "having come (lit. entered) near a woman".
[^06-27]: *Parasaṅga* (= *prasaṅga*) — "matter, point [of the argument]" or "contact, association"; possibly "with Madhavi [ours is] real contact".
[^06-28]: Probably a pun: "not all are Chaitanya", i.e. not all of Mahaprabhu's companions are like Him, or "not all are awakened" (*caitanya* — "consciousness, awakening"). Madhava turns to the story of Junior Haridasa.
[^06-29]: 6.43–45 echo a well-known Sanskrit saying (attributed to Chanakya): not every mountain has gems, not every elephant a pearl, not every forest sandalwood, nor are sadhus found everywhere. Printed ≈ ଥାଇଟି is read as ଥାଇନ୍ତି "is found".
[^06-30]: Svarupa — Svarupa Damodara, himself a Bengali and the model Gaudiya Vaishnava; the understanding "not all Gaudiya Vaishnavas are alike in nature (*swarūpa*)" is also possible.
[^06-31]: Junior Haridasa (*sāna* "junior, little"; in the CC, Chhota Haridasa), a kirtan singer of Mahaprabhu. According to the CC (Antya 2), at Bhagavan Acharya's request he begged fine rice for Mahaprabhu from Madhavi-devi; learning that a renunciant had spoken with a woman, Mahaprabhu forbade him admittance; a year later Haridasa went to Prayaga and gave up his body at the Triveni, after which, according to the CC, he continued to sing for Mahaprabhu in a spiritual body. Gaudiya Vaishnavas see in this a lesson for renunciants, not a fall of Haridasa. Madhava's account (6.47–53: growing intimacy, pretexts, the neighbours' suspicion, the beating) is absent from the CC/CB. The reading *saṅgī* "companion" (printed ≈ ମଙ୍ଗୀ/ସଙ୍ଗୀ) is uncertain.
[^06-32]: In the CC (Antya 2) Haridasa asks Madhavi for rice to feed Mahaprabhu, not for himself. *Turite* "hastily" (printed ≈ ତୁଶତେ, "ri" as ଶ) is an uncertain reading; lit. "for the sake of rice and salt, at her place [he] quickly meets / obtains".
[^06-33]: Shikhi Mahanti — in the CC, Shikhi Mahiti, Madhavi's brother (cf. 6.26). Printed ମହାନ୍ତ (here and in 6.53) — probably ମହାନ୍ତି with the ‑i lost.
[^06-34]: *Sāibhāi* — "neighbours, people of the quarter" (*sāi* — ward, street); printed ≈ ସାଇ + ligature ଭା (?) + ଇ.
[^06-35]: There is no beating by Shikhi Mahiti in the CC.
[^06-36]: The second half-line is not read with confidence: printed ≈ ବ + େ + a dark sign (ଞ୍ଚ/ଷ/ଝ?) — ବଞ୍ଚେ "lives, survives"?; the translation "has Haridasa [behaved] well?" is from the sense. "[He] did well" is taken as approval of Shikhi Mahanti. In the CC (Antya 2) Mahaprabhu Himself, with no complaint from the Gaudiyas, tells Govinda not to admit Haridasa, saying that He cannot look at the face of a renunciant who converses with a woman.
[^06-37]: Haridasa is "Sana Haridasa," Junior Haridasa (cf. 6.46–56). According to the CC (Antya 2), Mahaprabhu rejected him because, as a renunciant, he had begged rice from a woman, Madhavi-devi; after a year Haridasa went to Prayaga and entered the waters of the Triveni, after which he attained a spiritual body and went on singing for Mahaprabhu unseen. Gaudiya Vaishnavas see in this a lesson of strictness for sannyasis and the Lord's mercy to Haridasa. In Madhava the cause of his leaving is different (a beating from Shikhi Mahanti, cf. 6.53), and how it all ended is not stated outright — only "threw himself into the Ganga" (6.58). *Rāti praharu* — "in [one of] the night watches"; a watch (prahara) is about three hours.
[^06-38]: In the CC (Antya 2) it is the Triveni at Prayaga, the confluence of the Ganga and the Yamuna; in Madhava simply "the Ganga."
[^06-39]: The last word is printed ≈ ବସୁଶଥା; read as ବିସୁରିଥା from *bisuribā* "to grieve, lament" ("ri" as ଶ, ‑i lost); the form in ‑ithā is unclear. The words of 6.60–64 are taken as the Gaudiyas' speech.
[^06-40]: Jaga Dasa, Jagadasa (6.63) is Jagannatha Dasa, called "Svami" by Madhava (cf. 6.6). The first unclear word (printed ≈ ଯ + ଢ଼ି with a dot) is read as ଯହିଁ "since" — a guess.
[^06-41]: Both marked words are unclear. The first (printed ≈ a sign like ଅ/ଝ/ମ + ଉ + ନ) is read as ମଉନ (= *mauna*) "silence": "He keeps silent with us," i.e. pays us no heed. The second (printed ≈ blotted ନ/ଚ + ା + କ) is read as ନାକ "nose," in the idiom "the nose is gone" = "honour is lost"; this reading is very uncertain.
[^06-42]: *Aṇa-upadeśiā* — "one who has received no instruction (upadesha)," i.e. no initiation from a guru; this is the Gaudiyas' reproach against Jagannatha Dasa. In Madhava, Prabhu nevertheless calls him "Svami"; Jagannatha Dasa is not mentioned in the CC/CB.
[^06-43]: The Gaudiyas' departure from Nilachala in resentment against Mahaprabhu, their halt at Yajanagara and their return to Gauda (6.59–80) are an Odia tradition; there is nothing of this in the CC/CB. According to the CC, the Gaudiyas headed by Shivananda Sena came to Mahaprabhu every year for the Ratha-yatra and spent the chaturmasya with Him (Madhya 16; Antya 10, 12).
[^06-44]: Printed ≈ ଉ + ପ/ଦ with a subscript + ାଧି; read as ଉପାଧି "trouble, difficulty" — uncertain.
[^06-45]: In 6.68–70 Shivananda addresses Prabhu with the familiar "thou" (*tohara, tora, tote*); rendered "You." The first word of 6.69b (printed ≈ ନମିତ୍ତ, first sign blotted) is read as ନିମିତ୍ତ "for the sake of."
[^06-46]: The last word is printed ≈ େ + କ୍ଷ + ତ/ତ୍ର + ର; read as କ୍ଷେତ୍ରର "of the Kshetra" (genitive meaning "to the Kshetra"?) — uncertain.
[^06-47]: The speakers in 6.71–73 are not marked. Understood thus: Prabhu wants Svami to go with Him after those who left; Svami turns away (*bimukha*) and declines. The word in 6.72a (printed ≈ ଚ/ବୁ + ହାଁ; cf. 6.71a ≈ ଚ + ହିଁ + ଲ) is read as ଚାହାଁ "look" (from *cāhi̐bā*); ବୁଝା "understand" ("Do you not understand?") is also possible. ମୋ ତହୁଁ (?) is taken as "with Me / from Me."
[^06-48]: "A fool" — apparently Svami speaking of himself (self-abasement) or of those who left. The end of the line (printed ≈ ଡାକିଲେ ଆସିବେ + a blotted ନ/ମ + କଲେ) is unclear: "if You call, they will come; [but if You] do not do [so]…" or "…they did not do [it]."
[^06-49]: The "eight shlokas" are apparently the *Shikshashtaka* (cf. 6.77: Shivananda passes them off as written by Prabhu; after 6.93 the edition gives these verses under the heading "Svami's eight shlokas"). Madhava ascribes them to Jagannatha Dasa, who expressed Prabhu's bhava in them — an Odia tradition. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, revere the *Shikshashtaka* as the composition of Sri Chaitanya Himself: according to the CC (Antya 20), Mahaprabhu Himself recited and relished these verses with Svarupa Damodara and Ramananda Raya in the last years of His lila; a number of these verses are given in Rupa Gosvami's *Padyavali* as the words of the Lord Himself.
[^06-50]: *Lekhichu* is taken as an old perfect form "has written" (cf. ‑achu); the words "Prabhu wrote this" are apparently what Shivananda told those who had left.
[^06-51]: Yajanagara is Jajpur (Yajapura) on the Vaitarani river, on the road from Puri to Bengal. *Pariyantare* — "as far as" (= *paryanta*).
[^06-52]: According to the CC, Shivananda Sena is a close companion of Mahaprabhu who brought the Gaudiyas to Puri every year (Madhya 16; Antya 12); that he left with the offended party and did not return is not in the CC/CB.
[^06-53]: Rupa and Sanatana Gosvami lived in Vrindavana by Mahaprabhu's order, restoring the holy places and the Deity service (CC, Madhya 19–20, 23).
[^06-54]: The third word of 6.82a (printed ≈ ସେ + an incomplete sign like ଯ/ବ + ାନନ୍ତ) is not identified; the sense of the line is a guess. *Yābanta* — "as far as."
[^06-55]: Vallabha is Vallabhacharya, founder of the Pushti-marga Vaishnava sampradaya; according to the CC (Antya 7), he met Mahaprabhu in Puri. The Deity of Gopala on Govardhana was manifested by Madhavendra Puri (CC, Madhya 4); later His service passed to Vallabha's followers (Shrinathji). The quarrel of the Gaudiyas with Vallabha's disciples at Govardhana (6.83–86) is Madhava's account; it is not in the CC. *Cāṭa* — "disciple." The word in 6.83b (printed ≈ ନରେଟି) is read as ନିରେଖି "on seeing" — uncertain.
[^06-56]: *Māṛiā* — "beaten, having received a beating" (cf. 6.92 *māṛiā sbabhāba* "the nature of one who gets beaten").
[^06-57]: *Arajana* (= *arjana*) — "what is acquired, income," i.e. the temple offerings. The first unclear word (printed ≈ େ + ଦ/ଢ + ଉ + ଳୁ) is read as ଦେଉଳୁ "from the temple"; the rhyme in *‑u* (*deuḷu / khāibu*) supports this.
[^06-58]: Gopala is the Deity revealed by Madhavendra Puri on Govardhana (CC, Madhya 4); later His service passed to the followers of Vallabha (cf. the note to 6.83). According to Madhava, the Gaudiyas wanted to take over the service and the temple income (6.85). The key word (printed ≈ ଭୁଞ୍ଜଲେ, first sign smudged) is read as ଭୁଞ୍ଜିଲେ "enjoyed, obtained the use of" — uncertain. The quarrel between the Gaudiyas and Vallabha's disciples is not in the CC/CB.
[^06-59]: Sri Vallabha — Vallabhacharya (cf. 6.83). *Māile māṛa* — "gave a beating" (*māile* = ମାରିଲେ).
[^06-60]: The reading is uncertain. 6.88a: printed ≈ ବଞ୍ଚଲେ, taken as ବଞ୍ଚିଲେ "survived". 6.88b: printed ≈ ଛୁଡ଼ି ଗଲେକ ଦେଥୁ ଥୋକେ; ଦେଥୁ is corrected to ଦେହୁ "from the body" — "some departed from the body", i.e. perished; ଦେଶୁ "from the country" — "some fled the land" — is also possible. The contrast with "survived" supports the first sense, but it remains doubtful. The CC/CB say nothing of this.
[^06-61]: Rupa Gosai — Rupa Gosvami (cf. 6.81). Printed ≈ କହୁ with an inner stroke in କ (as in କିପାଁ, 6.Sh4b) is read as କିଛୁ "some".
[^06-62]: *Rathaku* is taken as "for the Ratha [festival]", i.e. the Ratha-yatra, when pilgrims gather in Puri (the first printed letter is ର; the reading ଏଥକୁ is less likely). *Śuṇilu* — "we heard": Madhava speaks as an eyewitness. Kanhai is the same as in 6.3–22; possibly Kanai Khutiya, an Odia associate of Mahaprabhu (in the CC, Madhya 15, he appears at the Nandotsava festival dressed as Nanda Maharaja) — the identification needs checking.
[^06-63]: The speaker in 6.91b–93 is Prabhu. The account of the Gaudiyas leaving Puri, of the fray in Vrindavana and of such a remark by Mahaprabhu is not in the CC/CB. According to the CC (Madhya 11, 16), the Bengali devotees, led by Shivananda Sena, came to Puri every year for the Ratha-yatra and returned to Bengal by Mahaprabhu's will; Gaudiya Vaishnavas see in His dealings with them unfailing love.
[^06-64]: *Māṛiā swabhāba* — lit. "the nature of one beaten" (cf. 6.84): one who by nature invites a beating; "a quarrelsome nature" is also possible.
[^06-65]: The heading "ସ୍ୱାମୀର ଅଷ୍ଟଶ୍ଲୋକ" ("The Swami's Eight Shlokas") stands in the edition; whether it belongs to the manuscript is unknown. Sanskrit shlokas 1–5 (sheets 92–93; shlokas 6–8 are on sheet 94) coincide with the *Śikṣāṣṭaka*; each is followed by an Odia paraphrase in couplets (whether by Madhava or by the Swami is not stated). Madhava ascribes the shlokas to the Swami, i.e. Jagannatha Dasa: in 6.75 "the Swami wrote eight shlokas — all the bhavas of Prabhu there are" — as a letter calling the Gaudiyas back; in 6.77 Shivananda presents them as written by Prabhu; on sheet 94 (outside this portion) the closing couplet again says that the Swami set forth the eight shlokas. This is Odia tradition. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, honour the *Śikṣāṣṭaka* as the composition of Sri Chaitanya Himself: according to the CC (Antya 20), Mahaprabhu Himself composed the eight shlokas to instruct people and in the last years of His lila relished them with Svarupa Damodara and Ramananda Raya; several of these shlokas are cited in Rupa Gosvami's *Padyavali* as the words of the Lord Himself. The Sanskrit text is written in its usual spelling where the edition's type loses ‑i/‑ī; printed deviations are noted in the word-for-word.
[^06-66]: The word before *badanti* (printed ≈ ଦ + ଶ with the signs ି and ୁ/୍ର + େ + ଛ with a dot) is not read with confidence; ଦିଶୁଛେ "is seen, appears" is adopted. *Badanti* is Sanskrit for "they say".
[^06-67]: Printed ≈ ମୋହ — taken as "my" (= ମୋହର?); *moha* "delusion [in the heart]" is also possible. The last words (printed ≈ ?ଦର େହଇ) are read as ଆଦର ହେଇ "love arises"; the negation is understood from the first half, following the sense of the Sanskrit shloka (*nānurāgaḥ* "no attachment").
[^06-68]: In print the line ends with ପାଦପଙ୍କଜମ and the next begins with ସ୍ଥିତ; in the usual text of the shloka this is a single compound, *pāda-paṅkaja-sthita-dhūlī-sadṛśam* "like a particle of dust resting at the lotus feet". The final ‑ମ is probably a misprint.
[^06-69]: Shlokas 6–8 of "The Swami's Eight Shlokas" coincide with the sixth to eighth verses of the *Shikshashtaka* (CC, Antya 20.36, 20.39, 20.47); on Madhava's ascription of them to the Swami (Jagannatha Dasa) and on the position of the Gaudiya Vaishnavas, who honour the *Shikshashtaka* as the words of Sri Chaitanya Himself, see the notes to 6.75 and 6.Sh1. In the printed Sanskrit text ‑i is lost in places; *gadgada* is printed ≈ ଗଦନଦ (the sign after the first ଦ looks like ନ). The translation follows the usual text of the shloka.
[^06-70]: *Cakā-ākhi* — "round-eyed," lit. "with wheel- (disc-) eyes": a favourite Odia epithet of Jagannatha, whose round eyes are wide open. The Sanskrit shloka does not name the Lord; the Odia rendering addresses Jagannatha. The last word (printed ≈ ଆସିବକ) is read as ଆସିବ କି "will [it] come?"; the rhyme *ākhi / ki* supports this.
[^06-71]: A yuga is a world age lasting thousands of years; "the rainy season" stands for ceaseless tears. According to the CC (Antya 20), Mahaprabhu utters this shloka in the mood of Sri Radha's separation from Krishna.
[^06-72]: The print has ମା instead of *mām* "me" and ମଦର୍ଶନାନ୍… instead of *adarśanān…* "by not showing Himself" (the first sign is ମ, not ଅ); probably misprints — the translation follows the usual text of the shloka. *Pāda-ratām* is feminine: the speaker is a devoted gopi. According to the CC (Antya 20), Mahaprabhu explains this shloka in the mood of Sri Radha: whatever Krishna does, He is the Lord of Her life.
[^06-73]: *Khaṭāa* is the imperative of *khaṭāibā* "to set to work, make serve": "employ [me] at Your feet," i.e. make me Your maidservant. In 6.Sh8a–d the Lord is addressed with the familiar "thou" (*to, tu*); the translation has "You."
[^06-74]: The second half (printed ≈ ଯାପନ ଗୃହି ଉନ୍ମଥଳ) is not understood. Possible meanings of the words: *yāpana* "spending [time]," *gṛhi* "in a house / householder," *unmatha-* "agitating, maddened"; from the sense of the Sanskrit shloka (*yathā tathā vā vidadhātu lampaṭaḥ* "let the rake do as He likes") one might expect "[You] spend Your time in others' houses" — but the printed text does not yield such a reading. *Mata* in the first half is taken as *matta* "intoxicated."
[^06-75]: The first word is printed ≈ ରଧାର (the ‑ā after ର is not visible); read as ରାଧାର "Radha's" (the OCR reads the same). *Bhāba* is printed ଭବ (the ଭା ligature). The couplet closes the "Eight Shlokas": the Swami (Jagannatha Dasa) expressed in them the bhava of Radha; cf. 6.75, where the same shlokas are "the bhavas of Prabhu." For Gaudiya Vaishnavas there is no contradiction here: according to the CC (Antya 20), Mahaprabhu, Krishna Himself in the mood and radiance of Radha, Himself composed the *Shikshashtaka* and relished it in Radha's bhava; the Swami's authorship is an Odia tradition (cf. the note to 6.Sh1).

---

# Madhava Pattanayak. *Vaishnava-lilamrita*, Chapter 7
## The Swami Takes a Mantra from Balarama Dasa; Kashi Mishra's Anger


*Chapter Seven*

**7.1.** Listen, O Vaishnavas, to [this] account — all sins will be destroyed.  
**7.2.** The Swami said: "I shall take a mantra and worship Krishna on the path of raga;[^07-1]  
**7.3.** I shall give up (?) the shastras (??) [and] the Puranas — what need have I of them now?"[^07-2]  
**7.4.** Prabhu said: "Take diksha — go to Balarama.[^07-3]  
**7.5.** Balarama's word comes true — take the mantra from him.[^07-4]  
**7.6.** Apart from him there is no other guru: [his] devotee's bhava is the greatest."  
**7.7.** The Swami took mantra-diksha and bowed down to Balarama.  
**7.8.** Kashi Mishra, hearing this, became very angry with the Swami:[^07-5]  
**7.9.** "You, a brahmana, receive (?) mantra-diksha from the mouth of a shudra!  
**7.10.** There is no … (??) in this — it is the misconduct (?) of the Kali age."[^07-6]  
**7.11.** The Swami said: "Sin does not cling to a Vaishnava even a particle.  
**7.12.** Be it a shudra or a brahmana — all human beings are equal.[^07-7]  
**7.13.** If a jewel lies in a low place, to take it is dharma.[^07-8]  
**7.14.** This is firm in my heart: [my] steadfast (?) devotion (?) is to Balarama.[^07-9]  
**7.15.** Why then do you reject (?) [me] on this account? We worship on the path of raga (?)."[^07-10]  
**7.16.** Hearing this, Sarvabhauma and also … (??) Lakshmidhara[^07-11]  
**7.17.** said: "Say nothing [more]: Jagannatha has a siddha's body."[^07-12]  
**7.18.** They brought Kashi Mishra to understanding and increased [his] reverence for the Swami.  

## The Govardhana Stone for Raghunatha Dasa; the Panchasakha and the Path of Raga; the War with Krishnadevaraya and the Famine


**7.19.** A few days after this, Prabhu, to Raghunatha Dasa —[^07-13]  
**7.20.** calling [him], says: "Take this; keep (?) this stone from Govardhana.[^07-14]  
**7.21.** This stone is a shalagrama; do not regard it as ordinary (?)."[^07-15]  
**7.22.** Sarvabhauma and Lakshmidhara heard [of it] — and in vexation (??) they kept silent.[^07-16]  
**7.23.** A shudra obtained the right to worship the shalagrama stone.[^07-17]  
**7.24.** "Is a shudra to be called a brahmana? What could be (??) beyond this?"[^07-18]  
**7.25.** An unheard-of thing had happened — Kashi Mishra became angry.[^07-19]  
**7.26.** Achyuta, Ananta, Yashovanta, Balarama and Jagannatha,[^07-20]  
**7.27.** together with Prabhu, tasted rasa every day; disregarding the shastras (?) and the Puranas,[^07-21]  
**7.28.** they became intoxicated with sankirtana and abandoned the path of rules (vaidhi).[^07-22]  
**7.29.** All the rasa of Krishna's lila — up to the bhava of the gopis —[^07-23]  
**7.30.** they ponder constantly in their minds, accepting the path of raga as [the highest] authority.[^07-24]  
**7.31.** Meanwhile, hear briefly a particular account of the Gajapati.[^07-25]  
**7.32.** He had a war with Krishnadeva Raya, and more than once.  
**7.33.** The Gajapati's might and valour (?) Krishnaraya brought to nothing (?).[^07-26]  
**7.34.** Famine came — a torment: "Save us, O Breaker of affliction!"[^07-27]  
**7.35.** The crops and the harvest (?) were ruined: without water everything perished.[^07-28]  
**7.36.** The subjects cried out in distress: "Save us, O Lord Jagannatha!"[^07-29]  

## Famine and the Panchasakha's Kirtana; the Siege and Fall of Kondavidu


**7.37.** Without water the crops perished; … (??)[^07-30]  
**7.38.** So it went year after year — floods, droughts, every kind of calamity.  
**7.39.** The flood would come and wash everything away; the drought destroyed the crops.  
**7.40.** From this calamity of floods and droughts the subjects raised a cry of distress.  
**7.41.** Balarama and Jagannatha, Achyuta, Ananta, Yashovanta[^07-31]  
**7.42.** performed kirtana from village to village, spreading (?) the name of Krishna.[^07-32]  
**7.43.** In this way all the Gajapati's forces broke up (?).[^07-33]  
**7.44.** From Udayagiri [and] Kondukur (?) the troops, defeated in battle,[^07-34]  
**7.45.** gathered in the fort of Kondavidu (?): "We shall hold this fort well!"  
**7.46.** Rudradeva, the jewel among kings, took counsel (?) with the five (?)[^07-35]  
**7.47.** and stationed Bhadradeva [there]. "Defend this fort," he said.[^07-36]  
**7.48.** "When there is a hero equal to you, how could any misfortune occur?"  
**7.49.** He summoned the five (?) samantas: "Defend this fort!" he said.[^07-37]  
**7.50.** Jivadeva, the mighty (?), stayed in that place without leaving it.[^07-38]  
**7.51.** "We shall kill — or else we shall die; other than this we shall do nothing."[^07-39]  
**7.52.** Valiant Bhadradeva said: "Do not even think of anything else."[^07-40]  
**7.53.** Krishnaraya's troops appeared and besieged [the fort] on all four sides.[^07-41]  
**7.54.** Bhadradeva was there with ten (?) thousand soldiers.[^07-42]  
**7.55.** Within one month the soldiers there were dying of hunger.  
**7.56.** They cried out for water when the eatables had run out.[^07-43]  
**7.57.** From inside the fort the cry was heard: "Save us, O Jagannatha!"  
**7.58.** After a month the enemy (?) entered the fort.[^07-44]  
**7.59.** They bound Bhadradeva and cut down Jivadeva.  
**7.60.** And those five (?) samantas — they took them all (?) away with them.[^07-45]  

## Virabhadra a Captive of Krishnadevaraya: the Duel with a Common Soldier and the Prince's End; the Gajapati's Peace with Krishnadevaraya


**7.61.** Bhadradeva was taken prisoner and remained (?) at the border of Benuru.[^07-46]  
**7.62.** Once (?) Krishnaraya came [and] addressed (?) Bhadradeva.[^07-47]  
**7.63.** He said: "Son of the Gajapati, you truly know the art of the sword.  
**7.64.** I have heard of your fame; when we see a bout, then we shall know, shall we not?"  
**7.65.** He called a soldier (?): "Now fence with this one."[^07-48]  
**7.66.** In Virabhadra's hand is a sword; it flashes (??), [he himself] is fierce.[^07-49]  
**7.67.** [Virabhadra] challenged Krishna Raya: "You fight with me," he said.  
**7.68.** At this the Raya refused: "I long [to see] your bout."[^07-50]  
**7.69.** Then the soldier (?) stepped forward: "This one (?) will fence [with you]."[^07-51]  
**7.70.** Bhadradeva says: "I am of royal blood; my contest (?) is with a king.[^07-52]  
**7.71.** Against another this sword is not matched (?). You [yourself] shall fight — come out (?), and well and good!"[^07-53]  
**7.72.** The Raya said, seeing [his] presumption (??): "To test (?) your skill [against me] — that shall not be (??).[^07-54]  
**7.73.** Fight with this soldier; if you disobey, we shall punish you."  
**7.74.** The soldier came and stood up [before him]; in his hand a sword gleams.[^07-55]  
**7.75.** [Bhadradeva thought:] "He is no king's son; there is no royal blood in him."[^07-56]  
**7.76.** Bhadradeva, his whole body shaking, trembling with anger,  
**7.77.** drew the sword from its sheath and thrusts [it] into his own chest (?).[^07-57]  
**7.78.** He fell to the ground with froth at the throat (??); Krishnaraya closes his eyes.[^07-58]  
**7.79.** He said: "What a deed he has done! A worthy offspring of a king is this!"  
**7.80.** Lifting Bhadradeva, [he] exalted him with royal honours.  
**7.81.** He said: "Blessed is Virabhadra, blessed are your father and mother!  
**7.82.** You did not bear a slighting word, and you kept the Gajapati's honour."[^07-59]  
**7.83.** Having said this, he grieved greatly and sent word to the Gajapati.  
**7.84.** Champadevi, the chief queen, is the Raya's captive.[^07-60]  
**7.85.** The Gajapati was troubled [and] meditated on the Lord in his heart:[^07-61]  
**7.86.** "Save us, O Lord Jagannatha! You are all the aims of human life."[^07-62]  
**7.87.** The Raya sent word: "Virabhadra has perished."  
**7.88.** Great anxiety for the Gajapati: how could he get out of this?  
**7.89.** The Raya says: "Give your daughter — and set the chief queen free."  
**7.90.** On that basis he made peace, giving [in marriage] his daughter Jagamohini.[^07-63]  
**7.91.** The lands on the southern side of the Krishna he gave as dowry.[^07-64]  
**7.92.** [The Gajapati] returned to the Kshetra; then (?) he had darshan [of the Lord].[^07-65]  

## The King Seeks Solace from the Panchasakha and Prabhu; the Dance at the Ratha-yatra and Prabhu's Mahabhava


**7.93.** [The king] summoned Swami Balarama Dasa and brought [him] to his side.[^07-66]  
**7.94.** He said: "Tell of the devotees. Call Prabhu here.  
**7.95.** Perform kirtana near me — for the sake of my devotion (?) and faith.[^07-67]  
**7.96.** Let me hear the name of Krishna; remove the burning heat from my heart."  
**7.97.** Laying his head at the Swami's feet, laying his head at Prabhu's feet,  
**7.98.** to the Swami he says, "Give [me] the name"; to Prabhu he says, "Instruct [me]" (??).[^07-68]  
**7.99.** "Tell all the pastimes of Krishna; remove the torment from my heart.  
**7.100.** Seeing Your kirtana, dance and rasa, I shall be filled with joy (?)."[^07-69]  
**7.101.** The Friend of devotees (??) heard [him] and consoled [his] heart in many ways.[^07-70]  
**7.102.** Balarama, Achyuta, Ananta, Yashovanta and Jagannatha —[^07-71]  
**7.103.** in their midst Chaitanya stays and dances in the kirtana.  
**7.104.** The time of the chariot came: He danced in front of the chariot.[^07-72]  
**7.105.** Who can describe that dance of Prabhu? He is mahabhava, He is Rasaraja.[^07-73]  
**7.106.** In a moment He grows, [in a moment] shrinks (?), in a moment becomes invisible.[^07-74]  
**7.107.** [There appear] all the traits of Radha's nature: in a moment, tears in His eyes.  
**7.108.** Seeing the unrestrained dance in bhava, the jewel among kings was filled with love.  
**7.109.** He said: "This is no human being — this is Radha's bhava in person."[^07-75]  
**7.110.** Rama Raya confirmed: "Truly, this is no human being."[^07-76]  
**7.111.** The divine bhava takes possession [of Him] and becomes manifest in the delight of the dance.[^07-77]  
**7.112.** "This is Prabhu!" — so [the king] proclaimed (?) in the crowd of people at the Ratha-yatra.[^07-78]  
**7.113.** Hearing this, all rejoiced: "Chaitanya is Prabhu, this is certain (?)."[^07-79]  
**7.114.** However many mahabhavas Prabhu has — who could describe them?  
**7.115.** Sometimes He remains in mahabhava — in the Gambhira, for a moment (?).[^07-80]  
**7.116.** He rises, sits down, rolls [on the ground], crying out, "O Krishna!"  
**7.117.** Tears stream from His eyes — He is full of love for Krishna.  
**7.118.** This torment of separation from Krishna became manifest in Prabhu's body.[^07-81]  
**7.119.** He gives up the habit of counting the names in japa on a waist-cord.[^07-82]  
**7.120.** In frenzy He passes (?) the day; like a child (?) He shows [His] bhava.[^07-83]  
**7.121.** Sometimes He runs to the temple — "Where is my Krishna?" — to see [Him].[^07-84]  
**7.122.** Crying "O Krishna!", He weeps aloud, and sometimes He reviles His own form.[^07-85]  
**7.123.** Sometimes He runs to the sea to see the form of Krishna.  
**7.124.** Seeing a cloud rise in the sky, He is overcome with agitation.[^07-86]  
**7.125.** Sometimes He [is] at Tota-Gopinatha, and sometimes He sits in the Kunja-matha.[^07-87]  

## Prabhu's Mahabhava in Nilachala; the Swami Knows His Bhava; the Panchasakha with Prabhu; the Author's Signature


**7.126.** And at times He goes to Gundicha (?) and, sitting in the mandapa, [becomes absorbed] in contemplation.[^07-88]  
**7.127.** In solitude, meditating on Krishna's form, He ponders, relishing bhava.  
**7.128.** Again and again He faints, and at times the hairs of His body stand on end.[^07-89]  
**7.129.** And at times He rolls in the dust, smearing His whole body with dust.  
**7.130.** He runs to the Narendra tank, and at times plunges into the Markandeya [tank].[^07-90]  
**7.131.** Intoxicated with the rasa of kirtana, He dances wildly (?).[^07-91]  
**7.132.** He sways back and forth, and with loud cries makes leaps.  
**7.133.** And at times He abides in [His ordinary] nature (?) and converses joyfully.[^07-92]  
**7.134.** He is solely intoxicated (?) with the joy of dance, full of love for the rasa of kirtana.[^07-93]  
**7.135.** His mind does not [rest] on sleep or food — know [this].  
**7.136.** The Swami brings a plate of food and makes [Him] eat by force (?).[^07-94]  
**7.137.** Beside Svarupa Damodara He reveals mahabhava.[^07-95]  
**7.138.** Seeing [this], people are astonished; do (?) they understand Prabhu's state?[^07-96]  
**7.139.** The treasure of prema of Nilachala — know: this is what is called "Prabhu."[^07-97]  
**7.140.** Manifestly [He] is the embodiment of prema; He is the utmost limit of the rasa of prema.[^07-98]  
**7.141.** No one knows His bhava — this nature [of His] the Swami knows.[^07-99]  
**7.142.** [The Swami] says: "Prabhu, be calm; give Your mind to the rasa of kirtana.  
**7.143.** In kirtana is the embodiment of Your bhava; how can people understand (??) [it]?"[^07-100]  
**7.144.** Balarama and Jagannatha, Ananta, Achyuta, Yashovanta —[^07-101]  
**7.145.** these five, gathering beside Prabhu, go about intoxicated with the rasa of kirtana.  
**7.146.** Prabhu said: "So be it (?)! Stay always with Me."[^07-102]  
**7.147.** Prabhu manifests bhava — and calls it a human lila.[^07-103]  
**7.148.** So says Madhava-dasa, placing his hope in Prabhu's feet.[^07-104]  

[^07-1]: The Swami is Jagannatha Dasa (cf. Ch. 6). In 6.63 the Gaudiyas reproached him as "uninstructed" (*aṇa-upadeśiā*); now he resolves to take a mantra. "The path of raga" (raga-marga) is worship of Krishna moved by spontaneous love. According to the CC (Madhya 22), raganuga-bhakti is following the mood of the eternal residents of Vraja; Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, stress that it is practised under the guidance of the guru and does not cancel respect for the shastras. Jagannatha Dasa and this episode are not mentioned in the CC/CB.
[^07-2]: The second word (printed ≈ ସାହାସ୍ର, likewise in 7.27) is not identified: perhaps *sahasra* "thousand" — "the thousands [of verses] of the Puranas," or a corruption of *śāstra* — "the shastras and Puranas"; as in 7.27, the translation adopts the latter. The sense of the verse is that the Swami renounces the bookish study of the Puranas for worship on the path of raga. The first word is printed ≈ ଛୁଡ଼ବ; read as ଛାଡ଼ିବ "I shall give up." *Etheṭi* — "here, in this," with the particle ‑*ṭi* (cf. 7.10, 7.11, 7.13).
[^07-3]: Balarama is Balarama Dasa, one of the Panchasakha (cf. 1.76, 5.126–131). In Madhava, Prabhu Himself sends Jagannatha Dasa to take diksha from Balarama Dasa; Kashi Mishra calls Balarama a shudra (7.9), i.e. he is not a brahmana by birth. This is an Odia tradition; the Panchasakha are not mentioned in the CC/CB.
[^07-4]: *Bacana-siddha* — "one whose word comes true," perfected in speech. *Tā tahu̐* — "from him"; in 7.6, "apart from him."
[^07-5]: Kashi Mishra is the brahmana in whose house in Puri Mahaprabhu lived (CC, Madhya 10). The words of 7.9–10 are his reproach to the Swami (with the familiar "thou").
[^07-6]: The third word of 7.10a (printed ≈ ଅ + a sign like େ + ନ + a blotted ସ/ବ + ର) is not identified; from the sense one expects "propriety, right conduct": "there is nothing proper in this." *Ethuṭi* — "in this," with the particle ‑*ṭi*. The last word is printed ≈ ଦୂରବୁର; read as ଦୁରାଚାର "misconduct."
[^07-7]: Cf. Mahaprabhu's words in the CC (Madhya 8.128): whether a brahmana, a sannyasi or a shudra — whoever knows the science of Krishna is a guru. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, accept a Vaishnava of any birth as guru and as brahmana by his qualities and devotion, not by birth. In Madhava the Swami puts the thought more broadly: "all human beings are equal."
[^07-8]: A saying close to the *Manu-smriti* (Ch. 2): good knowledge may be accepted even from a lower person, a jewel even from an impure place. *Karibāṭi* — infinitive with the particle ‑*ṭi*.
[^07-9]: The first words of 7.14b (printed ≈ ନ/ଲ with a superscript sign + ଷ with a subscript sign | ଭ + a sign like ଲ୍ଲ) are read as ନିଷ୍ଠ ଭକ୍ତି "steadfast devotion" — uncertain. *Dṛṛhe* (printed ≈ ଦୃ + େ + ଢ଼) — "firmly."
[^07-10]: *Kipā̃* "why" is printed ≈ କପାଁ (cf. 6.Sh4b). The last word of 7.15a (printed ≈ ତ + େ + ଜ with a small mark below) is read as ତେଜୁ "you reject" (pres. 2 sg.), which gives a rhyme with *bhaju*. The first word of 7.15b (printed ≈ ର + a sign like ଙ୍ଗ) is read as ରାଗ "raga" after 7.2.
[^07-11]: Sarvabhauma is Vasudeva Sarvabhauma, the court pandit of Prataparudra (CC, Madhya 6). Lakshmidhara is not identified by us; he does not appear in the CC/CB. The word before his name (printed ≈ େ + ମ + ର + ଦ୍ଧ/ଢ) is not read — perhaps a byname or surname.
[^07-12]: "Jagannatha" here is apparently Jagannatha Dasa: he has the body of a siddha, a perfected one — and so worldly rules of caste do not apply to him. The reading "[the Swami] is a perfected body of Jagannatha" is also possible.
[^07-13]: Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami came from a wealthy kayastha family of Saptagrama; having renounced the world, he lived for many years near Mahaprabhu in Puri in the care of Svarupa Damodara (CC, Antya 6). According to the CC (Antya 6), Mahaprabhu gave him a stone from Govardhana and a garland of gunja beads, which He Himself had kept for three years, and told him to worship the stone in the mode of goodness — with water and tulasi leaves — explaining that this stone is Krishna Himself. Madhava calls the stone a "shalagrama" (7.21) and Raghunatha a "shudra" (7.23). The first word of the verse is printed ≈ ଚ/ତ + ହୁଁ; read as ତହୁଁ "after this"; *kichudina* is printed ≈ କଛୁଦନ (both ‑i lost).
[^07-14]: The last word (printed ≈ ସ + ମ with subscript ଭ + ା + ଅ) is read as ସମ୍ଭାଅ and understood as an imperative "keep, look after" (cf. ସମ୍ଭାଳିବା "to keep, manage") — a guess; the rhyme *nia / sambhāa* supports it. Instead of *ethu nia* "take this (from here)" a reading *bege nia* "take it quickly" is known.
[^07-15]: The second word of 7.21b (printed ≈ ଇ + an unclear sign like ତ/େ + ର; OCR: ଇତର) is read as ଇତର "ordinary, common"; *na maṇa* — "do not regard" (from *maṇibā*). The rhyme *śāḷagrāma / maṇa* is imperfect. Instead of *ehuṭi* "this [indeed is]" a reading *e aṭe* "this is" is known. On the Govardhana stone see the note to 7.19.
[^07-16]: Sarvabhauma and Lakshmidhara — cf. 7.16 and the note to it. The first word of 7.22b (printed ≈ ବ with an inner hook, i.e. ବି, cf. ବିଶେଷ 7.31, + ସ with a subscript sign + େର) is read as ବିସୁରେ "in grief, in vexation" (cf. *bisuribā* "to lament", 6.59) — very uncertain; the sense of the line is that they did not object but kept silent. In the CC Sarvabhauma is a devotee of Mahaprabhu (Madhya 6); this episode is not in the CC/CB.
[^07-17]: By smarta norms the worship of the shalagrama is open only to brahmanas; hence the indignation of 7.24–25. The *Hari-bhakti-vilasa* admits pious shudras (sat-shudras) to shalagrama worship as well. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, hold that a Vaishnava who has received proper initiation is entitled to worship the shalagrama whatever his birth (cf. CC, Madhya 8: "whether a brahmana, a sannyasi or a shudra — whoever knows the science of Krishna is a guru").
[^07-18]: The verse is taken as the indignation of the orthodox (cf. 7.9 and 7.25: Kashi Mishra's anger); the speaker is not indicated. The last word (printed ≈ ସ + ମ with subscript ଭ + ା + ଇ; OCR: ସ୍ତମ୍ଧାଇ) is understood as a contraction of ସମ୍ଭବଇ "becomes possible": "what could be beyond this?" It may be a form of the same verb as ସମ୍ଭାଅ in 7.20 ("who else could cope with this?"). The rhyme *bolaï / sambhāi* supports the ending ‑i.
[^07-19]: *Nāhi̐ nohibā kathā* — lit. "a thing that ought never to be", i.e. something unheard-of. Kashi Mishra — cf. 7.8–9, where he is angry with Svami for taking initiation from a shudra. In the CC Kashi Mishra is a devoted servant of Mahaprabhu, in whose house the Lord lived in Puri (Madhya 10); his anger at Mahaprabhu is not in the CC/CB.
[^07-20]: The Panchasakha ("five friends") are Odia Vaishnava poets: Achyutananda, Ananta, Yashovanta, Balarama Dasa and Jagannatha Dasa (in Madhava usually "Svami"). They are not mentioned in the CC/CB.
[^07-21]: *Prabhura tule* — "together with Prabhu" (cf. 6.73). The word ସାହାସ୍ର (the same as in 7.3) is read as a corrupt *śāstra* "the shastras"; *sahasra* "a thousand" ("thousands [of verses] of the Puranas") is also possible, see the note to 7.3. *Upekṣi* — "disregarding". Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, do not set raganuga-bhakti against the shastras: Rupa Gosvami's *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu* (1.2.101) cites a verse that devotion to Hari without reliance on shruti, smriti, the Puranas and the Pancharatra leads only to disturbance.
[^07-22]: *Mata hele* — "became intoxicated, mad [with ecstasy]" (*mata* = *matta*). Vaidhi-marga is the path of sadhana-bhakti impelled by the injunctions of the shastras; raga-marga (raganuga) is the path impelled by spontaneous attraction, following the mood of the residents of Vraja. According to the CC (Madhya 22), these are the two kinds of sadhana-bhakti; for Gaudiya Vaishnavas raganuga does not mean giving up the rules of sadhana. The words "abandoned the path of rules" are Madhava's Odia interpretation.
[^07-23]: *Pariyante* "up to" (printed ≈ ପଶଯନ୍ତେ, "ri" as ଶ). Worship of Krishna in the gopis' bhava as the summit of devotion is the theme of Ramananda Raya's conversation with Mahaprabhu (CC, Madhya 8).
[^07-24]: *Mane huanti sadā guṇi* — lit. "in the mind they remain, always pondering"; *huanti* is printed ≈ ହୁଅନ୍ତ (‑i lost), *guṇi* (OCR: ଗୁଝି) is the converb of *guṇibā* "to ponder". *Paramāṇi* (= *pramāṇi*) — "accepting as the measure, as authoritative".
[^07-25]: The Gajapati is Prataparudra (ascended the throne ≈1497). Krishnadeva Raya was king of Vijayanagara (cf. the note to 4.8); in the 1510s he waged wars with the Gajapati over the southern fortresses of Orissa (Udayagiri, Kondavidu and others), which ended in peace and Krishnadevaraya's marriage to Prataparudra's daughter. This war is not described in the CC/CB. *Lesa* = *leśa* "a little, briefly".
[^07-26]: The last word of 7.33a (printed ≈ ବ/କ + ଳ + a sign with a vertical stroke (ବୀ?) + ଯ with reph) is read as ବଳବୀର୍ଯ୍ୟ "might and valour"; the rhyme with *kṛṣṇarāẏa* supports this. The first word of 7.33b (printed ≈ ନା/ବା + ହ with a superscript sign) is read as ନାହିଁ: *nāhi̐ kalaka* — "made non-existent, brought to nothing". *Kalaka* is an archaic past form in ‑*ka* (cf. *helaka* in 7.34).
[^07-27]: *Kaṣaṇa* — "torment, calamity". *Ārata-bhañjana* "Breaker of affliction (of suffering)" is an epithet of the Lord; this is apparently the people's prayer (cf. 7.36).
[^07-28]: The second word (printed ≈ ଫ + ସ with a subscript sign + ା/ଲ) is read as ଫସଲ "harvest"; it is set the same way in the edition's notes. *Jaḷa bihune* — "without water" (drought); *bihune* is printed ≈ ବ with a hook (= ବି) + ହୁ + େନ. Verses 7.34–36 speak of a famine during the war.
[^07-29]: *Prabhu jagannāthe* — an address; the ending ‑*e* is for the rhyme with *ārate*.
[^07-30]: *Jaḷa bihune* — "without water," as in 7.35. The second half of the couplet has not been read. The first word is printed ≈ ମୁଖୁ ("from the mouth"?), the second ≈ ଖାଇଲ + a sign like ଭ/ଉ, the third ≈ ସ/ଯ/ଜ + ଢ଼ାଇଲ. Possible guesses: "…what was [already] in the mouth it made rot (?)" or "mice (ମୂଷା?) ate…". From the context it apparently speaks of further ruin of the harvest or of food.
[^07-31]: The Panchasakha ("five friends"): Balarama Dasa, Jagannatha Dasa, Achyuta (Achyutananda), Ananta and Yashovanta (cf. the note to 7.26). They are not mentioned in the CC/CB.
[^07-32]: *Pracuraṇe* (printed ≈ ପ୍ର + ଚ with a subscript sign + ରଣେ) is taken as "spreading" (cf. *pracura*, *pracāra*). For Gaudiya Vaishnavas, congregational chanting of the holy name, sankirtana, is the yuga-dharma of the Kali age established by Sri Chaitanya (CC, Adi 3). Mahaprabhu Himself is not mentioned in these verses.
[^07-33]: The first word of 7.43b is set with the ଭା ligature + a sign like ଜି. The word ଭାଙ୍ଗିବାକୁ is set the same way in the notes on this page, so we read ଭାଙ୍ଗିଲେ "broke up, were shattered". ଭାଜିଲେ "fled" is also possible. The verse concerns Prataparudra's war with Krishnadeva Raya, king of Vijayanagara (cf. the note to 7.31).
[^07-34]: Udayagiri (cf. 4.7), Kondukur and Kondavidu are southern fortresses of the Gajapati's realm (now in Andhra Pradesh). According to historical sources, Krishnadeva Raya took them in the 1510s; Kondavidu fell about 1515. The name Kondukur is printed ≈ େକ + ଃ/ା + ଣ୍ଡ with a subscript sign + କୁରୁ: ‑*ru* is either part of the name or the ablative "from". The name Kondavidu is printed ≈ େକ + ା + ଣ୍ଡ + ା + ବି + ଡୁ.
[^07-35]: Rudradeva is King Prataparudra (ascended the throne ≈1497; cf. 4.6–8). The word ପାଞ୍ଚଙ୍କ "with the five" is read from a sign resembling ଞ୍ଚ; ପାତ୍ରଙ୍କ "with the ministers" is also possible (cf. 7.49, 7.60). *Bhāḷeṇi* is taken as "deliberation, counsel" (cf. ଭାଳିବା "to ponder").
[^07-36]: Bhadradeva is apparently Virabhadra, Prataparudra's son. According to historical sources, he was taken captive when Kondavidu fell. *Rakhāile* — "stationed [as guardian]", causative of *rakhibā* "to keep, defend". He does not appear in the CC/CB.
[^07-37]: Samantas are the Gajapati's vassal chiefs and commanders. "Five" (*pāñca*) is read from a sign resembling ଞ୍ଚ; *pātra sāmanta* "ministers and samantas" is also possible, a common pairing. *Ho* is a vocative particle ("O you!").
[^07-38]: We have not identified Jivadeva; he does not appear in the CC/CB. *Sāmarathā* is taken as "mighty, capable" (cf. Skt. *sāmarthya*); possibly an adverb, "with all his strength".
[^07-39]: The words of the fort's defenders (cf. 7.49–50); the speaker is not marked.
[^07-40]: *Ānaṭi* — "anything else", with the particle ‑*ṭi* (cf. 7.3). The imperative is singular: Bhadradeva is apparently answering the king (cf. 7.47–48).
[^07-41]: Krishnaraya is Krishnadeva Raya (reigned 1509–1529; cf. the notes to 4.8 and 7.31). *Hele* — lit. "became, came to be [there]".
[^07-42]: The first word is printed ≈ a blotted round sign + ଶ/ଣ; it is read as ଦଶ "ten". *Tule* — "together with" (cf. *prabhura tule* in 7.27).
[^07-43]: *Khāila drabya sarilare* — lit. "when the things to be eaten had run out" (*sarilare* printed ≈ ସଶଲରେ, 'ri' as ଶ). ଖାଇଲ may be a misprint for ଖାଇବା ("provisions").
[^07-44]: The first word of 7.58b is printed ≈ ଅ + ର/ବ + a round sign like ତ; it is read as ଅରାତି "the enemy", from the sense. *Prabeśiṇa* is an archaic past form in ‑*iṇa*.
[^07-45]: *Sabunta* (printed ≈ ସ + ବୁ + ନ୍ତ) is taken as "all [of them]" (from *sabu* "all"), with the ending ‑*nta* for the rhyme with *sāmanta*. *Sehu* (printed ≈ େ + ସ/ଯ + ହୁ) — "those same"; possibly ଯେହୁ "who". *Neleka* is an archaic past form in ‑*eka*.
[^07-46]: Bhadradeva is Virabhadra (so named in 7.66, 7.81, 7.87), son of the Gajapati Prataparudra, who defended the fortress of Kondavidu (cf. 7.44–60). Krishnadevaraya's capture of Kondavidu and of the prince is dated ≈1515. The place "Benuru" (printed ବେନୁରୁ) has not been identified; from the context, a border region of the Vijayanagara realm. *Rahile* "remained" is printed ≈ ରହଲେ (‑i lost, as in ବାନ୍ଧଲେ 7.59). This episode is not described in the CC/CB.
[^07-47]: The first sign of the verse is ink-filled (≈ କ/ଭ); read as କାଳେ "once, at [a certain] time" (OCR: କାଲେ). The last word is printed ≈ ସମ୍ଭା + a sign like the "ରି" of ହକାରିଲ (7.65) + ଲ, i.e. ସମ୍ଭାରିଲ; we suggest ସମ୍ଭାଷିଲ "addressed, greeted" (ସମ୍ଭାଳିଲ "took into his care" is also possible). *Bhadradebaṅkaï* is an archaic object form in ‑ଙ୍କଇ.
[^07-48]: The word "soldier" (7.65, 7.69, 7.73) is printed ≈ ସ + ଇ + a sign like ମ or like ନ with an attached ା (OCR: ସଚ, ସରମ, ସଇମା); in 7.74 it is ସଇନଏ. Read as ସଇନା *saïnā* (= ସୈନ୍ୟ "soldier, troops"), as in 7.53–54; the rhyme with *puṇi* is imperfect. *E tule* — "with this one" (*tule* "together with", cf. 7.27).
[^07-49]: The first word of 7.66b is printed ≈ a curl with a tail (unlike the ଖ of ଖଣ୍ଡା in the same line) + େ + ଲ + କ; read as ଝଲେକ (= ଝଲକ "gleam, flash") — a guess. Possibly ଢାଲେକ "a shield" (ଢାଲ + ଏକ), but no ା is visible. *Pracaṇḍā* "fierce" has ‑ā for the rhyme with *khaṇḍā*.
[^07-50]: *Nāsti kala* — "said no", i.e. he refused to fight in person. *To kheḷe mora tṛṣṇā hela* — lit. "a thirst for your play has arisen in me", i.e. the Raya wants to watch the captive prince's skill (cf. 7.62–64).
[^07-51]: The first sign of ଏହୁ "this one" is printed ≈ "<" (apparently a damaged ଏ). The words of 7.69b seem to be spoken by the Raya, pointing at the soldier; the speaker is not indicated.
[^07-52]: The last word (printed ≈ ତ/ଚ + ର + ଜା; OCR: କରଜା) is read as ତରଜା and taken as "contest, duel" (cf. Beng. *tarajā* "verbal duel") — a guess. The sense is clear from the context: the prince agrees to fight only an equal — the king himself.
[^07-53]: The last word of 7.71a (printed ≈ the second form of ତୁ, like ବୂ, + େ + ଲ; OCR: ବୁଲେ) is read as ତୁଲେ "is matched, measures up" (from *tuḷibā* "to weigh"). In 7.71b *kheḷibu* is printed ≈ ଖେଳବୁ, *tuhi* ≈ ତୁହ; ଅସ without ା is read as ଆସ "come out". The syntax of the half-line is uncertain.
[^07-54]: The verse is unclear. *Bānā* (ବାନା) means "banner, flag", here perhaps "presumption, bravado"; *paramāṇi* — "having verified, tested" (cf. 7.30); *mānā* is taken as "forbidden, not allowed" (cf. Hindi *manā*); *māna* "honour" or an imperative "obey" are also possible. Translated according to the neighbouring verses: the Raya refuses to fight in person and orders the prince to fight the soldier (7.73).
[^07-55]: *Ubhārichi* — "stood up, rose" (from *ubhāribā*, cf. 4.50); in 7.80 *ubhāri* — "raising, exalting". The endings ‑ଛି in 7.74 and 7.78 are printed as ଛ with a lower loop, with no visible ‑i; the rhyme *gāji / muduchi* (7.78) confirms ‑i. *Śohuchi* = *śobhuchi* "gleams, is resplendent".
[^07-56]: Bhadradeva's thought (the speaker is not indicated): a duel with a common soldier would demean royal blood.
[^07-57]: The penultimate word is printed ≈ a sign like ନ/କ + ଷ (with a cross, as in କୋଷରୁ) + ରେ; read as ବକ୍ଷରେ "into the chest"; କକ୍ଷରେ "into the side" is also possible. A story that the captive son of the king of Orissa took his own life, insulted by the demand to fight a commoner, is also known in the Vijayanagara tradition (the Portuguese chronicle of Nuniz) — to be checked.
[^07-58]: The last word of 7.78a is printed ≈ ଗଲଗାଜି; taken as *gaḷa* "throat" + *gāji* "froth" ("with froth at the throat") — a guess; possibly *gala gāji* "went off frothing". *Paṛila* is printed ≈ ପଡ଼ଲ (‑i lost).
[^07-59]: *Sāmānya kathā* — lit. "a common word", i.e. the humiliation (the order to fight a common soldier). *Paṇa* — honour, vow, word of honour.
[^07-60]: Champadevi is the Gajapati's chief queen (pattarani), apparently Virabhadra's mother; she had been taken captive by Krishnadevaraya. *Bandinī* is printed ≈ ବନ୍ଦନୀ.
[^07-61]: Prabhu here is Lord Jagannatha (cf. 7.86). *Dhāile* = *dhyāile* "meditated, contemplated" (printed ଧାଇଲେ without subscript ୍ୟ).
[^07-62]: The purusharthas are the aims of human life: dharma, artha, kama and moksha. Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, call prema — love of God — the highest, "fifth" aim (CC, Adi 7).
[^07-63]: The peace between Prataparudra and Krishnadevaraya, sealed by Krishnadevaraya's marriage to the Gajapati's daughter, is dated ≈1518/19 (±1 year). Madhava calls the daughter Jagamohini (printed ଜଗମୋହିନୀ). *Sehu thāne* — lit. "in that place", i.e. "on that basis, then and there"; *sandhi* is printed ≈ ସନ୍ଧ. These events are not described in the CC/CB.
[^07-64]: The Krishna river. *Pāruśe* — "on the side" (*pāruśa* = *pārśva*; cf. ପାରୁଶ in 7.93). Under the treaty the lands south of the Krishna (the Kondavidu region) remained with Vijayanagara.
[^07-65]: The Kshetra is Sri Kshetra, Puri; the Gajapati is meant (honorific plural *aile*). The word ତେତେକେ "then" (printed ≈ େ + an ink-filled ତ/ଜ + େ + ତ + େ + କ) is a guess (cf. *eteke* "thereupon"); the darshan is apparently of Lord Jagannatha.
[^07-66]: In Madhava "the Swami" is usually Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 7.2). Here the word stands next to the name of Balarama Dasa (*swāmī se baḷarāma dāsa*): it is either apposition ("Swami Balarama Dasa") or a list of two — "the Swami [Jagannatha Dasa] and Balarama Dasa." Balarama Dasa is one of the Panchasakha; according to 7.4–5 he gave diksha to Jagannatha Dasa, so the request "give the name" (7.98) may be addressed to him. The subject is King Prataparudra (cf. 7.108). *Pāruśa* — "to his side, near" (= *pārśwa*). The Panchasakha are not mentioned in the CC/CB.
[^07-67]: The word is printed ≈ ଭ with a separate stroke above + କ + ତ: read as ଭକତ — "devotion" (*bhakati*, with ‑i lost) or "devotee" (*bhakata*). The sense of the half-line is unclear: perhaps "by faith in my devotees." *Karaho* — imperative plural with the particle ‑*ho*.
[^07-68]: "Give the name" — probably initiation into the holy name. The last word of 98b is printed ≈ a round sign (ସ/ଦ/ଚ) + ମ/ସ/ଯ + ଜ/କ + ା + ଅ. We read ସମଜାଅ, i.e. *samajhāa* "make [me] understand, instruct"; ଚମକାଅ "astonish [me]" is also possible.
[^07-69]: *Tora* — "your," the familiar "thou." These seem to be the king's words to Prabhu (cf. 7.98). The last word is printed ≈ ଉ + ଲ with a subscript sign + ା + ସ, with a stroke above; read as ଉଲ୍ଲାସ "joy, delight."
[^07-70]: The second word is printed ≈ a round sign with a filled lower half (like କ/ବ) + a sign like ନ/ର with a subscript resembling ଧ. We read ଭଗତ ବନ୍ଧୁ — "Friend of devotees," an epithet of the Lord (here perhaps Prabhu). "Devotee-friends," i.e. the Panchasakha, is also possible. The reading is not established.
[^07-71]: The Panchasakha — Balarama Dasa, Achyuta (Achyutananda), Ananta, Yashovanta and Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 7.41). Couplet 7.102 continues syntactically into 7.103. In the CC (Madhya 13) the kirtana at the Ratha-yatra is led by seven groups of Bengali devotees, and Mahaprabhu dances, moving from one to another. The participation of the Panchasakha is a tradition of the Odia school, not attested in the CC/CB (cf. the note to 5.11).
[^07-72]: "The time of the chariot" is the Ratha-yatra of Jagannatha in Puri. Mahaprabhu's dance before the chariot and King Prataparudra's astonishment are described in detail in CC, Madhya 13–14.
[^07-73]: For Gaudiya Vaishnavas, Sri Chaitanya is Rasaraja (Krishna, the king of rasa) and mahabhava (Sri Radha) in one form. This is how Mahaprabhu revealed Himself to Ramananda Raya (CC, Madhya 8).
[^07-74]: *Chuṛaï* (printed ≈ ଛୁଡ଼ଇ) is contrasted with *baṛhaï* "grows" and is therefore taken as "shrinks, contracts" (?). The CC describes both the lengthening of Mahaprabhu's limbs (Antya 14) and His contraction into a tortoise-like form (Antya 17). Below the line the edition gives another variant of 106a: "In a moment He weeps and rolls [on the ground]" (*kṣaṇake kāndi se loṭaï*).
[^07-75]: The speaker is the king (*nṛpamaṇi*, 7.108). For Gaudiya Vaishnavas, Mahaprabhu is Krishna Himself, having accepted the bhava and radiance of Radha (CC, Adi 4).
[^07-76]: Rama Raya is Ramananda Raya. In the CC (Madhya 11) Ramananda Raya and Sarvabhauma tell the king about Mahaprabhu and strengthen his faith.
[^07-77]: *Daibatā bhāba* — "divine bhava" or "the bhava of the Deity." *Ābeśaï* — "takes possession, enters" (cf. *ābeśa* "absorption, possession").
[^07-78]: Printed ≈ a sign like ଉ/ଜ + ଘୋଷିଲେ; read as *ughoṣile* = *udghoṣile* "proclaimed." In the CC (Madhya 12–14) the king sweeps the road before the chariot at the Ratha-yatra, and Mahaprabhu embraces him in the garden. A public proclamation is not described in the CC.
[^07-79]: *Biśeṣa* (printed ≈ ବଶେଷ) — "special, certain." The rendering "Chaitanya is a special Prabhu" is also possible.
[^07-80]: The Gambhira is a small room in Kashi Mishra's house in Puri, where Mahaprabhu spent His last years in deep feelings of separation (CC, Antya 14–20). The last word is printed ≈ ଲବେ: *labe* "for a moment" (*laba* "instant") or *labhe* "attains"?
[^07-81]: Vipralambha — the experience of separation from Krishna in the mood of Radha. Almost the entire Antya-lila of the CC depicts it.
[^07-82]: Literally, "japa of the name on the waist-cord — He gives up the habit of counting." Apparently this refers to counting repetitions of the holy name with knots on a cord. We have not found this detail in the CC.
[^07-83]: *Mata* = *matta* "intoxicated, in frenzy." *Nie* (printed ≈ a filled ନ + ଏ; ନି as in ନିଜ, 7.122) is taken as "passes [the day]." *Parāẏa* (printed ≈ ପ + ର/ରା + ୟ) is taken as *prāẏa* "like."
[^07-84]: The deula is the temple of Jagannatha. According to the CC, Mahaprabhu went daily for darshan of Jagannatha and saw Krishna in Him.
[^07-85]: Perhaps, in the mood of Radha longing for Krishna, He reproaches His own body. Cf. Mahaprabhu's words in separation in the CC (Antya 17–19).
[^07-86]: A cloud recalls the color of Krishna's body. Cf. the CC (Antya 14, 18): Mahaprabhu takes the Chataka hill for Govardhana and the sea for the Yamuna.
[^07-87]: Tota-Gopinatha is the Deity of Gopinatha in the garden (*ṭoṭā*) by the sea in Puri; Gadadhara Pandita, Madhava's teacher, served Him (CC, Antya 7). Printed ତୋଟା (usually ଟୋଟା). The Kunja-matha (*kuñjamaṭha*) has not been identified. The first word of 125a is printed ≈ େକ + a distorted େ + ବ; read as କେବେ "sometimes."
[^07-88]: Gundicha is the temple in Puri to which Jagannatha is drawn at the Ratha-yatra; the mandapa is the temple's open pavilion. Mahaprabhu's stay at Gundicha during the chariot festival is described in CC, Madhya 12–14. The last sign of *guṇḍicā* is printed ≈ ଚ with a subscript hook, with no visible ା; the reading ଗୁଣ୍ଡିଚା is restored from the rhyme with *bhābanā*.
[^07-89]: The first words are printed ≈ "କ୍ଷଣେ କ୍ଷଣ କେ" (with a gap) and read as *kṣaṇe kṣaṇake* "again and again, every moment" (cf. *kṣaṇake* 7.106). *Kāẏe* "in the body" is the locative of *kāẏa*. Fainting and hair standing on end are sattvika-bhavas, signs of ecstatic love (CC, Madhya 2; Antya 14).
[^07-90]: Narendra-sarovara and Markandeya-sarovara are sacred tanks of Puri. In the CC Mahaprabhu and the devotees perform water pastimes in Narendra-sarovara (Madhya 11 and 14 — to be checked); the bath in the Markandeya tank is a detail of Madhava's that apparently does not appear in the CC.
[^07-91]: *Matta* "intoxicated" is printed ମ + a sign like ଢ (likewise in 7.145); taken as the ligature ତ୍ତ (cf. ମତ in 7.120). The last word is printed ≈ ପ୍ରବରଳ (so also the OCR); taken as ପ୍ରବଳ "vehemently, wildly."
[^07-92]: *Prakṛtire* — lit. "in nature, in [one's own] state." Taken as "in the ordinary state," i.e. in external consciousness, when Mahaprabhu converses with the devotees (cf. the three states — internal, half-external and external, CC, Antya 14–20). Another understanding is possible: *prakṛti* as "feminine nature," i.e. the mood of a gopi. *Sukhare* "joyfully" (so also the OCR), not *mukhare* "with the mouth."
[^07-93]: The word after *ekā tā* is printed ≈ ପ/ସ + the same sign as in *matta* 7.131 (OCR: ସଭ); read as *matta* "intoxicated," although the first sign looks more like ପ/ସ than ମ.
[^07-94]: The Swami is Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 7.2). The printed ≈ ବଲକ୍କାର is taken as ବଳାତ୍କାର "by force." In the CC, Svarupa Damodara, Govinda and Jagadananda look after Mahaprabhu's meals in the years of deep separation (CC, Antya); Jagannatha Dasa is not mentioned in the CC/CB — this is Madhava's Odia tradition.
[^07-95]: Svarupa Damodara was Mahaprabhu's closest companion in Puri in the last years. According to the CC (Antya 14–20), it was to him and Ramananda Raya that the Lord disclosed His feelings of separation in the Gambhira.
[^07-96]: *Udanta* — "tidings, account, circumstances," here Prabhu's state. The verb is printed ≈ ବୁ + ଝ with a stroke above + ନ୍ତ (‑i not visible; likewise in 7.143); read as *bujhanti* "understand." An affirmative understanding is also possible: "people… grasp Prabhu's state."
[^07-97]: The syntax of 7.139b ("ପ୍ରଭୁ ଏ ବୋଲିବାଟି ଜାଣ") is taken as "know: this is what is called 'Prabhu'." *Bolibāṭi* is printed ≈ ବୋଲବାଟି (‑i lost). Cf. the proclamation of Chaitanya as "Prabhu" in 7.112.
[^07-98]: *Pratimā* (printed ≈ ପ୍ରତମା) here means the "embodiment, likeness" of prema, not a temple Deity.
[^07-99]: In Madhava, the Swami (Jagannatha Dasa) alone knows Prabhu's bhava, calms Him and addresses Him with the familiar "you" (*tora*, 7.143). According to the CC (Antya 14–20), it was Svarupa Damodara who understood Mahaprabhu's inner mood and consoled Him with songs and verses in tune with His feelings; Gaudiya Vaishnavas, following Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati, follow this account. That the Swami (and not Prabhu) is speaking is inferred from the sense (cf. 7.141b); the speaker of 7.142a is not marked.
[^07-100]: The word after *manuṣya* is printed ≈ ଭ without the stroke of the ligature ଭା + ର (OCR illegible). Read as *bhara* (??) — meaning unclear ("the fullness [of bhava]"?); possibly a misprint for ଭାବ "state." Translated by the general sense.
[^07-101]: The Panchasakha are Balarama Dasa, Jagannatha Dasa, Ananta, Achyuta (Achyutananda) and Yashovanta (cf. 7.102, 8.4). The couplet continues syntactically in 7.145. *Cāli* "go about, move" (7.145b) — the nuance is unclear.
[^07-102]: The word in 7.146a is printed ≈ ଅ + ସ with a subscript sign (ତ/ଥ) and ୁ (OCR: ଅସ୍ତ); read as *astu* "so be it." *Āmbha tule* — "with Us," honorific "with Me." To whom the singular *tu* "you" is addressed (with five companions in 7.145) is unclear — perhaps the Swami.
[^07-103]: The subject of *bolaï* "calls" is taken to be Prabhu: He manifests bhava but calls it a human lila. Cf. CC, Madhya 21: of all Krishna's pastimes the highest is *nara-lila*, the pastimes in human form; Mahaprabhu, concealing His divinity, acts as a devotee.
[^07-104]: The closing formula with the author's name (cf. 2.131). With this verse Chapter 7 ends: it is followed by an ornament, with no colophon "ଇତି… ସମ୍ପୂର୍ଣ୍ଣ"; Chapter 8 ("ଅଷ୍ଟମ ଅଧ୍ୟାୟ") begins on leaf 105.

---

# Madhava Pattanayak. *Vaishnava-lilamrita*, Chapter 8
## Nagara-kirtana at Dawn; the Child Who Fell from the Pinda, and the Mother's Anger


*Chapter Eight*

**8.1.** Listen attentively, O sadhus, to the description of the nagara-kirtana.[^08-1]  
**8.2.** The mridanga came to be called "khol" — this is how it came about (?).[^08-2]  
**8.3.** In the rasa of many kirtanas they go out into the town from dawn (?).[^08-3]  
**8.4.** Achyuta, Ananta, Yashovanta, Balarama and Jagannatha.[^08-4]  
**8.5.** With them are two mridanga players, surrounding (??) [them].[^08-5]  
**8.6.** Prabhu is the great dancer: in the rasa of kirtana He dances.  
**8.7.** The kirtana — a booming sound, the sounds of jhanj, mridanga and talas.[^08-6]  
**8.8.** At every door the children (?), hearing the sound of the kirtana, [wake up].[^08-7]  
**8.9.** And in every house the mothers set about (?) the morning chores.[^08-8]  
**8.10.** One child got up: "I will go and see the kirtana," he said.  
**8.11.** Getting up from his bed he runs headlong to see the kirtana at the door.  
**8.12.** He rolled (?) down from the pinda — from his head [poured] a heavy (?) stream of blood.[^08-9]  
**8.13.** The mother, busy with household work, runs [to him] wailing (??).[^08-10]  
**8.14.** She lifted the child into her arms and became angry with Chaitanya.[^08-11]  
**8.15.** All the child's filth — she ties (??) [it] in a rag of thin [cloth].[^08-12]  
**8.16.** Raising (?) [it] in her hand, she headed (?) for the street door.[^08-13]  
**8.17.** Reaching the threshold — [and there] the kirtana, a booming sound —  
**8.18.** the mother scolded at length and cried out in a loud voice.  
**8.19.** All the soiled rags she throws onto the kirtana.[^08-14]  

## How the mridanga came to be called khol; Prabhu and the Panchasakha travel with kirtana: Alarnath, Bhubaneswar, Niali, Kenduli, Chandrabhaga


**8.20.** [The mother], in disgust, rages and shouts without end.[^08-15]  
**8.21.** Prabhu caught up that rag and placed it on the mridanga.  
**8.22.** His body trembling with ecstatic thrill, He speaks in a faltering voice.  
**8.23.** He said: "We have obtained a treasure — the bliss of the rasa of kirtana!  
**8.24.** This is not a child's excrement" — He calls it real sandal paste.[^08-16]  
**8.25.** "Smear this sandal on your heads — [it is] the veneration of the rasa of kirtana."[^08-17]  
**8.26.** Gathering the soiled rags, He laid [them] on the mridanga  
**8.27.** and says: "From today it shall be so: the mridanga shall be covered with this.  
**8.28.** In memory (?) of the angered (?) mother, make the infant's rag a covering.[^08-18]  
**8.29.** If this covering is not on the mridanga, the kirtana does not go on."  
**8.30.** From that day [the mridanga] was covered with cloth, and the mridanga came to be called khol.[^08-19]  
**8.31.** Listen, good people, to your heart's delight, to the particulars of the rasa of kirtana.  
**8.32.** One day Prabhu summoned the Panchasakha and says: "Come!  
**8.33.** Do come, Swami, Balarama! Come, Achyuta and Ananta![^08-20]  
**8.34.** Yashovanta, you come too! Swami, sit beside Me.  
**8.35.** Let us perform abundant (?) kirtana and wander from town to town.[^08-21]  
**8.36.** On the days of anavasara we shall go to Alarnath."[^08-22]  
**8.37.** From the first lunar day [of the dark half] of Ashadha [they are away], and by the new moon they return.[^08-23]  
**8.38.** Kirtana at Alarnath; [there] they taste the khiri-bhoga.[^08-24]  
**8.39.** Rama Raya, delighted, honours the Vaishnavas in every way.[^08-25]  
**8.40.** Beside the murtis of the five Pandavas they perform kirtana without end.[^08-26]  
**8.41.** Thus every year they set out for Alarnath.  
**8.42.** On the full moon of Pausha they gather at Ekamra-kshetra.[^08-27]  
**8.43.** At Ananta-Vasudeva's they manifest the rasa of kirtana.[^08-28]  
**8.44.** Krittivasa is that Lingaraja who manifests the form of Harihara.[^08-29]  
**8.45.** Offering tulasi and bilva leaves, they worship Lingaraja there.  
**8.46.** Bathing in the Gauri-kunda (?), worship of Kedareshvara,[^08-30]  
**8.47.** the bhoga of Ananta-Vasudeva, [a bath] with love in the Bindusagara.[^08-31]  
**8.48.** Without end they perform kirtana together with the Vaishnava devotees.  
**8.49.** [Then] the journey to Madhava at Niali, bathing in the Prachi.[^08-32]  
**8.50.** Devotion (?) is offered to Madhava; they spread the rasa of kirtana.[^08-33]  
**8.51.** A multitude of people gathers — they celebrate the Makara-mela.[^08-34]  
**8.52.** In the rasa of dancing to the *Gita-Govinda*, they glorify Jayadeva.[^08-35]  
**8.53.** In the midst of the village of Kenduli they spread the rasa of kirtana.  
**8.54.** From there they go to the Chandrabhaga, where (??) [the river] falls into the sea at its mouth (?), lovely (??).[^08-36]  
**8.55.** Taking a dip [in the sea] on Chala-saptami (?), they perform kirtana, loudly proclaiming [the Name] (?).[^08-37]  
**8.56.** They worship Aditya-Vishnu and render service to the Sun.[^08-38]  
**8.57.** At the rising of the red sun Prabhu becomes intoxicated and forgets Himself.[^08-39]  

## Prabhu's yearly round of travels with the Panchasakha: Chandrabhaga, Kakatpur, the Dola in Cuttack, Jajpur — the home of Prabhu's ancestors, Tulasi-kshetra; the Panchasakha in kirtana


**8.58.** [Prabhu] manifests mahabhava and dances in many ways (?).[^08-40]  
**8.59.** Together with the Panchasakha, in the circle of devotees [absorbed] in kirtana,  
**8.60.** they taste the bhoga of khiri and laddus and perform the worship of Vishnu.[^08-41]  
**8.61.** On the way — worship of Mangala; Prabhu is seized by "Radha-bhrama" (?).[^08-42]  
**8.62.** By the road through the village of Kakatpur they arrive at the borders of the Kshetra.[^08-43]  
**8.63.** For the Dola they go to Cuttack, to the festival at the royal palace.[^08-44]  
**8.64.** The Gajapati Rudradeva shows honour to the Vaishnavas.[^08-45]  
**8.65.** The queens (?) greet [them] and perform the worship of the Vaishnavas.[^08-46]  
**8.66.** [The Vaishnavas] manifest the rasa of kirtana; seeing Prabhu's mahabhava,  
**8.67.** the king becomes beside himself and is immersed (?) in the rasa of Krishna.  
**8.68.** From there they set out for Viraja (?), [take] their bath in the Vaitarani,[^08-47]  
**8.69.** halt at the Dashashvamedha Ghat; the Vaishnavas perform kirtana.  
**8.70.** This is Prabhu's ancestral place; it is called Yajanagara (?).[^08-48]  
**8.71.** [Prabhu's] grandfather was named Upendra; [he was] of the Vatsasa, a samanta, a kulina.[^08-49]  
**8.72.** He bore the title of Mishra, a Vedic [brahmana], the holder of a sasana,  
**8.73.** a pandit in the six darsanas, versed in Nyaya and Vedanta.[^08-50]  
**8.74.** Maharaja Kapileshvara threatened [him] with many quarrels (?).[^08-51]  
**8.75.** Out of fear of the king he fled, went to Shrihatta and settled there.[^08-52]  
**8.76.** His son was Jagannatha, and from him Prabhu appeared (lit. "was born").  
**8.77.** When they came to Jajpur, in joy, in the circle of kirtana,  
**8.78.** [His kinsmen], learning who Prabhu was, bow down to His feet.[^08-53]  
**8.79.** Plastering doors and houses with cow-dung, they set out lamps and full pitchers.[^08-54]  
**8.80.** Lovingly (?) they honoured Prabhu, and He manifested mahabhava.[^08-55]  
**8.81.** After staying there some days (?), they go on again to Tulasi-kshetra.[^08-56]  
**8.82.** There they have darshan of Baladeva and partake of prasada.  
**8.83.** By the time of the bathing of the Deities they return to the Kshetra.[^08-57]  
**8.84.** Thus, with the Panchasakha, Prabhu travels in the joy of dance.  
**8.85.** Year after year this is the custom: the Panchasakha accompany [Him].  
**8.86.** Prabhu, golden-fair in radiance, is the forgetting of sorrow.[^08-58]  
**8.87.** Achyuta … (??) fathomless (??) — the narrative of the rasa-lila.[^08-59]  
**8.88.** Swami is the guru (?), a great Meru of the rasa of kirtana.[^08-60]  

## The roles of the Panchasakha in kirtana; Gadadhara and Svarupa remain in Puri; the fruits of kirtana; the Swami's *Bhagavata* in the villages; a song of the Swami


**8.89.** Balarama [Das] is sattvika, the one who sets bhava-rasa going.[^08-61]  
**8.90.** Ananta and Yashovanta, know, are connoisseurs of the rasa of kirtana.[^08-62]  
**8.91.** Guru Gadadhara performs worship; his abode is Tota-Gopinatha.[^08-63]  
**8.92.** He cannot go anywhere; he listens to [the narrations of] Krishna's lila.[^08-64]  
**8.93.** Svarupa is frail of body; in [his] heart there is not this (?) maya (?).[^08-65]  
**8.94.** He cannot go anywhere: he constantly endures suffering.  
**8.95.** The Panchasakha perform kirtana so as to give sons to the sonless.[^08-66]  
**8.96.** From the jaws of wild beasts kirtana rescues living beings.[^08-67]  
**8.97.** Wherever nature turns hostile, people arrange the rasa (?) [of kirtana].[^08-68]  
**8.98.** The Swami's *Bhagavata* manuscripts they worship as (??) a Deity.[^08-69]  
**8.99.** As many manuscripts of the *Sri Bhagavata* as there are — many have them copied and take them.  
**8.100.** In the villages the *Bhagavata* is read; they place it on a seat and worship it.[^08-70]  
**8.101.** There they perform the rasa-lila and dance — the play of bhava and rasa.[^08-71]
