**8.102.** Singing the Swami's pada songs, they dance, playing the khol and the tal.[^08-72]  

*A song [of the Swami]*[^08-73]

**8.P0.** [My] mind clings to Your feet! Giridhara! [My] mind clings to Your feet!  
**8.P1.** Best of dancers, nagara, thief of the gopis' garments — [my] mind clings to Your feet![^08-74]  
**8.P2.** The Lotus-born is entangled (?) by You, [while You are] bound by the bhava of devotees — [my] mind clings to Your feet![^08-75]  
**8.P3.** Stealer of the gopis' hearts, thief of Radha's heart — [my] mind clings to Your feet!  
**8.P4.** Jaga Das says, abiding in the mood of a servant: the mind longs (?) for the treasure of prema — [my] mind clings to Your feet![^08-76]  

## The invitation to Jhankad: the rasa before Sarala Chandi; wanderings with kirtana


**8.103.** Guru Achyuta gave instruction — [and] his disciple Ramachandra came (?).[^08-77]  
**8.104.** He says to the Swami: "Come, you (?), to Jhankadpur Patana!"[^08-78]  
**8.105.** That (?) place where the rasa was arranged is truly a dhama (?).[^08-79]  
**8.106.** The sakhas gathered together, and Prabhu was with them.  
**8.107.** In that place they arranged the rasa — in the full measure of the rasa of kirtana.  
**8.108.** "This is not [merely] Sarala Chandi; [this] is truly Radha's … (??)."[^08-80]  
**8.109.** She is the limit of mahabhava, manifestly Radha's pratima.[^08-81]  
**8.110.** Prabhu plays the rasa-lila; intoxicated (?) with bhava, He dances.[^08-82]  
**8.111.** Very lovely is this place: in it mahabhava arises (?) again.[^08-83]  
**8.112.** Thus a good while passes, with minds given to the rasa of kirtana.  
**8.113.** They wander through many towns and villages, spreading the rasa of kirtana.[^08-84]  
**8.114.** Says Madhava the cowherd (?): they go about intoxicated with the rasa of kirtana.[^08-85]  

[^08-1]: Nagara-kirtana is a procession singing the holy names through the streets of a town. In the CC/CB nagara-kirtana is connected above all with Navadvipa (CC, Adi 17; CB, Madhya 23); Madhava describes the morning kirtana of Mahaprabhu and the Panchasakha in Puri. *Sādhabe* is the vocative plural "O sadhus."
[^08-2]: Khol (*khoḷa*) is the Bengali and Odia name of the clay drum, the mridanga. According to Madhava, the name is connected with Odia *khoḷa* "cover, sheath": further on (8.20–30) it is told how the child's rag was laid on the mridanga and how since then the mridanga has been covered with cloth. The word *tahi̐ka* (printed ≈ ତ + ହିଁ + କ; OCR: କହିକ) is taken as "of that [event]"; the syntax of the half-line is uncertain.
[^08-3]: *Prabhātu* "from dawn" is printed ≈ ପ୍ରଭା + ତୁ in the second form, resembling ରୂ.
[^08-4]: The Panchasakha: Achyuta (Achyutananda), Ananta, Yashovanta, Balarama Dasa and Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 7.102, 7.144).
[^08-5]: The last word is printed ≈ େ + ବ + ହା/ଢ଼ା + େ + ୟ (OCR: ବେଢ଼ାସ୍ତେ); tentatively read as *beṛhāẏe* "surrounding" (from *beṛhibā* "to surround"). Another word is possible (cf. *behāẏā* "unabashed"). The mridanga players are not named.
[^08-6]: Jhanj are large paired cymbals; talas (*tāḷa*) are small hand cymbals (kartals). *Gaha gaha* imitates a booming, rolling sound (likewise in 8.17).
[^08-7]: The word in 8.8a is printed ≈ ବା + a sign like ଜୁ + େ + an ink-filled ତ — the same signs as *bāḷuta* "child" in 8.10; read as *bāḷute* "children." The predicate of 8.8 is not expressed; by the sense the children wake at the sound of the kirtana (cf. 8.10–11).
[^08-8]: *Bāsipāiṭi* — the morning household work (clearing up after the night: *bāsi* "of yesterday," *pāiṭi* "work"). The last word is printed ≈ ଉ + ଭ/ଭା + େ + ର (OCR: ଭଦ୍ର); read as *ubhāre* "take up, begin" (?) (cf. ଉଭାରିଛି 7.74).
[^08-9]: The pinda is the raised earthen platform-veranda in front of an Odia house. *Gaṛilara* (printed ≈ ଗଡ଼ଲର) has an unusual past ending ‑ilara, apparently for the rhyme with *dhāra*. *Bahu* (printed ≈ ବହୁ) is taken as "heavy, abundant"; possibly a verb "flowed."
[^08-10]: The first word of 8.13b is printed indistinctly: a sign with a loop on top and a flag at lower left (subscript ର?) + a round sign with an inner stroke (ନ୍ଦ?) + େ + ନ (OCR illegible). Tentatively *krandane* "wailing, with a cry." *Yāanti* is printed ≈ ଯାଅନ୍ତ (‑i lost).
[^08-11]: *Caïtana* is a colloquial form of Chaitanya's name. The episode of the mother's anger is not in the CC/CB; it is Madhava's Odia tradition.
[^08-12]: The last word of 8.15b is printed ≈ a sign like ସ/ବ + େ + a sign like ଷ/ଧ (OCR: ଘ୍ଷେ); tentatively *bandhe* "ties up." *Pataḷara* is taken as "of thin [cloth]" (*pataḷā* "thin"). The sense of the half-line is uncertain; cf. 8.26 (she "gathered" the rag, ଗୋଟାଇ).
[^08-13]: *Uñcāiṇa* "raising" is printed ≈ ଉ + a sign like ଞ୍ଚା + a sign like ନି/ଟି + ଣ. *Boligale* (printed ≈ ବୋଲିଗଲେ, lit. "saying, went") is taken by the sense as "headed for"; *dāṇḍa duāra* is the front door opening onto the street (*dāṇḍa* "street"). 8.17 continues syntactically in 8.18.
[^08-14]: *Kīrttana upare* — "onto the kirtana," i.e. onto the kirtana party. *Sabunta* "all" has the ending ‑nta, apparently for the rhyme with *phiṅganti* (printed ≈ ଫିଙ୍ଗନ୍ତ). The story continues on leaf 106 (8.20 ff.).
[^08-15]: This continues the story on leaf 105 (8.10–19): a child jumped out of bed and ran to see the kirtana, fell into a drain and cut his head; in her anger the mother threw the child's soiled rags into the kirtana. *Karanti* is an honorific plural; the subject is the mother (perhaps the mothers).
[^08-16]: Prabhu sees sandal paste in the child's excrement. This episode is found neither in the CC nor in the CB; it is an Odia tradition. Cf. CC, Antya 4: Mahaprabhu embraces Sanatana Gosvami, whose body is covered with oozing sores.
[^08-17]: Printed ବୋଲ; by the sense we read ବୋଳ, "smear, apply" (the edition does not clearly distinguish ଲ and ଳ). The reading "say" (*bola*) is less likely. *Bandana* means "veneration, worship".
[^08-18]: The first word is printed ≈ ସ with a subscript sign and ୂ + ରଣେ: we read ସ୍ମରଣେ, "in memory". The third word ≈ କୁ + a sign like ସ/ମ/ପ with ‑i + ଲର: we read କୁପିଲର, "who was angered" (?). *Bāḷuta* means "infant"; *khoḷa*, "covering, sheath".
[^08-19]: Khol is the Odia and Bengali name of the clay mridanga drum used in kirtana. Madhava derives the name from ଖୋଳ, "covering, sheath" (printed ଖୋଲ). The CC and CB often call the mridanga khol, but they give no such explanation of the name.
[^08-20]: In Madhava "the Swami" is usually Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 7.2). *Swāmī baḷarāma* in 8.33a may be one person ("Swami Balarama"; cf. 7.93) or two addresses, to the Swami and to Balarama. The latter is more likely: 8.33–34 then name all five of the Panchasakha, and in 8.34b Prabhu again addresses the Swami (Jagannatha Dasa). Cf. the note to 7.93.
[^08-21]: Printed ≈ ପ୍ର + ଚ with a subscript hook + ର. We read ପ୍ରଚୁର, "abundant"; perhaps ପ୍ରଚାର, "preaching, propagation". According to the CC, Mahaprabhu entrusted the preaching of the holy name in Bengal to Nityananda (CC, Madhya 15; CB, Antya 5), while He Himself spent His last years in Puri without leaving it (CC, Madhya 1). Prabhu's travels through Orissa with the Panchasakha (8.36–57) are not described in the CC/CB; this is an Odia tradition.
[^08-22]: Anavasara is the period of about two weeks after the Snana-yatra when Jagannatha is not shown to pilgrims. According to the CC (Madhya 11), on these days Mahaprabhu, unable to bear the separation, would go to Alalanatha — the CC's name for Alarnath at Brahmagiri, south of Puri. Printed ଅଲ୍ଲରନାଥେଣ.
[^08-23]: When months are counted from full moon to full moon, the dark half of Ashadha begins right after the full moon of Jyeshtha (the Snana-yatra). Anavasara lasts from its first day to the new moon; then come the Netrotsava and the Ratha-yatra.
[^08-24]: Khiri is sweet rice cooked in milk; the khiri-bhoga of Alarnath is famous in Orissa to this day. Printed ଖିଶ୍ଭୋଗ ('ri' as ଶ).
[^08-25]: Rama Raya is Ramananda Ray. The village of Bentapur, which Madhava connects with his family (3.78–81), lies not far from Alarnath. *Caracaï* means "honours, entertains, serves".
[^08-26]: Apparently murtis of the five Pandava brothers at the Alarnath temple. In the CC (Madhya 10) Mahaprabhu called Ramananda's father, Bhavananda Ray, Pandu, and his five sons the Pandavas.
[^08-27]: Ekamra-kshetra (Ekamra-kanana) is Bhubaneswar, the holy city of Shiva. The full moon of Pausha falls in December–January. The CB (Antya 2) describes how Mahaprabhu, on His way to Puri, visited Bhubaneswar and bathed in the Bindu-sarovara. Printed ପୂର୍ଣ୍ଣିମୀ (= ପୂର୍ଣ୍ଣିମା).
[^08-28]: Ananta-Vasudeva is a temple of Krishna (Vasudeva) with Balarama (Ananta) and Subhadra on the bank of the Bindusagara in Bhubaneswar.
[^08-29]: Krittivasa ("clad in a hide") is a name of Shiva; Lingaraja is the principal Deity of Bhubaneswar. In Odia tradition Lingaraja is worshipped as Harihara, Shiva and Vishnu in one form. Hence both bilva and tulasi are offered to Him (8.45). Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour Shiva as the best of Vaishnavas (Bhag. 12.13.16). 44b may also be understood as "the form of Harihara is manifest".
[^08-30]: Kedareshvara and Gauri are the Kedara-Gauri temples in Bhubaneswar, with a pond attached to them. The first word is printed ≈ ଗ + ଉ + a sign like ଷ/ଶ. We read ଗଉରି, "Gauri" ('ri' as ଶ). *Snāhāna* is *snāna*, "bathing", lengthened for the metre.
[^08-31]: The Bindusagara (Bindu-sarovara) is the sacred lake of Bhubaneswar. The couplet has no verb. We take "with love" (*anurāga*) with the bath.
[^08-32]: Niali is a village on the Prachi river with a temple of Madhava (cf. 2.58). Printed ନଆଳ.
[^08-33]: *Bhagata* is "devotion" (*bhagati*, with the ‑i lost) or "devotees": possibly also "the devotees make offerings to Madhava".
[^08-34]: The Makara-mela is the festival on Makara-sankranti, when the Sun enters Capricorn (mid-January).
[^08-35]: Jayadeva is the author of the *Gita-Govinda*. Odia tradition holds that his birthplace is Kenduli-sasana on the Prachi, near Niali (cf. 2.56–58); Bengali tradition places it at Kenduli in Birbhum. Mahaprabhu relished the songs of the *Gita-Govinda* in Puri with Svarupa Damodara and Ramananda Ray (CC, Madhya 2). *Paraśaṁse* = *praśaṁse*, "they praise".
[^08-36]: Chandrabhaga is a holy place on the seashore near Konark, where the Chandrabhaga river used to flow into the sea. Line 54b is not understood: it is printed *paṛaï muha̐ kahi̐ raṅgā*. Perhaps "where [the river] falls [into the sea] at its mouth". *Raṅgā* may mean "lovely", "red" or "joyful". The third word is printed ≈ a sign with a filled bottom (like କ) + ହିଁ; the OCR has ମୁହିଁ ("I") for ମୁହଁ.
[^08-37]: Probably Achala-saptami (Magha-saptami), the seventh day of the bright half of Magha (January–February), when pilgrims bathe in the sea at Chandrabhaga and greet the sunrise. The first word is printed ≈ a filled ଚ/ଭ + ଳ; we read ଚଳ, i.e. [ଅ]ଚଳ (?). *Buṛa pāri* means "having taken a dip". The last word is printed ଉଚ୍ଚର; the rhyme with ପାରି requires ଉଚ୍ଚରି, "proclaiming".
[^08-38]: Aditya-Vishnu is the Sun worshipped as Vishnu (Surya-Narayana); the Sun temple of Konark stands near Chandrabhaga. According to the *Brahma-samhita* (5.52), the sun is the eye of Govinda and runs its course by His order. A visit by Mahaprabhu to Konark is not described in the CC/CB.
[^08-39]: The sunrise over the sea throws Prabhu into ecstasy. Cf. CC, Antya 14 and 18: in Puri the Chataka hill and the sea reminded Him of Govardhana and the Yamuna. *Mata* = *matta*, "intoxicated"; *bhoḷa*, "beside oneself" (ଭୋ is printed as a ligature resembling ଭେ). The sentence continues in 8.58 (leaf 108).
[^08-40]: This continues 8.57: at sunrise over the sea at Chandrabhaga, Prabhu enters ecstasy. Mahabhava is the highest stage of prema, proper to Sri Radha. Gaudiya Vaishnavas see Mahaprabhu as Krishna, who has taken on the feelings and complexion of Radha (CC, Adi 1 and 4). We take *anekāi* as an adverb, "in many ways".
[^08-41]: Khiri is sweet rice in milk, and laddus are sweet balls. Vishnu here is Aditya-Vishnu, the Sun worshipped as Vishnu at Konark (cf. 8.56). Printed ଷିଶ୍ର ('ri' looking like ଶ, cf. 8.38) and ଆସ୍ୱଦନ.
[^08-42]: Mangala is a goddess whose well-known temple stands at Kakatpur on the Prachi river, between Konark and Puri (8.62). The last word of 61b is printed ≈ ରଧାଭ୍ରମ; we read ରାଧାଭ୍ରମ, "Radha-bhrama" (?). This may mean "the bewilderment [of feeling] of Radha" or "Prabhu takes [the goddess] for Radha" (?). Gaudiya Vaishnavas honour Durga as the Lord's external energy, acting by His will (Brahma-samhita 5.44). A visit to Kakatpur is not described in the CC/CB.
[^08-43]: The Kshetra is Sri Kshetra, Puri: from Kakatpur the road leads back to Puri.
[^08-44]: The Dola-yatra (Dola-purnima) is the swing festival at the full moon of Phalguna (February–March). According to the CC (Adi 13), Mahaprabhu appeared on this full moon (Gaura-purnima). Cuttack was the capital of the Gajapatis. *Naara* means the royal palace or residence; *rajā* = *rājā*. Printed ଦୋଲକୁ, ମେଲାକୁ. According to the CC (Madhya 16), Mahaprabhu passed through Cuttack on His way to Bengal, but the CC/CB do not describe yearly visits to Cuttack for the Dola (cf. the note to 8.35).
[^08-45]: Rudradeva is the Gajapati Prataparudra (r. from ≈1497). According to the CC (Madhya 11–14), Mahaprabhu at first refused to see the king and received him by His mercy only during the Ratha-yatra (Madhya 14).
[^08-46]: The first word is printed ≈ ରଣୀମାନ + a sign like ଙ୍କ/ନ୍ତ; we read ରାଣୀମାନଙ୍କ, "of the queens". *Sambhāṣaṇa* means "courteous greeting, address".
[^08-47]: Viraja-kshetra is Jajpur on the Vaitarani river, with the shrine of the goddess Viraja; the Dashashvamedha Ghat is a bathing place on the Vaitarani. According to the CC (Madhya 5), Mahaprabhu visited Jajpur (Yajapura) on His way to Puri. The last word of 68a is printed ≈ ବ/ଉ + ର + ଜା + ଇ; we read ବିରଜାଇ, "to Viraja" (?). The rhyme with ଅବଗାହ ("bathing") is weak; perhaps ଅବଗାହି, "having bathed".
[^08-48]: Yajanagara is Jajpur (Skt. Yayati-nagara, Yajna-pura). Printed ≈ ଯାଜନଗ୍ରଟିଏ; we take it as ଯାଜନଗର + ‑ଟିଏ ("a, one"). According to the CC (Adi 13), Mahaprabhu's grandfather Upendra Mishra lived in Shrihatta; the CC does not say that the ancestors came there from Orissa. The Odia tradition of the Mishras' origin in Jajpur is also reflected in some Bengali biographies. Mahaprabhu Himself is the Supreme Lord, Krishna (CC, Adi 1–2); the genealogy concerns only His family in His earthly lila.
[^08-49]: *Batsasa* appears to be the name of the gotra (Vatsa / Vatsasa). Samanta is an honorific title of Odia brahmanas holding sasanas. Kulina means "of noble lineage". A sasana is a village granted by the king to brahmanas.
[^08-50]: The six darsanas are the classical philosophical schools: Nyaya, Vaisheshika, Sankhya, Yoga, Mimamsa and Vedanta. *Sāmaratha* = *samartha*, "versed, skilled".
[^08-51]: Kapileshvara is Kapilendradeva (≈1435–1467), founder of the Solar dynasty of the Gajapatis and grandfather of Prataparudra. The second word of 74b is printed ≈ କଲ + a sign like ହିଁ; we read କଳହିଁ, "with quarrels" (?). We take *tarajā* as "threatened" (Skt. *tarjana*) (?).
[^08-52]: Shrihatta is Sylhet in eastern Bengal. According to the CC (Adi 13), Upendra Mishra's son Jagannatha Mishra moved from Shrihatta to Navadvipa, where Mahaprabhu appeared.
[^08-53]: These are Prabhu's kinsmen in Jajpur. Gaudiya Vaishnavas see in the worship of Mahaprabhu a recognition of His divinity, not merely the honouring of a sannyasi relative.
[^08-54]: Plastering the threshold and the house with cow-dung, lamps and the purna-kumbha (full pitcher) are the traditional signs of a solemn welcome to a holy man.
[^08-55]: The second word is printed ≈ ସ + ବ/ର + e + ଗ (ସରଗେ); we read ସରାଗେ, "lovingly" (?). The loss of ‑ā is common in this edition, and the metre requires nine syllables.
[^08-56]: Tulasi-kshetra is Kendrapara, with its temple of Baladeva (8.82). The second word of 81a is printed ≈ କ + ହୁ/ଛୁ + ଦ + ନ; we read କିଛୁଦିନ, "some days" (?). *Bije* is an honorific "procession, arrival".
[^08-57]: The bathing of the Deities is the Snana-yatra at the full moon of Jyeshtha. This closes the yearly round: the Snana-yatra, anavasara and Alarnath (8.36–41); the full moon of Pausha, Bhubaneswar (8.42–48); Makara-sankranti, Niali and Kenduli (8.49–53); Magha-saptami, Chandrabhaga, Konark and Kakatpur (8.54–62); the Dola, Cuttack (8.63–67); then Jajpur and Tulasi-kshetra (8.68–83). In the CC Mahaprabhu spends His last years in Puri (cf. the note to 8.35). *Snāhāna* = *snāna* (cf. 8.46).
[^08-58]: Cf. CC, Adi 3: Mahaprabhu is "Gaura", of golden complexion. *Pāśora* comes from *pāśoribā*, "to forget": "[He is] the forgetting of sorrow", i.e. at the sight of Him sorrow is forgotten. *Aṭaïṭi* (also in 8.88) appears to be *aṭaï*, "is", with the particle ‑ṭi.
[^08-59]: Achyuta is Achyutananda Das of the Panchasakha. The impression of 87a is faint: printed ≈ ଅଚ୍ୟୁତ + କ, ର/ଚ with ‑i, ଣ/ଖ + ଅ(?)ଥାଇ. The reading କରିଣ ଅଥାଇ is tentative. From the context (8.86–90 describe Prabhu and each of the Panchasakha in turn), the sense is probably that Achyuta composes [songs] on the rasa-lila. Printed ରସଲୀଳାର; perhaps ରାସଲୀଳାର, "of the rasa-lila". The rasa-lila is Krishna's dance with the gopis (Bhag. 10.29–33).
[^08-60]: Swami is Jagannatha Dasa (cf. 8.33–34). The last word of 88a is printed ≈ ଗୁ/ଲୁ + ରୁ; we read ଗୁରୁ from its shape and from the rhyme with ମହାମେରୁ. He appears to be the teacher and leader in kirtana. Meru is the world mountain, a figure of greatness. In the CC, Svarupa Damodara is named as Mahaprabhu's foremost connoisseur of rasa and kirtana (Madhya 10).
[^08-61]: Balarama Das, one of the Panchasakha (cf. 8.33). "Sattvika" apparently means full of the sattvika bhavas, the eight bodily signs of ecstasy (stupor, perspiration, hair standing on end, faltering voice, etc.; *Bhakti-rasamrita-sindhu* 2.3). At kirtana gatherings he "sets going" the bhava-rasa.
[^08-62]: Ananta and Yashovanta, two of the Panchasakha (cf. 8.33–34). Couplets 8.89–90 apparently continue the list of the Panchasakha's roles in kirtana begun on leaf 109.
[^08-63]: Gadadhara Pandit, Madhava's guru. Tota-Gopinatha is the Deity of Gopinatha in the garden (*ṭoṭā*) by the sea at Puri, whom Gadadhara served. According to the CC (Madhya 16), Gadadhara took the vow of kshetra-sannyasa, never to leave Puri; according to the CC (Antya 7), Mahaprabhu heard the *Bhagavata* recited by him. Printed ପୂଜନ୍ତ, ବସତ (‑i not visible).
[^08-64]: Gadadhara is bound by his service to Gopinatha (and, according to the CC, by his vow), so he does not join Prabhu's travels with the Panchasakha (cf. 8.32–57). The first word is printed with the sign that in this font stands for କ: କାହିଁ, "anywhere" (not ତାହିଁ, "there"); likewise in 8.94 and 8.97.
[^08-65]: Svarupa Damodara. The second half-line is read with uncertainty: printed ≈ ହୃଦ + େ + a sign with a thickened foot (like ୟ in ମାୟା) + a sign resembling ଏ. We read ହୃଦୟେ ଏ ମାୟା, "in the heart there is not this maya" — perhaps "he is not attached [to the body or to worldly things]"; the sense is unclear. According to the CC (Antya 14–20), Svarupa Damodara stayed constantly with Mahaprabhu in the Gambhira until the end of His lila; the CC says nothing of an illness of Svarupa.
[^08-66]: In 8.95–97 Madhava speaks of the worldly fruits of kirtana: sons for the childless, protection from beasts and calamities. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the highest fruit of sankirtana is prema, and worldly benefits come only incidentally (cf. *Shikshashtaka* 1; CC, Antya 20). Printed apart as ଅପୁତ୍ରି କୁ.
[^08-67]: Cf. CC, Madhya 17: in the Jharikhanda forest tigers and elephants chanted the name of Krishna and danced together with Mahaprabhu. Printed ହଂସ୍ର (= ହିଂସ୍ର). The form *uddharu*, "rescues", is unclear.
[^08-68]: "Nature turns hostile" — drought, flood, epidemic and other calamities. The third word of the second half-line is printed ≈ ର + ସ + ଟି (OCR ଗ୍ରସଝି); we read ରସଟି, "the rasa [of kirtana]" (?). *Bhiāi* — "arrange".
[^08-69]: The Swami is Jagannatha Dasa, author of the Odia *Bhagavata*. *Pothi* — a palm-leaf manuscript. The second word of the second half-line is printed ≈ ଦୁଆଣେ (OCR ଭୁଆଣେ); by the sense, "as a Deity", possibly "doubly more than a Deity". Honouring the *Bhagavata* as the Lord Himself agrees with the *Bhagavatam* (1.3.43): after Krishna's departure to His own abode this Purana has risen like the sun.
[^08-70]: Cf. the Odia village custom of the "bhagavata-tungi" (bhagavata-ghara): a house where Jagannatha Dasa's *Bhagavata* is kept on a raised seat, worshipped and read aloud.
[^08-71]: Rasa-lila here is a performance of Krishna's lilas with dance (cf. rahasa, 2.103). The first sign of the word ରାସଲୀଳା is printed as ର with a tail — apparently the ligature ରା.
[^08-72]: Padas are songs in Odia; Madhava quotes one of the Swami's songs below (8.P0–P4). The khol is the mridanga (cf. 8.30); the tal — small hand cymbals (kartals). Printed ନୃତନ୍ତ (= ନୃତ୍ୟନ୍ତି), ତାଲ.
[^08-73]: The song is printed without numbers, as a separate block after 8.102; the next verse (8.103) is on leaf 112. The labels 8.P0–8.P4 are ours. The refrain "ତୋର ଚରଣେ ମନ ଲାଗଇରେ" stands in the edition at the beginning and after each stanza; in the translation it is repeated at the end of each line. *Tora* is the familiar "Your" ("thy"): so Krishna is addressed. Giridhara is Krishna, who lifted Govardhana hill (*Bhagavatam* 10.25). Printed ≈ ଗିଶଧର («ରି» as ଶ).
[^08-74]: The stealing of the gopis' garments — *Bhagavatam* 10.22. Nagara — the elegant, skilful lover.
[^08-75]: Padmaja, "the Lotus-born", is Brahma (less often the name is used of Lakshmi). The third word is printed ≈ ଛ + a sign like ଦ + ା (OCR ଚନା); we read ଛନ୍ଦା, "entangled" (?). Perhaps a hint at how Krishna bewildered Brahma (*Bhagavatam* 10.13–14). "Bound by the bhava of devotees" — cf. *Bhagavatam* 9.4.63 ("I am subordinate to My devotees") and the Damodara lila (10.9).
[^08-76]: The signature (bhanita) "Jaga Das" is a short form of the name Jagannatha Dasa: the song belongs to the Swami (cf. 8.102). *Āsaïre* is printed ଆସଇ, "comes"; possibly = ଆଶଇ, "hopes, longs". In 8.P3 ଚିତ୍ତ is printed ≈ ଚତ୍ତ.
[^08-77]: Achyuta is Achyutananda, one of the Panchasakha (cf. 7.144, 8.33). Ramachandra is his disciple; Madhava gives no other information about him. The last word is printed ≈ ଅ + a sign like ଇ (or ଣ) + ଲ (OCR ଅଇଲ); we read ଅଇଲ, "came" (as in 3.52, 4.130). The first sign of ଉପଦେଶିଲ is blotted with ink.
[^08-78]: Jhankad (Jhankadpur Patana) is a place in Orissa, east of Cuttack, where the goddess Sarala (Sarala Chandi) is worshipped; the poet Sarala Das (15th c.), author of the Odia *Mahabharata*, is held to have been her devotee. *Tuhi*, "you", is a familiar form of address; printed ≈ ରୁ + ହି (the first sign is the second form of ତ; OCR ରୁଦ୍ଧ); the reading is confirmed by the rhyme with ପାଟଣାହିଁ. A visit of Mahaprabhu to Jhankad is not mentioned in the CC or CB; this is Odia tradition.
[^08-79]: The rasa is an enactment of Krishna's lilas with dance (cf. 8.101, rahasa 2.103). Printed ରଚତ (= ରଚିତ). The third word is ≈ େ + a sign like ଏ/ସ/ଯ + ହୁ: we read ସେହୁ, "that very"; possibly ଯେହୁ, "where". The word ଅଟଇଟି is apparently ଅଟଇ "is" with the particle ‑ଟି; its nuance is unclear.
[^08-80]: The first word is printed ≈ ସା + େ + a ର with a tail (as in the ligature ରା) + ଲ; by sense and by the editor's note we read ସାରଳା, Sarala. The last word is unclear: ≈ ବ + a sign like କ୍ଷ + ହେ (OCR କର୍ତହେ). If it is ବକ୍ଷ, the sense would be "[Radha's] bosom"; it may be a misprint. The speaker is not named — apparently Prabhu or the sakhas at the darshan of the goddess. The identification of Sarala Chandi with Radha is Madhava's Odia tradition (cf. Mangala, 8.61). For Gaudiya Vaishnavas, Durga is the Lord's shadow, external energy, acting by His will (*Brahma-samhita* 5.44). Radha is His internal energy of bliss, the hladini (CC, Adi 4).
[^08-81]: *Pratimā* here means the "embodiment, likeness" of Radha (cf. 7.140), not a Deity in a temple. Printed ≈ ପ୍ରତମା. Mahabhava is the highest stage of prema, proper to Sri Radha (CC, Adi 4).
[^08-82]: Printed ମ + the ligature ତ୍ତ with ‑i; we read it as "intoxicated" (ମାତି, gerund of ମାତିବା?; cf. ମତ୍ତ 7.131). According to the CC (Adi 4; Antya 14–20), Mahaprabhu relishes the mood of Radha. That He "plays the rasa-lila", dancing in bhava, is Madhava's own picture.
[^08-83]: Printed ≈ ପୂରମ୍ୟ; we read ସୁରମ୍ୟ, "lovely" (as the editor has it). The verb is printed ≈ ଉପୁଜ with a superscript mark; we read "arises" (ଉପୁଜଇ?).
[^08-84]: Printed ଭ୍ରମନ୍ତ (= ଭ୍ରମନ୍ତି) and ନଗ୍ର (= ନଗର). According to Madhava, Prabhu goes with the Panchasakha through the villages of Orissa with kirtana (cf. 8.32–57, 8.95–102). According to the CC (Madhya 16 – Antya 20), after the journey to Vrindavana Mahaprabhu lived in Puri; the CC and CB do not describe such tours.
[^08-85]: The closing formula of the chapter with the author's name (cf. 2.131, 7.148). *Guāḷa* (printed ≈ ଗୁଆଲ) means "cowherd": probably Madhava's humble self-designation, like *dāsa* in other chapters. Another reading is possible: "the cowherds", i.e. the Panchasakha as Krishna's cowherd friends. Then: "Madhava says: the cowherds go about intoxicated with the rasa of kirtana." *Matta* is printed ମ + a sign like ଢ, which is the ligature ତ୍ତ (cf. 7.131). *Cāla* means "go about" (cf. "କୀର୍ତ୍ତନ ରସେ ମତ୍ତ ଚାଲି" 7.145). With this verse chapter 8 ends; there is no "ଇତି…" colophon. Chapter 9 ("ନବମ ଅଧ୍ୟାୟ") begins on leaf 113.

---

# Madhava Pattanayak. *Vaishnava-lilamrita*, Chapter 9
## The ishtagoshthi of Nilachala; Rupa brings his drama


*Chapter Nine*

**9.1.** The ishtagoshthi of Nilachala gathers on the northern (?) side [of the temple].[^09-1]  
**9.2.** Rama Raya is the chief in it; Prabhu and the Swami sit in the middle.[^09-2]  
**9.3.** Balarama [Das] is beside the Swami; [there too are] Ananta, Achyuta, Yashovanta.[^09-3]  
**9.4.** Svarupa [and] Raghunatha Das are distinguished scholars.[^09-4]  
**9.5.** Sarvabhauma sits in the middle; he conducts the deliberation on the shastras.[^09-5]  
**9.6.** From Vrindavana comes Rupa — in the forty-fourth anka of the king.[^09-6]  
**9.7.** Rupa showed his drama; Prabhu bids (?) [him] read [it].[^09-7]  
**9.8.** [All] came to the ishtagoshthi; Rupa showed the drama.  
**9.9.** Prabhu handed [it] to Rama Raya: "Examine [it]," [He] said.[^09-8]  

## Ramananda tests Rupa's drama; the passing of Svarupa Damodara; Gadadhara is sent to Gauda; the passing of Balarama Das


**9.10.** Raya said: "Recite the shlokas," in order to test [his] poetic gift.[^09-9]  
**9.11.** Rupa recites all the shlokas, from the nandi to Radha's mana.[^09-10]  
**9.12.** Having heard Rupa, Raya said: "Your poetry is a treasure-trove of nectar.[^09-11]  
**9.13.** Such (?) a drama of rasa you have made! We have tasted (?) the rasa of bhakti (?).[^09-12]  
**9.14.** Your drama is excellent; may it become known in all lands."  
**9.15.** This drama, the *Vidagdha-madhava*, became known among the people.[^09-13]  
**9.16.** Rupa stayed [in Puri] four months; at the beginning of Karttika he departs.[^09-14]  
**9.17.** Raghunatha [Das] set out with Rupa on the road to Vrindavan.[^09-15]  
**9.18.** But Svarupa, remaining here, endures many sufferings.[^09-16]  
**9.19.** In the torment (??) of this breathing disease he tosses about [in pain].[^09-17]  
**9.20.** On the ekadashi of the bright fortnight of Magha came his leaving [of the body] in the Gambhira.[^09-18]  
**9.21.** Ananta, Achyuta [and I], we gathered; we called Yashovanta.[^09-19]  
**9.22.** We carried the body to the sea; we gave the saint to the waves.[^09-20]  
**9.23.** No word comes forth (?) from Prabhu's mouth; Raya stood stunned (?).[^09-21]  
**9.24.** Together with the Swami [and] Balarama they grieved deeply there.  
**9.25.** Prabhu calls Gadadhara: "Go from here (?) to Gauda;[^09-22]  
**9.26.** Svarupa has passed away (?) here: inform the devotees properly."[^09-23]  
**9.27.** Gadadhara went to Gauda at the beginning of the month of Phalguna.[^09-24]  
**9.28.** Balarama [Das] is an excellent pandit, devoted to the doctrine of the Vedanta shastra.[^09-25]  
**9.29.** He composed a *Ramayana* [and] an exposition of the *Bhagavad-gita*.[^09-26]  
**9.30.** His mind is firm in brahma-jnana; in bhakti (?) … (??) … (??).[^09-27]  
**9.31.** He always performs kirtana; he says nothing (?) to anyone.[^09-28]  
**9.32.** He chants the Name unceasingly. The time of [his] passing came.[^09-29]  
**9.33.** He said: "I will give up food," calling (?) it dharma (?) [and] austerity (?).[^09-30]  
**9.34.** He sits in seclusion (?) in a mandapa; on the tenth day (?) of the bright fortnight of Phalguna[^09-31]  
**9.35.** the breath leaves (?) the body; … (??) falls.[^09-32]  
**9.36.** Prabhu, the Swami, Yashovanta, Kanhai, Achyuta, Ananta[^09-33]  
**9.37.** [and] Rama Raya came; all recalled [his] virtues.  
**9.38.** He, dear to Jagannatha, enters Jagannatha's body.[^09-34]  
**9.39.** In the sand (?) of the seashore they buried the body there (??).[^09-35]  
**9.40.** The devotees heard the news, with heads bowed, shedding tears.[^09-36]  
**9.41.** Prabhu [and] the Swami did not rise (?); they spoke not a word of it.[^09-37]  

## Prabhu injures His foot while dancing; the night in the northern mandapa, the Swami beside Him


**9.42.** Listen attentively, good people: Prabhu's body is holy.[^09-38]  
**9.43.** Prabhu's body is a pure body; [hear] how in time it came to decline.[^09-39]  
**9.44.** One day [He] was dancing and did not leave His unrestrained bhava.[^09-40]  
**9.45.** To the khol and kartal [His] feet moved incessantly.  
**9.46.** A brick lay on the path — He did not notice it.[^09-41]  
**9.47.** [The brick] struck the big toe of [His] left foot; blood flows without stopping (?).[^09-42]  
**9.48.** The foot twisted at the joint (?); with a loud cry [He] fell to the ground.[^09-43]  
**9.49.** The Swami, Kashishvara Mishra, Yashovanta, Srivatsa, Ananta —[^09-44]  
**9.50.** all of them together lift Prabhu from the ground.  
**9.51.** Prabhu has lost consciousness: He lies on the ground without awareness.  
**9.52.** They lifted Prabhu onto their shoulders — [it was] the evening of the Rukmini new moon.[^09-45]  
**9.53.** In the mandapa on the north side they laid [Him] down on the floor (??).[^09-46]  
**9.54.** Breath (?) comes from His nostrils; [He] lies with eyes closed (?).[^09-47]  
**9.55.** They sprinkled water on His face; after some time consciousness awakens.  
**9.56.** [He] opened His eyes and lay [so]; breath entered His body.  
**9.57.** All rejoiced; the Swami gazes at Prabhu's face.  
**9.58.** He says: "Prabhu, speak a word, give life back to my body!"[^09-48]  
**9.59.** In time Prabhu came to Himself (?); all the people went off to their own (?) places.[^09-49]  
**9.60.** At about the first watch of the night Rama Raya withdrew (?).[^09-50]  
**9.61.** In the temple the khol and tal sound; a deep, mighty roar arose (?).[^09-51]  
**9.62.** Prabhu says: "Carry Me there — I shall watch the dancing, staying a moment."  
**9.63.** But the foot cannot be set down; the Swami, giving his hand, seats [Him].[^09-52]  
**9.64.** [The Swami] said: "Prabhu, do not go; sit still in this place.  
**9.65.** There is pain in Your foot; do not move from here even a cubit.  
**9.66.** Drink, food, bathing (?) for You — I shall stay [here] and provide it all (?)."[^09-53]  
**9.67.** Thus the night passed; dawn came (?).[^09-54]  

## The fever and Prabhu's last words to the Swami; Prabhu's disappearance on Akshaya Tritiya; Jagannatha Dasa beside the body; word sent to Rama Raya


**9.68.** Prabhu's body is burning with fever — as though puffed rice would pop on it (?).[^09-55]  
**9.69.** By evening (?) the foot had swollen; the toe of the left foot gave pain.[^09-56]  
**9.70.** The Swami heated water and dipped a rag (?) in it while it was hot (?)[^09-57]  
**9.71.** [and] laid it on Prabhu's left foot: "Has it become warm?" — he asked (?).[^09-58]  
**9.72.** Receiving the warmth of the hot rag, [Prabhu] was soothed.  
**9.73.** Yet the fever (?) did not let go; at night (?) shivering seized the body.[^09-59]  
**9.74.** Collecting [Himself] (?), [Prabhu] said to the Swami: "While you are [here], there is no term (kala) for Me.[^09-60]  
**9.75.** Would I ever leave your company? [But] Hari's will is being fulfilled.  
**9.76.** All Hari's servants in the world — not for an instant (?) does dying befall them.[^09-61]  
**9.77.** You are the servant of Jagannatha; I am your servant, [and it was] unseemly (?):[^09-62]  
**9.78.** I received service from your hands. Jagabandhu knows all.[^09-63]  
**9.79.** Without Him there is no other refuge; He decides (?) the lot of living beings.[^09-64]  
**9.80.** Taking [Me] to His feet, will He take Me to His own abode?[^09-65]  
**9.81.** You, Swami, are My life (?) — wise, learned, knowing.[^09-66]  
**9.82.** You showed [Me] the path of bhakti; you bound Me with ties of friendship.[^09-67]  
**9.83.** Leaving you, where shall I go? [There] I shall be watching (?) the road by which you will come."[^09-68]  
**9.84.** Tears are in Prabhu's eyes; the Swami gazes at [His] moon-like face (?).[^09-69]  
**9.85.** Tears mingled with tears and flowed in an overflowing (?) stream.[^09-70]  
**9.86.** The Swami stays close beside Him; he serves Prabhu, never taking his eyes off [Him].  
**9.87.** The bond of feeling [as] of a faithful wife, when [she] gives (??) her life to the moon (??).[^09-71]  
**9.88.** The fever does not leave the body; the whole body has swollen.  
**9.89.** Akshaya Tritiya arrived — [the time of] the chandana, the garlands (?) [and] the abakasha.[^09-72]  
**9.90.** The hour of the brahma-muhurta came — the breath of life left Prabhu's body.[^09-73]  
**9.91.** Jagannatha Dasa alone was beside Him in the temple.[^09-74]  
**9.92.** He weeps aloud: "O Prabhu, where have You gone?"[^09-75]  
**9.93.** Tears stream from his eyes; [he] turns over (??) Prabhu's holy body.[^09-76]  
**9.94.** Having laid the body down carefully (?), he weeps, unblinking (?).[^09-77]  
**9.95.** A crow came and cried out; the east (?) grew bright.[^09-78]  
**9.96.** [It was] the time of the morning abakasha; the temple lies still [and silent].[^09-79]  
**9.97.** One or two servitors came to the temple.  
**9.98.** The Swami said: "Go quickly, inform Rama Raya.[^09-80]  
**9.99.** Let him come here without delay; go now, at a run (?)."[^09-81]  
**9.100.** Receiving the message, Rama Raya at once (?) arrived at the temple.[^09-82]  

## Rama Raya and the Gajapati; the secret burial in Koili Vaikuntha


**9.101.** Seeing Prabhu's grievous state, [Rama Raya] for a moment stood frozen in deep shock (?).[^09-83]  
**9.102.** Tears stream from [his] eyes; he took [Prabhu's] body onto his lap.[^09-84]  
**9.103.** He stroked Prabhu's face and passed his hand over [His] whole body.  
**9.104.** "Prabhu is no more!" — [thinking] thus, he let go of (?) the body and rose.[^09-85]  
**9.105.** Hastily (??) calling the servants, he gave an order aloud (?):[^09-86]  
**9.106.** "Today the gates will be bolted; the Deity's rites will not be performed."[^09-87]  
**9.107.** Summoning (?) a horseman, he at once sent a letter.  
**9.108.** As it happened, [it was the day of] Akshaya Tritiya, the joyful (?) Chandana Yatra.[^09-88]  
**9.109.** Gajapati Rudradeva, as it happened (?), had set out [for the temple] for darshan.[^09-89]  
**9.110.** Meeting the horseman on the way, he opens the letter and reads it.  
**9.111.** With grief [his] face was clouded with sorrow. It was about midday (?).[^09-90]  
**9.112.** Rama Raya informed [him] and says: "What are we to do with Prabhu?"  
**9.113.** The Gajapati stood stupefied (?): truly, not a word comes from his mouth.[^09-91]  
**9.114.** Rama Raya says: "Listen: if this body is carried out, [it will bring] harm.[^09-92]  
**9.115.** The Gaudiyas will stir up trouble and speak hostile words to the mleccha king.[^09-93]  
**9.116.** Great strife will arise; out of empty talk [they will make] both lies and truth.  
**9.117.** They will piece together many rumours — and in the end ill fame (?) will fall to us.[^09-94]  
**9.118.** Prabhu departed (?) in the temple — here we shall commit Him to the earth.[^09-95]  
**9.119.** Your Majesty, at the time of the Ratha Yatra you called [Him] 'Prabhu' before the pilgrims;[^09-96]  
**9.120.** [so] there is ground for saying: 'This Prabhu became absorbed in that Prabhu.'[^09-97]  
**9.121.** It is fitting to commit the body to the earth at the place (?) of Koili Vaikuntha,[^09-98]  
**9.122.** where the worn-out [forms] of the Deity Jagannatha are buried — carry it there."  
**9.123.** The Gajapati, with face cast down, agreed to what was fitting.  
**9.124.** Taking the body to Koili Vaikuntha, having a pit dug, they committed [it] to the earth —  
**9.125.** Rama Raya, the revered Swami, and one or two servants besides.[^09-99]  
**9.126.** No one else knows of this. The bolted gates are opened.[^09-100]  
**9.127.** They ordered a purification (?) — the whole temple was washed.[^09-101]  
**9.128.** With camphor, sandal [and] aguru the servants sprinkled [the temple] inside.  

## The news of Prabhu's disappearance; the grief of the Panchasakha; a Ratha Yatra without Prabhu


**9.129.** At that time, seeing that the festival of Chandana was beginning,  
**9.130.** the devotees came [and] gathered; [then] they set off, [making] for Markanda.[^09-102]  
**9.131.** On the festival day there is sorrow: on no one's face is there joy.  
**9.132.** Receiving the news of Prabhu's disappearance, all [were] in dismay:[^09-103]  
**9.133.** "He has merged into Jagannatha's body — as lightning hides in a cloud."[^09-104]  
**9.134.** They look (?) into one another's faces; at the talk of Prabhu [all] were beside themselves.[^09-105]  
**9.135.** All grew sorrowful at heart, remembering Prabhu's qualities:  
**9.136.** "Prabhu is Bhagavan Himself; He appeared (lit. "was born") in order to dance (?).[^09-106]  
**9.137.** And now, alas, He has merged into the holy body!" Hearing [this], all were overwhelmed with feeling.[^09-107]  
**9.138.** Achyuta, Kanhai, Yashovanta and Ananta came to know [of it].[^09-108]  
**9.139.** Hearing it, all were grieved; embracing Swami, they weep.  
**9.140.** Achyuta says to Swami: "Why did you let Prabhu go?[^09-109]  
**9.141.** We could not see [Him]. Why did you not bind [Him and] keep [Him]?"[^09-110]  
**9.142.** Yashovanta says this: "What sort of talk is [this] boy talking!"[^09-111]  
**9.143.** The child Ananta does not understand — he weeps like (?) a little child.[^09-112]  
**9.144.** Truly, all recalled Prabhu's deeds day and night.  
**9.145.** The Gaudiya devotees arrived; on the chariot the king proclaimed (?) [the news].[^09-113]  
**9.146.** The Gaudiyas all departed; going to Gauda (?), they told [it].[^09-114]  
**9.147.** The news of Prabhu's departure they all learned together at the Ratha Yatra.[^09-115]  
**9.148.** Not finding Prabhu at the chariot, the king grieved deeply.  
**9.149.** The golden broom fell: from the king's hand — this had never been (?).[^09-116]  
**9.150.** During the Ratha Yatra the people (?) learned this news.[^09-117]  
**9.151.** The month of Bhadra, Krishnashtami: knowing [it to be] the festival of Krishna's birth,[^09-118]  

## Rama Raya's departure; the circle disperses; the end of the poem


**9.152.** There was no joyful kirtan; in every heart there was dejection.[^09-119]  
**9.153.** To the mandapa on the northern side Rama Raya came and sat down.[^09-120]  
**9.154.** Remembering Prabhu's bhava, [he weeps] — tears brim over from his eyes.  
**9.155.** With the Swami he talks fondly, remembering the kirtan.[^09-121]  
**9.156.** The Swami said: "I will not come [any more]; I will live on the seashore."[^09-122]  
**9.157.** Achyuta went (?) to Nemala (?); Ananta went to his own place.[^09-123]  
**9.158.** On that [same] day, at evening time, Rama Raya departed to heaven.[^09-124]  
**9.159.** As the prana was leaving (?) [his] body, he uttered: "O Krishna!"[^09-125]  
**9.160.** At once (?) a stir arose; people hastily gathered.[^09-126]  
**9.161.** On every lip was the name of Hari; they say: "Rama's name is truth."[^09-127]  
**9.162.** Good or bad, a living being speaks its word at the hour of passing (??); knowing this,[^09-128]  
**9.163.** Jagannatha Dasa says: "He is a perfected one (a siddha)."[^09-129]  
**9.164.** This is so; it cannot be otherwise (?), — says Madhava, a shudra (?).[^09-130]  

*Thus the* Vaishnava-lilamrita *is complete.*  
*The account here is extensive (?);*  
*do not blame the scribe.*[^09-131]

[^09-1]: The ishtagoshthi is a gathering of intimate devotees for talks about Krishna; in the CC it is the usual expression for Mahaprabhu's discussions with His associates. Nilachala is Puri. Second half-line: printed ଉ + the ligature ତ୍ତ — we read ଉତ୍ତ, apparently a shortened ଉତ୍ତର, "northern" (?); ଉଚ୍ଚ, "high", is also possible. *Pāruśeṇa* is from *pāruśa* = *pārśwa*, "side". Printed ନୀଲାଚଲର, ବସନ୍ତ.
[^09-2]: Rama Raya is Ramananda Raya. The Swami is Jagannatha Dasa. The Swami's place beside Prabhu at the centre of the gathering is Madhava's Odia tradition. According to the CC (Antya 6, 14–20), Mahaprabhu's closest companions in conversation at Puri were Svarupa Damodara and Ramananda Raya. Jagannatha Dasa is not mentioned in the CC or CB. Printed ମଧେ (= ମଧ୍ୟେ), ବସନ୍ତ.
[^09-3]: Balarama Das, Ananta, Achyuta (Achyutananda), Yashovanta and the Swami are the Panchasakha (cf. 8.4, 8.33). Printed ବଲରାମ.
[^09-4]: Svarupa Damodara and Raghunatha Das (Gosvami). According to the CC (Antya 6), Mahaprabhu entrusted Raghunatha Das to Svarupa Damodara, and he lived for many years in Puri under his care. Printed ଅଟନ୍ତ, ବଶେଷ.
[^09-5]: Vasudeva Sarvabhauma, the Vedantist scholar of Puri converted by Mahaprabhu (CC, Madhya 6). Printed ମଧେ, ବସନ୍ତ, କରନ୍ତ.
[^09-6]: Rupa Gosvami. "The forty-fourth anka of the king" is the regnal year of Prataparudra counted in ankas, ≈1531/32 (±1 year). According to the CC (Antya 1), Rupa came to Puri a second time from Vrindavana by way of Gauda, soon after meeting Mahaprabhu at Prayaga (Madhya 19). In the chronology accepted by Gaudiya Vaishnavas this was long before the 1530s. Printed ଚଉରାଲିସେ.
[^09-7]: Madhava names the drama below, in 9.15 (leaf 114): *Vidagdha-madhava*. According to the CC (Antya 1), in Puri Rupa was writing two dramas, *Vidagdha-madhava* and *Lalita-madhava*. The verb is printed ≈ ବୋଲି + a sign ଇ (OCR ବୋଲଇ); we read ବୋଲଇ, "says, bids". *Paṛhibāka* = ପଢ଼ିବାକୁ, "to read" (printed ପଢ଼ବାକ).
[^09-8]: Cf. CC (Antya 1): Mahaprabhu came with Ramananda Raya, Sarvabhauma and other associates to where Rupa was staying (with Haridasa Thakura) and asked Rupa to read verses from the dramas. Ramananda questioned Rupa about the drama and praised his poetry. In Madhava the examination takes place in the ishtagoshthi. Printed ପରୀକ୍ଷ (= ପରୀକ୍ଷା).
[^09-9]: Raya is Ramananda Raya (cf. 9.2, 9.9). According to the CC (Antya 1), Ramananda questioned Rupa in detail about his dramas, and Rupa recited verses from them in the presence of Mahaprabhu, Sarvabhauma and other associates. The word ଶ୍ଳୋକ is printed with a subscript ଲ (ଶ୍ଲୋକ); *kabitwa* in 9.10 is printed ≈ କବତ୍ୱ (the ‑i broken). *Paramāṇibāka* means "in order to test" (a purposive infinitive).
[^09-10]: The nandi is the benedictory verse at the opening of a Sanskrit drama. Mana is Radha's loving pique towards Krishna. "From the nandi to Radha's mana" means from the first verse of the drama to one of its last scenes. According to the CC (Antya 1), Ramananda began his questions with the nandi verse.
[^09-11]: Cf. CC, Antya 1: Ramananda says that Rupa's verses are not mere poetry but a stream of nectar. We take *rūpe śuṇi* as "having heard Rupa"; an address, "Rupa, listen", is also possible.
[^09-12]: The first word is printed ≈ ଏ + e + a sign like ହ/ଡ଼ + କ (OCR ଏଡ଼େକ); we read ଏଡ଼େକ, "such, so great". *Bhakatā* (printed ଭକତା) is either "bhakti" or "of the devotees" (?). The form *āswādilu* can mean either "you tasted" or "we tasted"; we choose the latter, since Ramananda speaks for the listeners. Printed ≈ ଅଃସ୍ୱାଦଲୁ.
[^09-13]: The *Vidagdha-madhava* is Rupa Gosvami's Sanskrit drama on Krishna's lilas in Vrindavan. According to the CC (Antya 1), Rupa first planned a single drama. Then, at Satyabhama's instruction in a dream and with Mahaprabhu's approval, he divided it into two: the *Vidagdha-madhava* on the Vrindavan lilas and the *Lalita-madhava* on the Dvaraka lilas. Madhava names only the first.
[^09-14]: Karttika falls in October–November. According to the CC (Antya 1), Rupa stayed in Puri until the Dola-yatra, a spring festival, and then returned to Vrindavan through Gauda. The four-month stay and the departure in Karttika are Madhava's account. Printed ରହଲେ and ≈ ଚାଶମାସେ ("ri" looks like ଶ).
[^09-15]: According to the CC (Antya 6), Raghunatha Das Gosvami lived in Puri for sixteen years in the care of Svarupa Damodara. He went to Vrindavan only after the disappearance of Mahaprabhu and Svarupa (cf. CC, Adi 10), and he did not leave with Rupa. Madhava's account differs; cf. also 9.4.
[^09-16]: This is Svarupa Damodara. Madhava has already spoken of his frailty and sufferings in 8.93–94.
[^09-17]: The last word of the first half-line is printed ≈ ବେ + an unclear ligature with a subscript + ର (OCR ବେଭ୍ର); we read ବେଜ୍ଜର (??), perhaps from ବେଜାର, "worn out". *Kaḷabaḷa* (printed କଲବଲ) means "tosses about in agony". The breathing disease is apparently asthma.
[^09-18]: Magha falls in January–February. No year is named; in the sequence of the narrative this is the Magha after Rupa's arrival (9.6), i.e. ≈1532/33 (±1 year). *Deharakṣā*, literally "keeping the body", is a respectful expression for leaving the body. The Gambhira is the room in Kashi Mishra's house where Mahaprabhu lived (cf. 5.20). According to the CC (Antya 14–20), Svarupa Damodara served Mahaprabhu in the Gambhira without leaving Him until the end of His lila. In Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition, Svarupa left this world soon after Mahaprabhu's disappearance. That Svarupa passed away while Mahaprabhu was still present is the account of Madhava's Odia tradition; it is not in the CC/CB.
[^09-19]: We take the verbs in ‑*ilu* in 9.21–22 as first person plural ("we"). Madhava apparently counts himself among those who carried the body. Printed ≈ ମିଳଲୁ, ହକାରଲୁ. Ananta, Achyuta(nanda) and Yashovanta belong to the Panchasakha (cf. 9.3).
[^09-20]: Svarupa's body was given to the sea (jala-samadhi). This was often done with the bodies of renunciants. Svarupa Damodara was a sannyasi (CC, Madhya 10).
[^09-21]: The last word of the first half-line is printed ≈ ନ + a ligature like ଷ୍ଟ/ସ୍ଫ + ‑u + ରେ; we read ନ ସ୍ଫୁରେ, "does not come forth" (?). The word in the second half-line is printed ≈ ଥ/ଅ + ମ୍ଭ + ‑i + ତ; we read ଥମ୍ଭିତ, "stunned" (from ଥମ୍ଭିବା; cf. ଥମ୍ଭିଲ in ch. 4).
[^09-22]: Gadadhara Pandit is Madhava's guru and the servant of Tota-Gopinatha (cf. 8.91–92). According to the CC (Madhya 16), Gadadhara took the vow of kshetra-sannyasa, never to leave Puri. By Mahaprabhu's will he stayed there when the Lord went towards Vrindavan. The errand to Gauda is a tradition of the Odia school. Madhava himself says in 8.92 that Gadadhara "cannot go anywhere". We take *ethuṭi* (printed ଏଥୁଟି) as ଏଥୁଁ + ଟି, "from here" (?). *Tu* ("you") is printed with the second form of ତ, which resembles ର.
[^09-23]: Printed ମାରଗଲ; we read ମରିଗଲ, "has passed away" (the ‑i is lost and the ā is apparently superfluous). The rhyme with ଭଲ confirms the final ‑ଲ.
[^09-24]: Phalguna falls in February–March. The CC/CB know of no journey of Gadadhara to Gauda (cf. the note to 9.25).
[^09-25]: Balarama Das is one of the Panchasakha (cf. 8.89). We take *matarata* (printed ≈ ମଠରତ, with ତ in its second form) as "devoted to the doctrine" (*mata* + *rata*); cf. ମତ in 9.30. Printed ବଲରାମ.
[^09-26]: Balarama Das's Odia *Ramayana* is known as the *Jagamohana Ramayana* (*Dandi Ramayana*). An Odia exposition of the *Bhagavad-gita* is also attributed to him.
[^09-27]: The reading of the second half-line is uncertain. It is printed ≈ ଭ + ଗ + a sign like ଢ (we read ଭଗତି, "bhakti", an old form); then ≈ ସ/ଯ + ā + ହ with a subscript + ā + e + ସ୍ତ୍ର (perhaps a distorted ଶାସ୍ତ୍ରେ, "in the shastras"); then କବାଡ଼ ("door-leaf"?). The sense may be "in the shastras of bhakti [he] opens the doors [of meaning]", i.e. he is versed in the scriptures of bhakti; this is only a guess. *Brahmajñāneṇa* is an archaic case in ‑eṇa.
[^09-28]: Printed ≈ କଛୁ ନ କହ + a subscript sign + କାହାକଇ; we read କିଛୁ ନ କହୁ (?), "says nothing". This is apparently the beginning of a vow of silence before his passing (cf. 9.33).
[^09-29]: The first sign is printed as ଭ resembling ଉ (OCR ଉଜଇ); we read ଭଜଇ, "chants". Cf. *Shikshashtaka* 3 (CC, Antya 20): the holy name is to be chanted constantly. *Maraṇa kāla* means "the time of passing" (printed କାଲ).
[^09-30]: Giving up food before leaving the body; cf. *prayopavesha*, fasting until one's passing, as Parikshit did (*Bhagavatam* 1.19). The second half-line is printed ≈ ଧର୍ମଁ (?) ତପସ୍ୟ/ତପସ୍ତ କହି; we read ଧର୍ମ ତପସ୍ୟା କହି, "calling [it] dharma [and] austerity" (?). *Chāṛibaï̐* is a future first-person form: "I will give up".
[^09-31]: No year is named; in the sequence of the narrative it is ≈1532/33 (±1 year). Printed ≈ ଦଶଦନେ; we read ଦଶଦିନେ, "on the tenth day" (?), i.e. the dashami. The word before "mandapa" is printed ≈ ନ + an arc ‑i + ବୃ/ପୃ + ତ; we read ନିବୃତ, "in seclusion" (?) (Sanskrit *nivṛtta*). Which mandapa is meant is unclear.
[^09-32]: *Piṇḍu pabana* means "the breath from the body". The verb is printed ≈ ଛଡ଼ଯାଇ. The second half-line is printed ≈ ବୃ + e + ଛ/ଷ + ନ/କ, ≈ ଅ + ସ୍ଥ/ଷ୍ଟ + ଳ, ପଡ଼ଇ (OCR ବୂବଜ୍ଷକ କତ୍ଭଲ ପଡ଼ଇ). Only "falls" is clear; perhaps "[the body] falls like a [felled] tree" (??).
[^09-33]: For Kanhai, cf. 2.25, 5.77, 6.10 (possibly Kanai Khuntia). Yashovanta, Achyuta(nanda) and Ananta belong to the Panchasakha.
[^09-34]: "Enters Jagannatha's body" is an expression of the Odia tradition for a devotee's departure. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas, a devotee does not merge into the Lord but enters His eternal service: pure devotees do not accept oneness (sayujya) even when it is offered (*Bhagavatam* 3.29.13).
[^09-35]: Cf. CC, Antya 11: Mahaprabhu Himself buried the body of Haridasa Thakura in the sand on the seashore at Puri. The first word is printed ସୋମୋଦ୍ର (= ସମୁଦ୍ର); *taṭeṇa* is an archaic case in ‑eṇa. The last words of the half-lines are printed ≈ ବାଲିର and ≈ ର/ତ + a bold sign like ଝ/ହିଁ + ର (OCR ଗ୍ଵନ୍ଦର). We read ବାଲିର (= ବାଲିରେ, "in the sand") (?) and ତହିଁର, "there" (??).
[^09-36]: *Bhagate* means "the devotees" (an old Odia form of *bhakta*).
[^09-37]: Printed ≈ ନ + ଉ + ଠି with an arc + ଁ (?) + ଲେ; we read ନ ଉଠିଲେ, "did not rise" (?). The sense of the couplet is unclear. Perhaps Prabhu and the Swami remained seated in grief and silent; perhaps they did not speak of what had happened. A new narrative begins with 9.42 (leaf 116).
[^09-38]: *Puṇyadehī* — "one whose body is holy". With 9.42 Madhava begins the account of how Mahaprabhu's earthly stay came to an end; the account continues beyond this portion. This is an Odia tradition. The CC and CB do not report any injury to Mahaprabhu; the CC (Antya 20) ends with the *Shikshashtaka* and does not describe His disappearance. A similar account (the left foot injured by a brick during the dance, then fever and disappearance) is found in Jayananda's *Chaitanya-mangala*. The Gaudiya Vaishnava position (Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati): the Lord's body is sac-cid-ananda, not material (cf. CC, Madhya 6), and His disappearance is a withdrawal from view (antardhana), not the destruction of a body.
[^09-39]: *Kṣaẏa* — "decline, wasting". Madhava calls Prabhu's body "pure" and promises to tell how it "came to decline"; translated exactly (see the note to 9.42). Printed କାଲେ, ଯେପର.
[^09-40]: *Uddāma bhāva* — "unrestrained, tempestuous" ecstatic emotion. The verb ଛାଡ଼ିଲ ("left") is set with a sign resembling ଗ୍ର: this is how the font renders ଛା, also in the editor's notes on the same leaf. Printed ଦନକେ.
[^09-41]: *Iṭāe* — "a brick". Printed ପଡ଼ଥିଲ, ତହିଁକ.
[^09-42]: Printed ବୃଦ୍ଧାଙ୍ଗୁଷ୍ଟି (normally ବୃଦ୍ଧାଙ୍ଗୁଷ୍ଠ; the ‑i apparently for the rhyme). The last word is printed ≈ ନକଟି. We read ନ କଟି, "without being cut off, without stopping" (?); the first sign also resembles ଜ.
[^09-43]: *Khañjāi* (?) — apparently from ଖଞ୍ଜା, "joint, ankle", with the particle ‑ଇ: "the foot twisted at the joint". *Gaḷagāji* — "crying out loudly" (?). Printed ମୋଡ଼ହେଲ, ଗଲଗାଜି.
[^09-44]: The Swami is Jagannatha Dasa; Yashovanta and Ananta belong to the Panchasakha (cf. 9.3). Kashishvara Mishra is probably Kashi Mishra, in whose house Mahaprabhu lived in Puri (CC, Madhya 10; cf. 6.8). The CC also has Kashishvara, Mahaprabhu's servant sent by Ishvara Puri (CC, Madhya 10). Srivatsa is not identified.
[^09-45]: The "Rukmini new moon" is the name of a new moon in the Odia calendar; apparently the new moon before the bright half of the month of Vaishakha (April–May) (?). No year is given here. Printed ରୁକ୍ମଣୀ (normally ରୁକ୍ମିଣୀ), ସାୟଂକାଲେ.
[^09-46]: The mandapa on the north side [of the temple] — cf. 9.1, the place of the ishtagoshthi. Here ଉତ୍ତର, "northern", is clearly printed, which confirms the reading in 9.1. The last word is printed ଚ‑ତା‑ଟୋ‑ପେ (*catāṭope*) and is not identified. From the sense — "on the floor" or "on a mat" (ଚଟା "mat" + ଉପେ "upon"?) (??).
[^09-47]: Printed ଅନଲ. We read ଅନିଳ, "air, breath" (the ‑i lost): Prabhu is breathing. ଅନଳ, "fire, heat", i.e. hot breath, is also possible (?). *Mudiṇa* — printed ≈ ମୂଜିଣ; we read ମୁଦିଣ, "having closed" (?).
[^09-48]: The Swami (Jagannatha Dasa) speaks: "give life back to my body" — without a word from Prabhu he himself is as if lifeless. The Swami addresses Prabhu with the familiar "you" (*tu*) — Madhava's Odia tradition (cf. 9.2). Printed ଦଅ.
[^09-49]: *Sāṣṭāma* — the same unclear word as in 4.31 and 4.123. Here it again follows a swoon ("in time Prabhu became *sāṣṭāma*"), which supports the sense "come to oneself". Second half-line: printed ≈ ନ + ଜ (ନିଜ? the ‑i unclear) ଥାନୁ ("from the place"); we understand "went off to their own places" (?).
[^09-50]: *Bije hele* — the honorific "deigned to go"; it can mean both "withdrew" and "arrived". We take it as departure, following the others (9.59) (?). A prahara is a night watch of about three hours. Printed ପହରକ (= ପହରେକ), ବଜେ (cf. ବଜେ = ବିଜେ in 4.70, 4.72).
[^09-51]: The temple is the Jagannatha temple; the evening kirtan there goes on to khol and tal. The last word: the sign ହ is set without its usual thickening and resembles ର (?). Printed ମନ୍ଦରେ, ଖୋଲ ତାଲ.
[^09-52]: In 9.63–66 the Swami, Jagannatha Dasa, stays with the injured Prabhu; it is he who speaks in 9.64–66, addressing Prabhu as *tu*. According to the CC (Antya 14–20), in the last years Svarupa Damodara, Ramananda Raya and the servant Govinda were constantly with Mahaprabhu in the Gambhira. In Madhava, Svarupa has already passed away (9.20). Jagannatha Dasa is not mentioned in the CC or CB. *Mātraka* = *mātra*, "but".
[^09-53]: Uncertain reading. *Nāi* (?) — perhaps "bathing" (from ନାଇବା, "to bathe"): "drink, food, bathing". Or = ନାହିଁ: "You have no drink or food [here] — I shall stay and bring them". *Niyogāi* (?) — printed ≈ ନଯୋଗାଇ; we understand "providing, supplying" (cf. ଯୋଗାଇବା, "to supply"); ନ ଯୋଗାଇ is also possible.
[^09-54]: Printed ≈ ଠଳ (the ‑u sign unclear). We read ଠୁଳ, "come near": "dawn drew near, came" (?). Rhyme କାଳ / ଠୁଳ.
[^09-55]: Khai is rice parched on hot sand until the grains pop; the comparison conveys the intense heat of the body. The third word is printed ≈ ଖ + a sign like ଇ (the same sign as in ଝରଇ, 9.93); the OCR reads it as ଳ. We read ଖଇ (?). The last word is printed ≈ ଫ + ଟି; we read ଫୁଟି, "popping" (?). The rhyme ତପତ / ଫୁଟି is imperfect.
[^09-56]: *Sañjaku* — "by evening" (?): printed ≈ ସ + ଞ୍ଜ (OCR ସଞ୍ଚକୁ). The toe of the left foot is the one wounded by the brick (9.47). Printed ଫୁଲଗଲ, ଅଂଗୁଷ୍ଟି (norm. ଅଙ୍ଗୁଷ୍ଠ).
[^09-57]: The Swami is Jagannatha Dasa. He gives Prabhu a hot compress (Odia *sek*). *Tātahi̐* (?) — "while [the water] is hot", "into the hot [water]" (ତାତ + ହିଁ). *Kaniā* (?) — "a rag, a scrap of cloth" (cf. କନା, "cloth"); printed ≈ କ + ନ/ଣ + ଆ.
[^09-58]: The last word is printed ≈ ବୋ + a sign like ଇ/ଜ + ଲ; we read ବୋଇଲ, "said, asked" (?); rhyme ଦେଲ / ବୋଇଲ. Who speaks is unclear: perhaps the Swami asks Prabhu; perhaps Prabhu says "it has become warm". Printed କ (= କି, the question particle).
[^09-59]: The middle sign of the word is printed like ଠ (OCR ତାଉ); we read ତାପ, "fever" (?). *Rajane* (?) — "at night" (locative of ରଜନୀ); printed ≈ ର + ଜ/ଯ + େ + ନ.
[^09-60]: Kala (*kāḷa*) — "time", and also the appointed term of the end. Prabhu says that while the Swami is with Him, His term will not come. These are words of Madhava's Odia tradition. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the Lord is the master of time itself (kala is His energy, cf. BG 11.32), and His departure takes place by His own will (see the note to 9.90). *Sambhāḷa* (?) — printed ≈ ସମ୍ହାଲ / ସମ୍ଭାଲ; we take it as "collecting Himself" (?). It may also be an imperative "hold on!" addressed to the Swami.
[^09-61]: Cf. BG 9.31: "My devotee never perishes." *Labe* (?) — "even for an instant" (ଲବ, "an instant, a tiny part"). Printed ≈ ଲ/କ + େ + ବ (OCR ଲବେ). *Bhabe* — "in the world"; ଭ is printed like ଉ.
[^09-62]: Prabhu calls Himself the servant of Jagannatha Dasa. The mutual "swami" and "prabhu" are a feature of the Odia tradition (cf. 9.2, 9.58). According to the CC (Adi 3), Mahaprabhu Himself played the role of a devotee in order to teach bhakti by His own example. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas He is Krishna Himself, not the servant of His associates. The last word is printed ≈ ଅ + a sign like ଶ (ବି? ରି?) + େ + ବ + କ. We read ଅବିବେକ, "want of discernment, something unseemly" (?): Prabhu finds it unseemly that He, the servant, received service from the Swami (9.78).
[^09-63]: Jagabandhu, "Friend of the world", is a name of Jagannatha. Printed ≈ ପାଇଲ (= ପାଇଲି, 1st pers.: "I received"); the ‑u of ଜଗବନ୍ଧୁ is printed below the line.
[^09-64]: "He" is Jagannatha (Jagabandhu, 9.78). The word is printed ≈ ଅ + ଥ with repha; we read ଅର୍ଥ, "aim, good, lot" (?). The OCR gives ≈ ଅନ୍ତ, "end"; then: "He decides the end of living beings". Printed ବନା, ଗତ, ବଚାରଇ.
[^09-65]: Prabhu asks whether Jagannatha will take Him to His abode. This is a tradition of Odisha; according to it, Mahaprabhu at the end entered into Jagannatha. For the Gaudiya Vaishnava position see the note to 9.90. Printed କ (= କି), ନଜଧାମେ.
[^09-66]: Printed ପରଣ (the first sign is ପ, as in ପଣ୍ଡିତ in the same couplet). We take it as ପରାଣ, "life" (?). ଶରଣ, "refuge", may have been intended; in that case the first sign is a misprint.
[^09-67]: Prabhu says that the Swami showed Him the path of bhakti. This is Madhava's Odia tradition. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas Mahaprabhu is Krishna Himself, who appeared to give prema to the world (CC, Adi 1–4); He is the source of bhakti, not a learner in it. Printed ≈ ଭଗତ (‑i lost; ଭ looks like ଉ).
[^09-68]: We understand: if Prabhu goes first, He will wait for the Swami, watching the road by which he will come. The last word is printed ≈ ଚ/ର + ା + ହିଁ; we read ଚାହିଁ, "watching" (?). Printed ≈ ଥବ (= ଥିବି, "I shall be").
[^09-69]: The last word is printed ≈ ଚ/ଟ + a ligature with subscript ର + ମୁଖ (OCR ର୍ଟେମୁଖ). We read ଚନ୍ଦ୍ରମୁଖ, "moon-like face" (?).
[^09-70]: *Upaca* (?) — "overflowing, brimming over" (cf. ଉପୁଚିବା). Printed ≈ ଉପଚ/ଉପତ, ବୋହଲ.
[^09-71]: The second half-line is read with little confidence. Its second word is printed ≈ ଟ/ଚ + a ligature with a subscript + କୁ; we read ଚନ୍ଦ୍ରକୁ, "to the moon" (??) (cf. "moon-like face", 9.84); it may be ପ୍ରଭୁଙ୍କୁ, "to Prabhu". The last word is printed ≈ େ + ଦ + a sign like ଧ (rhyming with ବନ୍ଧ); the sense "gives" (= ଦେଇ?) is a guess. The first word may also be ସେବେ, "serves" (ସ/ଯ). The sense seems to be that the Swami served Prabhu with the devotion of a faithful wife who gives her life to her lord.
[^09-72]: Akshaya Tritiya is the third day of the bright fortnight of Vaishakha (April–May). The year is not named here; from the course of the narrative ≈1533 (±1 year). On this day the Chandana-yatra, the festival of sandal paste (21 days), begins in Puri. The abakasha is Jagannatha's morning service (cleaning of the teeth and bathing). The word after ଚନ୍ଦନ is printed ≈ ମ + ା with a broken top + ଲ/ଭ; we read ମାଳ, "garland" (?). The syntax of the half-line is unclear.
[^09-73]: Madhava writes: "the prana left Prabhu's body (*piṇḍa*)"; this is translated exactly. It is the account of the Odia tradition. The Gaudiya Vaishnava position (Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati): the Lord's body is sac-cid-ananda, spiritual (cf. CC, Madhya 6). It is not subject to birth and destruction, and His departure is a withdrawal from view (antardhana) by His own will, like the setting of the sun (cf. BG 4.9). The CC does not describe the circumstances of the disappearance: its narrative ends with the *Shikshashtaka* (Antya 20). The year of the disappearance is 1455 of the Shaka era, i.e. 1533 (CC, Adi 13). The time is the brahma-muhurta, the hour before dawn. Printed ମୁହୂର୍ତ୍ତି (with ‑i).
[^09-74]: Here and in 9.96–97 the temple is the temple of Jagannatha. Prabhu had been laid in the mandapa by the north side of the temple (9.53). According to the CC (Antya 14–20), Mahaprabhu spent His last years in the Gambhira, in Kashi Mishra's house, with Svarupa Damodara and Ramananda Ray. Printed ମନ୍ଦରେ (‑i lost); ଅଛୁ so (= ଅଛି?).
[^09-75]: *Keṇe* — "where" (archaic). *Tuhi* — "You"; ତ is printed in its second form, like ର.
[^09-76]: The first word of the second half-line is printed ≈ ଓ/ଠ + a ligature ଲ୍ଟ/ନ୍ତ + ା + ଇ (OCR ଭନ୍ତାଇ). We read ଓଲ୍ଟାଇ, "turning over" (??); the word is not identified with certainty.
[^09-77]: Madhava calls Prabhu's body *śaba*, "the body of one departed". For Gaudiya Vaishnavas the Lord's body is never a lifeless body (see the note to 9.90). The second word is printed ≈ a filled sign + ଲେ (OCR କଲେ); we read ଭଲେ, "carefully" (?). କୋଳେ, "in the lap", may have been meant, but there is no ୋ sign. *Nimiṣi* (?) — printed ≈ ନ + ମୀ + ଷି/ଖି; we take it as "unblinking" (?) or "for a moment". The rhyme ‑ଛୁ / ‑ଷି is imperfect.
[^09-78]: Indradig is Indra's quarter, the east: dawn is breaking. Printed ≈ ଇ/ଚ + ନ୍ଦ୍ର + ଦ(ି) + ଗ (OCR ଚଜ୍ଦଦ୍ତିଗ). The crow is apparently a sign of daybreak. *Raba* — "cry, voice" (printed ରବ).
[^09-79]: The abakasha (cf. 9.89) is Jagannatha's morning service. On an ordinary day the temple is full of servitors at this hour, but here it is "motionless", silent. Printed ମନ୍ଦର, ≈ ନଶ୍ଚଲ (= ନିଶ୍ଚଳ).
[^09-80]: Rama Raya is Ramananda Ray (cf. 9.2, 9.60). *Rāẏare* is a dative-locative form: "to Rama Raya".
[^09-81]: *Jhapaṭa* (?) — "headlong, at a run" (cf. ଝଟପଟ, ଝପଟିବା). Printed ≈ ଅବଲମ୍ବେ (= ଅବିଳମ୍ବେ).
[^09-82]: The first word of the second half-line is printed ≈ ତ + a broken sign + େ + ଣ; we read ତକ୍ଷଣେ (= ତତ୍‌କ୍ଷଣେ), "at once" (?). *Miḷa* — "arrived" (= ମିଳିଲେ). *Debāḷaẏa* — "the abode of God", the temple (printed ଦେବାଲୟ). The narrative continues on leaf 120 (v. 101 onward).
[^09-83]: From the sense, Rama Raya (Ramananda Raya; cf. note to 9.2) is the one acting here and in 9.102–107. He was called to the temple in 9.98–100, and in 9.112 he reports to the king. The last word is printed ≈ ସୂଗମ୍ଭୀର: *sugambhīra*, "very deep, grave". We take it as "stood frozen in deep shock" (?). The reading ସ୍ତମ୍ଭୀର / ସ୍ତମ୍ଭିତ, "stunned", is also possible, but the metre (9 syllables) supports ସୁଗମ୍ଭୀର. Cf. 9.23: Raya "stood stunned" after Svarupa's departure.
[^09-84]: *Śaba* is the ordinary word for a lifeless body. Madhava uses it in 9.102–124 (cf. 9.94); we translate "body". The Odia account of Mahaprabhu's disappearance continues here (cf. note to 9.42). In this account, Mahaprabhu's body remained in the temple and was secretly committed to the earth. For the Gaudiya Vaishnava position see the note to 9.42: the Lord's body is sac-cid-ananda, and His departure is a disappearance (antardhana). The CC (Antya 20) is silent about it. On other accounts accepted among Gaudiya Vaishnavas see the note to 9.120.
[^09-85]: The first word is printed ≈ ନା + ହିଁ + a sign resembling କି or ‑ନ୍ତି. We read ନାହିଁକି, "is no more" (?); ନାହାନ୍ତି is also possible. In the second half-line ଛା is set with a sign like ଗ୍ର (as in 9.44). We read ଛାଡ଼ିଣ, "letting go" (?): Rama Raya lays down the body he had held on his lap (9.102) and rises to give orders.
[^09-86]: The first word is printed ≈ a dark sign ନ/ତୁ + a ligature like ଷ୍ଟ/ଶ + ‑e + ତ. We read ତୁରିତେ, "hastily" (??): in this type ରି resembles ଶ, and ତ has a second form. The reading is chosen from sense and metre. *Uccari* (?) means "pronouncing aloud", from ଉଚ୍ଚରିବା. Rhyme ହକାରି / ଉଚ୍ଚରି.
[^09-87]: *Nīti* is the daily order of Jagannatha's rites. The temple is shut and the rites are suspended because the temple has become impure; after the burial it is purified (9.127–128). Printed ଆଜ, କଲା (= କିଳା, "will be bolted"), ନୀତ, ଚଲବ.
[^09-88]: Akshaya Tritiya is the third day of the bright fortnight of Vaishakha (April–May). Cf. 9.89: Madhava places Prabhu's departure at the dawn of this day. Akshaya Tritiya begins the Chandana Yatra, a summer festival in Puri: representative Deities (Madana-mohana and others) are taken on boats on the Narendra tank. No year is named here. According to the CC (Adi 13), Mahaprabhu remained on earth for 48 years; by the accepted dating, His departure was ≈1533/34 (±1 year). *Binodiā* (?) means "joyful, festive"; printed ≈ ବନୋଦିଆ (‑i lost).
[^09-89]: Gajapati Rudradeva is Prataparudra. *Bije* is the king's ceremonial setting-out (printed ବଜେ; cf. 9.60). The last word is printed ≈ ସ + ମ୍ଭ + ବ, with a hook on the ବ like a reph (ର୍ବ). We read ସମ୍ଭବ, "it happened" (?): because of the festival, the king was just then on his way to the temple.
[^09-90]: *Yugaprahara* means "two watches". A prahara is about three hours (cf. 9.60); two watches from dawn is about midday (?). Printed ବଷାଦତ (= ବିଷାଦିତ), ବେଲ.
[^09-91]: Printed ≈ ଦା + a sign like ଷୁ/ୟୁ + ଭୂତ. We read ଦାରୁଭୂତ, "turned to wood, stupefied" (?). *Phure* is from ଫୁରିବା, "to come forth [of speech]".
[^09-92]: In 9.114–122 Rama Raya speaks, addressing the king in the polite form (imperative ଶୁଣ, ‑ଛ in 9.119). *Nele* means "if [it is] carried away, out [of the temple]". *Akāraṇa* means "to no purpose", here "to [our] harm".
[^09-93]: The "Gaudiyas" are Mahaprabhu's devotees from Gauda (Bengal); the "mleccha king" is the Muslim ruler of Gauda, the Gajapati's adversary (cf. CC, Madhya 16). Rama Raya's fear belongs to the Odia account. According to the CC, the Bengali devotees came to Mahaprabhu in Puri every year for the Ratha Yatra, and Prataparudra received them with honour (CC, Madhya 11–14). The CC and CB report no such conflict. *Khara* means "sharp, hostile [words]"; *kadartha* means "distortion, disgrace, trouble".
[^09-94]: Printed ≈ ଅପନନ୍ଦା. We read ଅପନିନ୍ଦା, "ill fame, blame" (?) (‑i lost). *Pāibu* is a future 1st person plural, "we shall receive": the blame would fall on the king and his people.
[^09-95]: Printed ≈ ମା + a sign like ରି/ବ୍ର + ଗଲ. We read, as in 9.26, ମରିଗଲ, the ordinary verb for the end of a person's earthly life. The words belong to Rama Raya in the Odia account. We translate "departed" (see the notes to 9.42 and 9.102).
[^09-96]: *Mahimā*, "greatness", is here a form of address to the king. Rama Raya reminds him that the Gajapati once, before the pilgrims, called Sri Chaitanya "Prabhu", that is, the Lord. Jagannatha too is called "Prabhu"; hence the play on words in 9.120. Madhava does not say which occasion is meant. According to the CC (Madhya 13–14), at the Ratha Yatra Prataparudra served by sweeping the road before the chariot and then received Mahaprabhu's mercy. ଯାତ୍ର is printed without ‑ā (the metre has 9 syllables).
[^09-97]: "This Prabhu became absorbed in that Prabhu": Sri Chaitanya in Jagannatha. In Madhava these are the words Rama Raya proposes to announce to the people to conceal the burial. Cf. 9.38 and the note to 9.38 on the expression "to enter Jagannatha's body". Gaudiya Vaishnavas have different accounts of Mahaprabhu's disappearance. According to Lochana Dasa's *Chaitanya-mangala*, He entered the Deity of Jagannatha; according to another tradition, the Deity of Tota-Gopinatha. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself (CC, Adi 1), non-different from Jagannatha. His "entering" is the Lord's disappearance, not the merging of a soul into God, nor the burial of a material body.
[^09-98]: Koili Vaikuntha is a place in the northern part of the Jagannatha temple compound in Puri. There, at the Navakalevara (renewal of the Deities' forms), the former wooden forms of Jagannatha, Baladeva, Subhadra and Sudarshana are buried (cf. 9.122). Printed କୋଇଲ (‑i lost); *purate* is printed ≈ ପୂରତେ. We take it as "at the place, before" (?), from Skt *puratas* or ପୁର + ‑ତେ.
[^09-99]: The Swami is Jagannatha Dasa (cf. note to 9.49); *swāmī pāda*, the honorific "the Swami's feet", means "the revered Swami". The Gajapati is not named among those who performed the burial. 9.124–125 form one sentence.
[^09-100]: Only those named in 9.125 know of it: this is how Madhava explains why the account did not become known. *Paṛila duāra* means "the closed ('fallen') doors" (printed ପଡ଼ଲ): after the burial the temple is opened again.
[^09-101]: The first word is printed ≈ ସ + ସ୍କା/ସ୍ଥା + ର; the same sign appears in the editor's note on this leaf. We read ସଂସ୍କାର, "purification" (?) (no anusvara is visible). Who gives the order is not said: apparently Rama Raya (cf. 9.105). The temple is purified and sprinkled with fragrant water before the rites and the Chandana Yatra resume (9.108).
[^09-102]: The festival of Chandana is the Chandana Yatra, which begins on Akshaya Tritiya (see note to 9.108). Markanda is apparently the sacred Markandeya tank in Puri, one of the five tirthas of the town. Madhava does not explain why the devotees go there in these days (?). *Boli* is literally "saying": "[making] for Markanda". Printed ଚଲଗଲେ (= ଚଳିଗଲେ); in 9.131, ଦବସେଣ (= ଦିବସେଣ), ବଷାଦ.
[^09-103]: *Antarddhāna* means "disappearance, withdrawal from view". Here Madhava himself calls Prabhu's departure by this word — the same word Gaudiya Vaishnavas use for it (see note to 9.90). Verses 9.132–137 concern the news that the devotees and the people learned; only a few knew of the burial in Koili Vaikuntha (9.124–126).
[^09-104]: This is the news that Rama Raya proposed to announce in 9.120: "this Prabhu became absorbed in that Prabhu". The simile of lightning: lightning does not perish in a cloud, it only passes from view. On how Gaudiya Vaishnavas understand Mahaprabhu's "entering" Jagannatha, see the note to 9.120. Printed ଲୁଚଗଲ (= ଲୁଚିଗଲ).
[^09-105]: The last word of the first half-line is printed ≈ ହେ + ର + a sign resembling ଶ (or ‑ନ୍ତି). We read ହେରନ୍ତି, "they look" (?); ହେରଇ is also possible. The sense of the half-line is clear. *Bhoḷa* (printed ≈ ଭ୍ରଳ) means "bewildered, beside oneself".
[^09-106]: In 9.136–137a the devotees speak, remembering Prabhu. They call Him "Bhagavan Himself" (*sākṣāte bhagabāna*). This agrees with the Gaudiya Vaishnava position: Sri Chaitanya is Krishna Himself, the Supreme Lord (CC, Adi 1–2). The second half-line is unclear: we take *nṛtya kalaku* as "in order to dance" (= *karibāku*?) (?). Mahaprabhu's dancing in kirtan is a recurring motif of the poem (cf. 5.8–13).
[^09-107]: *Śrī-aṅga* — "the holy body", i.e. Jagannatha's body (cf. 9.133). *Ho* is a cry of grief. Printed ଶୁଣ (= ଶୁଣି), ≈ ଭାବଭୋଲା.
[^09-108]: Achyuta (Achyutananda), Yashovanta and Ananta belong to the Panchasakha; for Kanhai see the note to 9.36. The same persons are named together in 9.36. They did not know of the burial (9.125–126) and learned the general news. *Anantāi*, *kahnāi* are affectionate forms of the names.
[^09-109]: Swami is Jagannatha Dasa. He was with Prabhu to the end and at the burial (9.63–66, 9.125). Achyuta reproaches him and addresses him as "tu" ("thou") — a sign of intimacy; cf. note to 9.63. The ଛା of ଛାଡ଼ିଲୁ is set with a sign resembling ଗ୍ର, as in 9.44 and 9.104; here the reading is clear from the sense, which supports the reading of 9.104. *Kipā̐* means "why" (printed କପା, in 9.141 କପାଁ; ‑i lost).
[^09-110]: "Bind and keep": the motif of the Lord bound by a devotee's love is familiar to Vaishnavas from the *Bhagavatam* (10.9: Yashoda binds Krishna). Here it is apparently a reproach: Swami, so close to Prabhu, could have kept Him (?).
[^09-111]: Yashovanta checks Achyuta: such reproaches are childish. Madhava calls Achyuta a "boy" (*bāḷuta*) and Ananta (9.143) a "child"; apparently they were younger than the others (?). Printed ≈ କଂବା (= କିବା).
[^09-112]: The penultimate word is printed ≈ ପ + a sign resembling ଗ୍ର + ଏ. We read ପରାଏ, "like" (?): the same sign stands for ଛା in 9.140, and here for ରା.
[^09-113]: The Gaudiya devotees came to Puri every year for the Ratha Yatra (CC, Madhya 11; Antya 10). According to Madhava, at this Ratha Yatra — the first after Prabhu's disappearance (month of Ashadha) — the king announced the news from the chariot, as Rama Raya had advised (9.119–120). The CC and CB do not report this. The first sign of the last word is a dark blot with a superscript hook; we read ଉଦ୍ଘୋଷିଲେ, "proclaimed" (?), possibly ସଂଘୋଷିଲେ.
[^09-114]: The first word is printed ≈ ନ + ଅ/ଉ + ଡ଼େ. We read ଗଉଡ଼େ, "to Gauda" (?): in this type ଗ is close to ନ. ନଅରେ, "in the palace", is also possible, but the sign with the dot is ଡ଼, not ର. According to Madhava, this is how the news reached Bengal. In Jayananda, Mahaprabhu's departure is linked to an injury at the Ratha Yatra (cf. note to 9.42); in Madhava it falls on Akshaya Tritiya (9.89), and the Ratha Yatra is only the time when the news was announced.
[^09-115]: *Praẏāṇa* means "setting out, departure" (cf. *antarddhāna*, 9.132). *Rathaku* — "at / by the Ratha [Yatra]". *Sametā* = *sameta*, "all together" (‑ā for the rhyme with *bāratā*). Printed ପ୍ରଭର (= ପ୍ରଭୁର).
[^09-116]: At earlier Ratha Yatras the king performed the chhera-panhara — sweeping the chariots with a golden broom (cf. 2.119–121, 5.7–8; CC, Madhya 13) — while Mahaprabhu danced in kirtan before the chariot. Now "the golden broom fell". The syntax of the second half-line is unclear: we take it as "from the king's hand — this had never been" (?); it may also mean "it was not in the king's hand", i.e. the king did not perform the service (?). Printed ≈ ହାଥୁଁ (= ହାତୁଁ).
[^09-117]: The last word is printed ≈ ଲୌକକେ. We read ଲୌକିକେ, "among the people, publicly" (?) (‑i lost). *Abakāśe* — "on the occasion, during".
[^09-118]: Krishnashtami (Janmashtami) is the festival of Krishna's appearance, the eighth day of the dark fortnight of Bhadra (August–September). The narrative thus runs from Akshaya Tritiya (Vaishakha) through the Ratha Yatra (Ashadha) to Janmashtami — about four months. The sentence continues in 9.152 (beyond this portion).
[^09-119]: The account of Krishna Janmashtami continues (cf. 9.151). This festival of Krishna's appearance falls in the month of Bhadrava (August–September), about four months after Prabhu's departure on Akshaya Tritiya (cf. note to 9.108). No year is named; in the course of the story it is the same year, ≈1533/34 (±1 year). Formerly on this day the ishtagoshthi sang and danced together with Prabhu; now, without Him, the kirtan gives no joy. *Birasa* means "tasteless, devoid of rasa", i.e. "joyless".
[^09-120]: The mandapa on the northern side [of the temple] is the place of the ishtagoshthi (cf. 9.1, 9.53 and the notes there). There Rama Raya was the chief, and Prabhu and the Swami sat in the middle (9.2). *Pāruśe* = *pārśwe*, "at the side" (cf. 9.1, 9.53). After ପା a sign like "ଃ" is printed — apparently an ink spot.
[^09-121]: The Swami is Jagannatha Dasa (cf. notes to 9.2, 9.49). After Prabhu's departure Rama Raya and the Swami together remember His kirtans.
[^09-122]: The future in ‑*iba* is here 1st person: "I will not come", "I will live". From the sense, Jagannatha Dasa will no longer come to the temple and to the gathering at the northern mandapa, but will settle by the sea. In Puri a hermitage on the seashore is associated with Jagannatha Dasa (by tradition, the Satalahari Math). Printed କୂଲେଣ, ରହବ.
[^09-123]: Achyuta (Achyutananda) and Ananta belong to the Panchasakha (cf. 9.3, 9.21). Nemala (Nemal, Cuttack district) is, by Odia tradition, the place where Achyutananda lived and where his samadhi is. Thus in Madhava's account the Panchasakha circle in Puri disperses after Prabhu's departure. The first ଗଲ is printed with a subscript sign (≈ ଗଲ୍ଲ / ଗଲୁ); the second half-line has plain ଗଲ. The name is printed ≈ ନେମ୍ମ ଳକୁ, with a gap in place of ‑ā (?).
[^09-124]: *Swarga gale*, "went to heaven", is a respectful expression for leaving this life (cf. 3.119 on Madhavendra Puri). According to Madhava, Rama Raya (Ramananda Raya; cf. note to 9.2) left his body on the evening of that same Janmashtami. The CC and CB say nothing of Ramananda Raya's departure; according to the CC (Antya 14–20), he consoled Mahaprabhu in the Gambhira together with Svarupa Damodara to the end. For Gaudiya Vaishnavas Ramananda Raya is an eternal associate of the Lord (cf. CC, Madhya 8: his conversation with Mahaprabhu on the highest truths). His departure is a return to eternal service of the Lord, not a passage to the heavenly planets (svarga). Printed ସେଦନ, ସାୟଂକାଲ.
[^09-125]: "The prana left the body (*piṇḍa*)" is the same expression as in 9.90 (said of Prabhu; see note to 9.90). Here it is applied to Rama Raya. The word ଗଲାକାଳେ (?) is printed ≈ ଗ + ଲ with a subscript sign (as in 9.157) + a sign like ଭା/କା + ଲେ. OCR gives ‑କାଲେ; ଗଲାବେଳେ is also possible. *Sehu* is an emphatic "he".
[^09-126]: Printed ଖେଣେ. The letter is the same as in ମୁଖେ (9.161). We take it as ଖଣେ / କ୍ଷଣେ, "in that instant" (?). The rhyme ଚହଳ / ସତ୍ୱର is inexact. Unlike the secret burial of Prabhu (9.124–126), Rama Raya's departure takes place openly, before the people. Printed ମିଲଲେ.
[^09-127]: "Rama nama satya" ("Rama's name is truth") is the customary cry in India when a body is carried to the cremation ground. Madhava describes an ordinary funeral with the chanting of Hari's name. Printed ସକଲ.
[^09-128]: The second half-line is printed ≈ ମ + a sign like ୬/ଚ with a dot, + ବୋଲେ ତାହା ଜାଣି. OCR gives ≈ ମରଣ. We read ମରଣେ, "at the hour of passing" (??). The sense seems to be: what a person is can be seen from the words he speaks as he leaves this life. Rama Raya departed with Krishna's name on his lips (9.159). Cf. Bhagavad-gita 8.5–6: whoever remembers the Lord while leaving the body attains Him. 9.162–163 form one sentence.
[^09-129]: *Siddha puruṣa* is a "perfected" devotee, one who has reached the goal. We understand that Jagannatha Dasa bears witness to Rama Raya. The form *kahuchu* is unusual: in the language of the poem ‑*achu* also occurs in the 3rd person (cf. ହୋଇଅଛୁ). If it is taken as 1st person plural, the line would read: "We say: Jagannatha Dasa is a perfected one" (?). The context (9.158–162) supports the first reading.
[^09-130]: This is the author's closing signature and the last numbered couplet of the poem. Cf. the chapter signatures: "says Madhava Das" (2.131, 7.148), "says the lowest of shudras" (1.82). The first half-line is printed ≈ ଏ + the second form of ତ + ହୁଁ. We read ଏ ତହୁଁ (?): "this [is so]; from it nothing other will be", i.e. "it cannot be otherwise". Another reading is possible: "after this there will be no other [account]", i.e. a closing formula. The last word is printed ≈ ଶୂ/ଗ୍ରୁ + ର, i.e. ଶୂର, "hero" (?). We suggest ଶୂଦ୍ର, "shudra": a self-deprecating designation like ଶୂଦ୍ରାଧମ in 1.82. The rhyme with ହେବାର is kept, and so is the metre (9 syllables). No completion date is given here. Madhava named the time of composition at the beginning of the poem: the 48th anka of Prataparudra (1.73), ≈1534/35 (±1 year). So the poem was written soon after the events described (cf. notes to 1.71–72 and 9.152).
[^09-131]: Three unnumbered lines after the last couplet. *Iti … sampūrṇṇa*, "thus is complete…", is the usual formula at the end of a manuscript. *Lekhanakāra* means "writer, copyist"; the request not to blame him for errors is a common scribal addition, so these lines apparently belong not to Madhava but to the copyist. They give no date of copying, no anka and no copyist's name. *Biśeṣa* (?) in the second line means "special" and also "abundant, more besides". A possible sense: "the account here is extensive [and may have been copied with errors]", or "there is much else [about this]".
